點用（How to use）

  1. 先睇 E（考場一頁通）：公式卡、本科 conventions、陷阱表 — MC 前掃一次。
  2. 計數題用 P（Playbooks）：fork / pipe / scheduling / sync 步驟 + 預計表（Amdahl、powers、RM bound）。
  3. 概念題 / short question → 對應章節（1–6）入面 📝 Model answer（英文可直接抄）。
  4. 見到似曾相識嘅題目 → Q（歷屆全解）/ M（MC key）。搵字 → 最後嘅 Index（紅色 = 主要頁）。
  5. 右邊（雙數頁左邊）有彩色 tab：同一顏色 = 同一部分。

EExam-Day Essentials考場一頁通：公式・慣例・陷阱

E1. 必背公式卡 Formula Card

主題 公式 例
Average access time [1.32] AMAT = h × T_hit + (1 − h) × T_miss（若畀 penalty：T_miss = T_hit + penalty） 0.9×2 + 0.1×50 = 6.8 ns
Amdahl's Law [4.12] speedup ≤ 1 / (S + (1 − S)/N)；N→∞：1/S；S = serial S=0.25, N=2 → 1.6
Amdahl 反解 S = (1/X − 1/N) / (1 − 1/N) X=3, N=4 → S = 1/9
Turnaround [5.8] TAT = completion − arrival
Waiting [5.8] WT = TAT − burst（= Gantt 上嘅 gaps 總和）
Response（本科） [5.12] RT = end of FIRST run − arrival（RR/MLFQ：第一個 quantum 完；non-preemptive：= TAT） RR4：P1=4, P2=7, P3=10
Exponential averaging [5.19] τ(n+1) = α·t(n) + (1 − α)·τ(n) α=.25, t=6, τ=10 → 9
Little's formula [5.57] n = λ × W λ=3/s, n=12 → W=4 s
RR bound [5.11] 冇 process 等多過 (n − 1)·q
RT utilization [5.49] U = Σ tᵢ / pᵢ；U > 1 ⇒ 一定 miss 25/50 + 35/80 = 0.94
RM bound（課本） 保證可排：U ≤ N(2^{1/N} − 1)：N=1 1.000、2 0.828、3 0.780、4 0.757、5 0.743、∞ 0.693
EDF [5.52] U ≤ 1（D = T 時）理論上可排
fork 數目 n 個無條件 fork → 2ⁿ；if(fork()) fork(); 每輪 ×3 見 Playbook P1
Bounded buffer (in/out) [3.54] 最多 BUFFER_SIZE − 1 items
Semaphore（blocking） [6.26] value < 0 ⇒ |value| = 等緊嘅 processes 數
Storage units [1.28] KB = 1024 B；MB = 1024²；GB = 1024³；networks 用 bits

E2. 本科 Conventions（考試照用，唔確定就寫 assumption）

  1. Response time = completion of the first run − arrival（slide 5.12；S26 官方答案照用）。
  2. RR：quantum 用完 → tail；同一時刻有新 arrival → 新 arrival 先入 queue。
  3. MLFQ：新 job → Q0 tail；用晒 quantum 仍未完 → 降一級 tail；被高 queue 新 arrival preempt → 返自己 queue 嘅 HEAD，保留剩餘 quantum（Q1）/ 剩餘 burst（Q2），唔會降級；Q0 job 唔會被 preempt；Q1 job 唔會 preempt Q2 job。
  4. Priority：細數字 = 高 priority（除非題目話 larger = higher，例如 S26 dynamic priority）。
  5. Ties：題目有講就跟（S26：smaller ID；dynamic priority：larger ID）；冇講：FCFS（早到先）→ 細 ID；SRTF / EDF 平手 → 唔 preempt running process。
  6. Soft real-time：miss 咗 deadline 嘅 job 繼續跑完，但計一次 miss。
  7. fork 題：除非另有說明，假設 printf 即時 flush、fork 全部成功。
  8. Gantt chart：每段寫 start / process / end；idle 都要畫；MLFQ 標 queue（P1@Q0）。

E3. 最易中伏 Top Traps（MC 前睇一次）

✘ 錯誤講法 ✔ 正確
Main memory is nonvolatile / registers slower than memory volatile；registers 最快；main memory byte addressable
von Neumann：instructions 同 data 分開存 same memory（stored-program）
Speedup with N CPUs = N < N（contention / overhead）
NUMA：所有 memory 同樣 latency remote memory slower
VM 一定比 native 快 minor slowdown
printf() 係 system call printf calls write() system call
API 比 system call 快 / 可以 bypass protection 優點 = portability + hide details（convenient）
Microkernel performance 好過 monolithic worse（message passing）
Layered：任何 layer 可 call 任何 layer 嗰個係 LKM；layered 只 call 下層
LKM 要 recompile kernel + reboot 可以 run time load/remove
Policy = how Policy = what；mechanism = how
Interrupts 全部同一 priority / 只由 I/O 產生 有 priority levels；trap = software interrupt
DMA 每個 byte interrupt 一次 block 完先 interrupt 一次
Mode bit 1 = kernel 1 = user，0 = kernel
Context switch = user→kernel mode switch context switch = save/restore process state；越複雜 OS 越慢
fork() 令 parent 等 child 要 wait()
child 改 global variable → parent 都變 各自 copy
exec() 成功會 return 唔會 return
Zombie 仲行緊 code / 冇 PID terminated，PID + PCB 仍在，等 parent wait()
Ordinary pipe 任何 process 用到 parent–child only、單向、用完消失
Named pipe 用完自動消失 explicitly delete；UNIX FIFO half-duplex
Threads 有自己 address space / files share code、data、heap、files；own PC、registers、stack
Concurrency = 同時執行 同時 = parallelism；concurrency 單核都得
Many-to-many 易 implement / user thread map 去多個 kernel threads difficult；冇 one-to-many
所有 signals deliver 畀所有 threads sync → causing thread；部分 async → all
Asynchronous cancellation = 定期 check 嗰個係 deferred（Pthreads default）
FCFS 可以 preemptive FCFS must be non-preemptive
RR 一定比 FCFS 好 同長度 jobs 時 TAT 更差
q 越大 TAT 越好 唔一定；q 太大 → FCFS
MLFQ 要知 burst / 永不 starve 唔使知；可能 starve（要 aging/reshuffle）
Multilevel queue 可搬 queue 只有 MLFQ 可以
Multicore 簡化 scheduling complicates（faster + less power）
Hard RT 遲咗都得 / RT scheduler 唔使 preemption 遲 = 冇服務；要 priority + preemption
EDF 要 periodic 唔需要；只需 announce deadline
Little's formula 只適用 FCFS any algorithm
"Synchronization" 係 CS 三條件之一 ME + progress + bounded waiting
test_and_set 返回新值 / 會 release lock 返回 old value；release = lock = false
Binary semaphore = mutex 可當 mutex，但更通用（init 0 做 ordering）

E4. 答題模板 Answer Templates

Short question（2–4 分）：[Definition sentence with keywords]. [Why / how — one sentence]. [Example or advantage/disadvantage].

Gantt + averages：

Gantt:  0 P1 4 P2 8 P3 12 ...           (每個 boundary 都寫)
P | Arr | Burst | Comp | TAT=C-A | WT=TAT-B | RT=end1-A
avg WT  = (w1 + w2 + ... + wn) / n = x.xx
avg TAT = (t1 + ... + tn) / n = x.xx
Assumptions: ties -> ..., new arrival enters queue before preempted process.

fork 題：Draw tree → count per level → formula → final number；output 題：constraints (wait / same process order) → list all interleavings。

Race values：Case 1 (branch …): interleaving … → c = … 逐個列。

Semaphore 填空：用 init-0 semaphore：先做者 signal(s)，後做者開頭 wait(s)；冇需要嘅 blank 寫 /* Nothing */。

PPlaybooks解題流程 + 預計表

P1. fork() Playbook

Step 1 — 規則

  • fork() 返回：child 0 / parent child-PID (>0) / fail −1（只有 parent，冇 child）。
  • Child 由 fork() 下一個 operation 開始，複製晒 variables（包括 loop 嘅 i）。
  • 短路：A && B → A 係 0（child）唔做 B；A || B → A 非 0（parent）唔做 B。
  • return / exit / break 會停止該 process 嘅 loop；exec 成功 = 之後 code 唔執行。

Step 2 — Pattern 表（每個 iteration、所有 process 都繼續 loop）

每 iteration 每 process 變 n 次後 例
fork(); ×2 2ⁿ n=3 → 8；n=10 → 1024
fork(); fork(); ×4 4ⁿ n=3 → 64
if (fork()) fork(); ×3 3ⁿ n=3 → 27
if (fork() == 0) fork(); ×3 3ⁿ n=3 → 27（Sp21 MC8）
fork() && fork(); ×3 3ⁿ parent 做兩次
fork() || fork(); ×3 3ⁿ child 做第二次
if (fork()) fork(); else break; active ×2 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 1 total breaks 2ⁿ − 1（S26 Q3.1）
if (fork()) printf; else printf; lines 2ⁱ per iter 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 2 lines F19 Q2.6
if (fork()==0) break;（fan） +1 child n + 1 只 parent 繼續
if (fork() > 0) break;（chain） +1 child n + 1 只 child 繼續

Step 3 — 其他已驗證答案

Code 答案
if (fork() || fork()) fork(); 5 processes
if (fork() && fork()) fork(); 4（P0: T&&T → fork → 2；C1 false → 1；C2 false → 1）
fork(); fork() && fork(); fork(); 12
pid1=fork(); pid2=fork(); if(pid1>0) if(pid2==0) fork(); 5
if (fork()==fork() || fork()!=fork()) fork(); printf 23 lines
S26 MC10：loop 3：if(fork()==0){fork(); printf X; return;} 之後 printf Y 7 processes, X = 6, Y = 1
S26 Q3.1：if(fork()) fork(); else break; 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 1 processes, 2ⁿ − 1 breaks（n=2：7, 3）
F20 Q3.2：if(!fork()){loop n: if(fork()) fork();} fork(); printf a 2(3ⁿ + 1)
Sp21 Q1.2：if(fork()==0){loop 5: if(fork()==0) fork();} fork(); (3⁵ + 1)×2 = 488
F19 Q2.6：loop n，parent/child 都 printf 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 2 lines
Slide 3.44：loop 10 fork(); fork(); 4¹⁰ = 1,048,576
Slide 3.45：loop 10 if (fork()) fork(); 3¹⁰ = 59,049

Step 4 — Process tree：每個 node 寫 PID 名（P0, P1…）同佢喺邊個 iteration 出世；child 繼承 parent 嘅 i 值繼續。

Step 5 — Output ordering：

  1. 同一 process 內嘅 print 次序固定。
  2. wait()：parent 之後嘅 print > 所有 child（及其子孫，如 child 等咗佢哋）嘅 print。
  3. fork 之前印嘅（已 flush）最先。
  4. 列所有符合約束嘅排列（topological orders）；可以用「插空位」數：k 個自由字元插入固定序列。

Step 6 — exec 情況：(I) fork ✓ exec ✓：child exec 前嘅 print + parent 嘅 print；(II) fork ✓ exec ✗：再加 exec 後嘅 print；(III) fork ✗：只有 ret < 0 branch。

P2. Pipe / dup / exec 填空 Playbook

要做 Code
建 pipe（fork 之前） int fd[2]; pipe(fd); → fd[0] read，fd[1] write
Writer close(fd[0]); write(fd[1], &x, sizeof(x)); close(fd[1]);
Reader close(fd[1]); read(fd[0], &x, sizeof(x)); close(fd[0]);
stdin ← file int fin = open(name, O_RDONLY); close(0); dup(fin);（或 dup2(fin, 0)）
stdout → file int fo = open(name, O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0644); dup2(fo, 1); close(fo);
stdout → pipe dup2(fd[1], 1); close(fd[0]); close(fd[1]); execlp(...)
stdin ← pipe dup2(fd[0], 0); close(fd[0]); close(fd[1]); execlp(...)
等指定 child waitpid(pid, &status, 0);
exec 一個 program execlp("ls", "ls", "-l", (char *)NULL); / execvp(argv[0], argv);（argv 尾要 NULL）
數偶數（S26） if (number % 2 == 0) cnt++;

P3. CPU Scheduling Playbook

萬用步驟：(1) 抄表；(2) 由 t=0 起逐個 event（arrival / completion / quantum expiry / check point）；(3) 每個 event 寫 ready queue 內容；(4) 照算法揀；(5) 畫 Gantt（每個 boundary）；(6) C → TAT → WT → RT；(7) 平均連 working；(8) 檢查：最後完成時間 = Σburst + idle。

Algorithm 揀邊個 幾時重新揀 注意
FCFS 最早到 完成時 non-preemptive；convoy effect
SJF 已到達嘅最短 burst 完成時 一旦開始跑到完
SRTF 最短 remaining 完成 + 每個 arrival tie → keep running
RR (q) queue head 完成 / quantum 用完 新 arrival 先入 queue，再放被 preempt 嘅
Priority NP 最高 priority（細數字） 完成時 tie → FCFS
Priority P 最高 priority 完成 + 高 priority arrival
Priority + RR 最高 priority；同級 RR 完成 / quantum / 高 priority arrival 同級得一個就繼續跑
MLFQ 最高非空 queue 嘅 head 完成 / quantum / 高層 arrival preempted → head + 剩餘 quantum
Dynamic priority 計 init + α·waited + β·ran 每 check interval / 完成 tie 跟題目
LUF（F19） 用 CPU 最少 完成 + arrival descheduled → tail
RM period 最短 每個 release / 完成 static
EDF absolute deadline 最早 每個 release / 完成 dynamic；tie → keep running

MLFQ 追蹤表（考試用 rough paper 畫）：

t   | event            | Q0        | Q1 (rem q)   | Q2      | run (queue, q left, burst left)
0   | P1 arrives       | P1        |              |         | P1@Q0 q=4 b=9
4   | P1 q used        | P2        | P1(8)        |         | P2@Q0 ...

RM / EDF 追蹤：列 release times：task i 喺 0, pᵢ, 2pᵢ… release；deadline = release + dᵢ。逐個 time unit 揀；記低每個 job 完成時間，比較 deadline。

P4. Synchronization Playbook

Race 可能值（F19 Q4.3 / F20 Q5 type）

  1. 將 T1 拆做 atomic steps：read for condition → read b → read a → write c（c = b + a 由左至右讀）。
  2. T2 嘅 writes（次序固定）可以插入任何 gap。
  3. 分 branch（condition read 前 / 後寫）→ 每個 read 見新值 / 舊值 → 計 c。
  4. 列晒所有 distinct 值 + 每個值嘅 interleaving。

Semaphore 次序：每條 dependency「X 要喺 Y 前」→ semaphore s = 0：X 尾 signal(s)、Y 頭 wait(s)；多個前置 → 多個 wait。

Semaphore 序列可行性：逐字追蹤每個 semaphore 嘅值同每個 process 做到邊；任何 wait 時值必須 > 0；有 loop 嘅 process 可以重複。

Spinlock 任何 atomic instruction：while (instr(...) 返回「攞唔到」) ; + release 寫返 free 值：

Instruction（return） acquire release
test_and_set(&lock)（old） while (test_and_set(&lock)) ; lock = false;
compare_and_swap(&lock,0,1)（old） while (compare_and_swap(&lock,0,1) != 0) ; lock = 0;
swap(locked,1)（old） while (swap(locked,1) == 1) ; locked = 0;
compare_and_reset(&v,0,1)（1 = 成功） while (compare_and_reset(&v,0,1) == 0) ; v = 0;

Bounded-waiting TAS 檢查點：(1) cyclic scan j=(i+1)%n … j!=i；(2) 冇人等就 lock = false；(3) 有人等就 waiting[j] = false（直接交 lock）；三個性質：ME（只有第一個 TAS 得 key=false / 只放一個 waiting）、progress（exit 一定放 lock 或者叫醒一個）、bounded waiting（≤ n−1 turns）。

1Ch1 Introduction引言

📌 考試範圍提示：Ch1 主要出 MC（2–4 題） 同 short question（定義題）。最常考：caching / locality / average access time、von Neumann、storage hierarchy、SMP / multicore / NUMA、multiprogramming vs timesharing、virtualization / cloud。 ⚠️ 留意：今個 term（Fall 2026）嘅 slides 將 interrupt / DMA / dual-mode 放咗喺 Ch3 slides 3.21–3.31（舊 paper 寫 "1.40" 係舊版 slide number）。呢部分喺 Ch3 notes §0，但 MC 一樣會考。

1. What is an Operating System?（OS 係乜？）[1.20–1.24]

1.1 Computer system 四個組成部分 [1.20]

Component 解釋
Users 人、機器、其他電腦 / devices
Application programs 定義點樣用 system resources 去解決問題（editors, compilers, web browsers, database, games）
Operating system controls and coordinates use of computing resources among various applications and users
Hardware basic computing resources：CPU, memory, I/O devices

1.2 OS 嘅定義 [1.21, 1.23]

  • OS is a system software that acts as an intermediary between users (or application software) and computer hardware.（OS 係用戶/應用程式同硬件之間嘅中介）
  • OS hides the complicated details of the hardware 並提供 simple and easy-to-use interface（例如你處理 photos、web pages、files，而唔係 bytes 同 blocks）→ 呢個叫 abstraction。
  • There is no universally accepted definition of OS. "Everything a vendor ships when you order an operating system" 只係一個 approximation。
  • 兩個角色（必背）：
    • OS is a resource allocator — manages all resources (hardware and software); decides between conflicting requests for efficient and fair resource use.
    • OS is a control program — controls execution of programs to prevent errors and improper use of the computer.
  • 一句總結："OS manages and controls hardware and helps to facilitate programs to run on computers."

1.3 OS 嘅兩大目標（Chapter summary）

  1. User convenience — provides an environment for users to execute programs in a convenient, safe, protected and efficient manner.
  2. Resource allocation — allocates resources (hardware: CPU, memory; software: signals, locks) in a fair and efficient manner.

📝 HW1 MC3 例子：「幾個 job 喺 shared computer 等運行，OS 決定邊個 job 用 CPU、memory 點分配」→ OS acts as a resource allocator。

1.4 唔同環境嘅 OS [1.22]

環境 重點
Shared computers（mainframe / minicomputer） 要令所有 user 滿意 → performance vs. fairness
Individual systems（workstations） dedicated resources，重 performance 多過 fairness
Mobile devices resource constrained；touch screen、voice（Siri）；optimized for usability and battery life
Embedded systems 好少或無 UI；OS pre-installed in ROM（冷氣機、汽車、太空船）
Real-time systems strict time constraints

1.5 常見名詞 [1.24]

  • Kernel — "the one program running at all times on the computer"（核心功能）。
  • Middleware — a set of software frameworks providing additional services to application developers（databases, multimedia, graphics）；mobile OS（iOS, Android）好常見。
  • System programs — ships with the OS but not part of the kernel（例如 compilers）。
  • Application programs — 同 OS 無關嘅程式（App Store 啲 app）。
  • OS = always-running kernel + middleware frameworks + system programs.

2. Computer System Organization（電腦系統組織）[1.25–1.27]

  • One or more CPU cores 同 device controllers 經 common bus 連接，share memory。[1.25]
  • CPUs and devices execute concurrently，compete for memory cycles through the shared bus。
  • 每個 device controller 管一類 device，有自己嘅 local buffer；OS 用 device driver 同 controller 溝通（device driver = kernel module that controls a device）。
  • [1.27] 圖：processor + cache ↔ bridge/memory controller ↔ memory；PCI bus 連 graphics controller、SCSI controller、IDE disk controller、expansion bus interface（keyboard, parallel/serial port）。

2.1 von Neumann Architecture [1.26] ⭐（MC 常客）

  • Stored-program architecture：instructions and data are stored in the same memory（同一個 memory space，用 address 存取）。
  • CPU contains an ALU (arithmetic logic unit) and processor registers — PC (Program Counter), AC (Accumulator), MAR (Memory Address Register), MDR (Memory Data Register)。
  • Control unit — IR (Instruction Register) and PC。
  • Memory stores data and instructions（along with caches）；external mass storage = secondary storage；input and output mechanisms。
  • Instruction execution steps（instruction–execution cycle）：
    1. Fetch instruction（from memory/cache → 放入 IR）
    2. Decode instruction
    3. Fetch data（operands → data registers）
    4. Execute instruction
    5. Write back（if any，結果寫返 memory/cache）
  • Programs and data must be brought into memory before execution.
  • MMU (memory management unit) sees only a stream of memory addresses — 佢唔知 address 點產生、亦唔知係 instruction 定 data。

⚠️ 陷阱：

  • "Instructions and data are stored in separate physical memories" ✗（嗰個係 Harvard architecture）。
  • "PC stored in external mass storage" ✗；"CPU has no ALU" ✗。
  • Sp21 MC：「processing unit contains a/an ___ and processor registers」→ ALU（唔係 "all of the mentioned"，因為 IR/PC 屬 control unit）。

3. Storage（儲存）[1.28–1.35]

3.1 單位 [1.28]

  • bit（0/1）；byte = 8 bits（大部分電腦最細嘅 convenient chunk，冇 move 1 bit 嘅 instruction，但有 move 1 byte）。
  • word = 一個 architecture 嘅 native unit of data（64-bit registers → 64-bit (8-byte) word）。
  • KB = 1,024 bytes；MB = 1,024² bytes；GB = 1,024³；TB = 1,024⁴；PB = 1,024⁵。
  • 廠商通常 round off（MB ≈ 1 million bytes）；networking measured in bits（Mb/s, Gb/s）。

3.2 Storage Hierarchy [1.29] ⭐

按 speed, cost per unit, capacity (size), volatility 分層：

           registers        ┐
           cache            │ primary storage   (volatile)
           main memory      ┘
 - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - -
           nonvolatile memory (NVM) ┐ secondary storage (nonvolatile)
           hard-disk drives (HDD)   ┘
           optical disk     ┐ tertiary storage
           magnetic tapes   ┘
  上面：smaller, faster, more expensive per byte
  下面：larger, slower, cheaper per byte
  • 越近 CPU → 越快、越細、每 byte 越貴；越落 → cost per byte 減、access time 增、capacity 增。

3.3 Main Memory [1.30]

  • Main memory is the only large storage media that the CPU can access directly.（CPU 係 indirectly 存取 secondary storage）
  • Volatile；通常係 DRAM (Dynamic Random-Access Memory)。
  • load / store instructions to specific addresses；byte addressable — each address refers to one byte。⭐（S26 MC3 答案）
  • 開機第一個程式 bootstrap program 存喺 EEPROM（firmware）。

3.4 Secondary Storage [1.31]

  • Extension of main memory providing large non-volatile storage capacity。
  • 最常見：HDDs 同 NVM devices（例如 SSD）。
  • Mechanical（HDD, optical disk, magnetic tape）vs Electrical（flash, SSD, FRAM, NRAM → 統稱 NVM）。
  • Mechanical: larger and less expensive per byte. Electrical: more costly, smaller, more reliable, and faster.

3.5 Characteristics of storage [1.34]（睇表記「Managed by」）

Level 1 registers 2 cache 3 main memory 4 SSD 5 magnetic disk
Typical size < 1 KB < 16 MB < 64 GB < 1 TB < 10 TB
Technology custom memory, multiple ports CMOS on/off-chip CMOS SRAM CMOS SRAM（slide 原文） flash memory magnetic disk
Access time (ns) 0.25–0.5 0.5–25 80–250 25,000–50,000 5,000,000
Bandwidth (MB/s) 20,000–100,000 5,000–10,000 1,000–5,000 500 20–150
Managed by compiler hardware operating system OS OS
Backed by cache main memory disk disk disk or tape
  • Movement between levels of storage hierarchy can be explicit or implicit.（例如 cache ↔ memory 由 hardware 隱式處理；memory ↔ disk 由 OS 顯式處理）

3.6 Range of Timescales [1.35]（Jeff Dean "Numbers Everyone Should Know"）

L1 cache 0.5 ns；branch mispredict 5 ns；L2 cache 7 ns；mutex lock/unlock 25 ns；main memory reference 100 ns；compress 1KB 3 µs；send 2KB over 1 Gbps 20 µs；read 1MB from memory 250 µs；round trip in datacenter 500 µs；disk seek 10 ms；read 1MB from disk 20 ms；packet CA→Netherlands→CA 150 ms。

4. Caching ⭐⭐（必考 short question）[1.32–1.33]

4.1 Fundamental idea

📝 Model answer：The fundamental idea of caching is to temporarily copy a subset of information from a slower but larger storage to a faster but smaller storage, so that frequently used data can be accessed more quickly.（make frequently used case faster, less frequent case less dominant）

  • Caching performed at many levels：memory、address translation、file blocks、file names、file directories、network routes…
  • 存取時先 check cache：
    • Hit — information is in the cache → used directly (fast)
    • Miss — not in cache → data copied from slower storage to cache and used there
  • Cache is usually much smaller than the storage being cached.
  • Cache management：cache size 同 replacement policy。
  • Major criterion: cache hit ratio（percentage of content found in cache）。

4.2 Average access time（公式必背）

  • Miss Ratio = 1 − Hit Ratio。
  • HW1 例：hit ratio 90%、hit time 2 ns、miss time 50 ns → 0.9×2 + 0.1×50 = 1.8 + 5 = 6.8 ns。
  • 如果題目畀 miss penalty（額外時間）而唔係 miss time：Miss Time = Hit Time + Miss Penalty → AMAT = Hit Time + Miss Ratio × Miss Penalty（兩條公式結果一樣）。

📝 S26 Q2.2 Model answer（high hit ratio 夠唔夠？）：No. Cache performance is not judged only by cache hit ratio. Although hit ratio is a major criterion, the overall performance should be evaluated using the average access time, which depends on both the hit time and the miss time (miss penalty). A cache with a high hit ratio but a long hit time or a huge miss penalty can still perform poorly.

🧮 Average Access Time = (Hit Ratio × Hit Time) + (Miss Ratio × Miss Time)

4.3 Locality — why caching works ⭐

  • Temporal locality (locality in time) — recently accessed items are likely to be accessed again in the near future.
    • 例：loop counter i、loop 入面重複執行嘅 instructions、成日 call 嘅 function。
  • Spatial locality (locality in space) — items near (contiguous to) the recently accessed items are likely to be accessed shortly（data 同 program 都係）。
    • 例：順序讀 array a[0], a[1], a[2]…；順序執行 instructions。
  • Without access locality（e.g. all items accessed with equal probability）, cache would never work!
  • F20 MC1："Locality is essential to ___" → Cache。

5. Processor Systems（處理器系統）[1.36–1.41]

5.1 Single-Processor Systems [1.36]

  • 一個 CPU，single processing core；core 執行 instructions，有 registers 存 data。
  • 仲有 special-purpose processors（device-specific：disk controller、GPU）→ 跑 limited instruction set，do not execute instructions from user processes。

5.2 Multiprocessor Systems [1.37] ⭐

  • 而家由 mobile 到 server 都係 multiprocessor 主導。
  • Speed-up ratio with N processors is less than N — 因為 overhead, e.g., contention for shared resources (bus or memory)。
  • 三大優點（必背）：
    1. Increased throughput — more computing capability
    2. Economy of scale — share other devices（I/O devices, power supplies, housings, peripherals）→ 平過多部單 CPU 機
    3. Increased reliability — graceful degradation or fault tolerance
  • 缺點（summary）：hardware design 同 software（parallel programming）都複雜好多。
  • 兩類：
    • Asymmetric multiprocessing (AMP) — master–slave：master assigns specific tasks to slaves and handles I/O。（Ch5：master 可以變 bottleneck）
    • Symmetric multiprocessing (SMP) — each processor performs all tasks, including OS functions and user processes；I/O 可以由任何 processor 處理。

5.3 SMP [1.38]

  • Each CPU has its own set of registers and a private (local) cache; all processors share physical memory through the system bus.

5.4 Multicore [1.39] ⭐

  • Multiple computing cores reside on a single physical chip.
  • 優點：faster on-chip communication than between-chip communication；uses significantly less power（mobile/laptop 重要）。

📝 F19 Q2.5：Multicore SMP systems are faster and consume less power than systems in which each CPU has its own physical chip (on-chip communication is faster).

5.5 NUMA (Non-Uniform Memory Access) [1.40] ⭐

  • 問題：加更多 CPU 未必 scale，因為 contention for the system bus → bottleneck。
  • 解決：each CPU (or group of CPUs) has its own local memory accessed via a small, fast local bus；CPUs connected by a shared system interconnect；all CPUs share one physical memory address space。
  • Drawback: increased latency when a CPU must access remote memory across the system interconnect → 影響 scheduling and memory management（Ch5 processor affinity）。

⚠️ HW1 MC4：「In NUMA, every CPU accesses every part of memory with exactly the same latency」→ False（Non-uniform 就係唔同 latency）。

5.6 名詞定義 [1.41]（MC 易混）

Term Definition
CPU The hardware that executes instructions
Processor A physical chip that contains one or more CPUs
Core The basic computation unit of the CPU（executes instructions + registers）
Multicore Multiple computing cores on a single physical processor chip
Multiprocessor Including multiple processors

6. Multiprogramming & Timesharing ⭐ [1.42]

  • 所有 modern OS 都有兩個特徵：
    1. Multiprogramming (batch system) — needed for efficiency。
      • 以前一次 load 一個 program；single program cannot keep CPU or I/O devices busy at all times。
      • Multiprogramming organizes jobs so that the CPU always has one to execute.（一個 job 等 I/O → CPU 轉做另一個 job）
      • Mainframe：jobs submitted remotely and queued → job scheduling 揀邊啲 load 入 memory。
    2. Timesharing (multitasking) — logical extension of multiprogramming in which the CPU switches jobs so frequently that users can interact with each job while it is running → interactive computing。
      • Response time should be under a certain threshold（通常 < 1 秒）。
  • 相關概念：
    • A program in execution → process
    • Several processes ready at the same time → CPU scheduling
    • Processes don't fit in memory → swapping moves them in and out
    • Virtual memory allows execution of processes not completely in memory

📝 HW1 Q2.2 Model answer：Multiprogramming keeps several jobs in memory at the same time. When the running job has to wait (e.g., for I/O), the OS switches the CPU to another job, so the CPU always has something to execute and CPU utilization increases. Time sharing (multitasking) extends multiprogramming by switching the CPU among jobs so frequently (using CPU scheduling and timer interrupts) that each user gets a fast response time and can interact with the program while it is running — this supports interactive computing.

7. Virtualization ⭐ [1.43–1.46]

  • Virtualization abstracts the hardware of a single computer into multiple different execution environments，令每個 user/program 以為自己有部 "private computer"。
  • 創造 virtual machine (VM)，OS 同 applications 可以喺上面跑。
  • It allows an operating system to run as an application within another operating system. ⭐（F20 MC2 答案：Virtualization，唔係 Emulation）
  • Components [1.44]：
    • Host — underlying hardware system
    • Virtual machine manager (VMM) or hypervisor — creates and manages virtual machines by providing an interface identical to the host
    • Guest OS — process provided with a virtual copy of the host（通常係一個 OS）
  • Motivations [1.43]：
    • Server consolidation — 將多個 OS 合併到較少 hardware 上
    • Desktop 上跑一個 OS（Linux/macOS）但仍用到另一個平台（Windows）嘅 native apps
    • Developers can run many OS types/versions on one machine → easier testing and debugging
  • [1.45] 圖：(a) processes → kernel → hardware；(b) 每個 VM 有自己 kernel，VM1/VM2/VM3 → VMM → hardware。
  • History [1.46]：IBM mainframes (1972)；VMware 喺 Intel x86 跑多個 Windows guest；programs in a VM show only minor performance decreases（多咗幾層 software）；VMM is in complete control of system resources；1990s 後期 Intel CPU 夠快；Xen、VMware。
  • Emulation vs Virtualization（課本背景）：Emulation = 模擬 different CPU architecture（source CPU ≠ target CPU，慢）；Virtualization = guest OS natively compiled for the CPU。

⚠️ HW1 MC8：「An application always runs faster inside a VM」→ False（只會 minor performance decrease）。

8. Cloud Computing [1.47–1.48]

  • Delivers computing, storage, and apps as a service over a network.
  • Logical extension of virtualization（用 virtualization 作基礎）；例：Amazon EC2（pay based on usage）。
  • Types：
    • Public cloud — available via Internet to anyone willing to pay
    • Private cloud — run by a company for its own use
    • Hybrid cloud — both public and private components
  • Service models（MC 易考 match）：
    • SaaS (Software as a Service) — applications via Internet（e.g. word processor）
    • PaaS (Platform as a Service) — software stack ready for application use（e.g. database server）
    • IaaS (Infrastructure as a Service) — servers or storage over Internet（e.g. storage for backup）
    • MaaS — Machine learning as a Service

9. I/O Subsystem（Chapter 1 summary 提及）

  • I/O 硬件三元素：buses, device controllers, devices。
  • 搬 data（device ↔ main memory）：CPU in programmed I/O 或者 offloaded to a DMA controller。
  • Device driver = kernel module that controls a device。
  • Kernel I/O subsystem services：I/O scheduling, buffering, caching, spooling, device reservation, error handling。
  • （Interrupt、DMA、dual-mode 詳細見 Ch3 notes §0（slides 3.21–3.31））

10. 常見陷阱 & MC 速查（Traps）

敘述 真/假 原因
Main memory is byte addressable ✔ each address refers to one byte [1.30]
Main memory is nonvolatile ✘ DRAM 係 volatile
Registers are slower than main memory ✘ registers 最快
HDD is smaller than volatile memory ✘ HDD 大好多
CPU can directly access secondary storage ✘ 只可 directly access main memory
Instructions and data stored in same memory (von Neumann) ✔ stored-program
Speed-up with N processors = N ✘ < N due to overhead/contention
Multicore uses less power than multiple single-core chips ✔ [1.39]
NUMA: same latency for all memory ✘ remote memory slower
SMP: each processor has private cache, shares memory ✔ [1.38]
AMP: master handles I/O ✔ [1.37]
Timesharing is an extension of multiprogramming ✔ [1.42]
VM always faster than native ✘ minor slowdown
Virtualization lets an OS run as an app within another OS ✔ [1.43]
Cache works even with uniform random access ✘ needs locality
High hit ratio alone guarantees performance ✘ also hit time & miss time
Cache is managed by OS ✘ hardware（[1.34]）；registers by compiler

11. Past-paper 題目（Ch1 相關）

  1. S26 MC3：storage hierarchy 正確嘅係？→ D. Main memory is byte addressable。
  2. S26 MC4：von Neumann 核心特徵？→ B. Instructions and data stored in the same memory space, accessed by address。
  3. S26 Q2.2：caching 基本概念 + 高 hit ratio 夠唔夠？→ 見 §4。
  4. F19 Q1.2：temporal vs spatial locality？→ 見 §4.3。
  5. F20 MC1：Locality is essential to → Cache。
  6. F20 MC2：___ allows a guest OS to run as an application on a native OS → Virtualization。
  7. Sp21 MC1：von Neumann processing unit contains a/an ___ and processor registers → ALU。
  8. HW1 MC3 resource allocator；MC4 NUMA same latency → false；MC8 VM always faster → false；Q2.1 AMAT = 6.8 ns；Q2.2 multiprogramming vs timesharing。

12. 自我測驗（Self-test）

  1. 寫出 average access time 公式；hit ratio 95%、hit time 1 ns、miss time 100 ns？（答：0.95 + 5 = 5.95 ns）
  2. 點解 N 個 processors speedup < N？（contention for shared resources e.g. bus/memory, overhead）
  3. Multiprocessor 三個優點？（throughput, economy of scale, reliability/graceful degradation）
  4. NUMA 嘅 drawback？（remote memory access latency）
  5. VMM 又叫咩？做咩？（hypervisor; creates and manages VMs, interface identical to host）
  6. Cache 由邊個 manage？Main memory 呢？（hardware；OS）

2Ch2 OS Structures作業系統結構

📌 考試範圍提示：Ch2 每年都有 3–5 題 MC + 1–2 題 short question。必考：API vs system call（點解要分開）、printf() → write()、parameter passing 三種方法、linker vs loader、monolithic / layered / microkernel / LKM / hybrid 比較（優缺點）、policy vs mechanism、OS services。 ⚠️ Dual-mode / interrupt / trap 今個 term 放咗喺 Ch3 slides（3.x），見 Ch3 notes §0。

1. Operating System Services（OS 服務）[2.4–2.7]

OS provides an environment for execution of programs and other services to programs and users。分兩組：

1.1 對 user 有用嘅服務（helpful to the user）

Service 解釋
User interface (UI) CLI (command-line), GUI, touch-screen
Program execution load a program into memory and run it; end execution normally or abnormally (indicating error)
I/O operations running program may require I/O（file 或 I/O device）
File-system manipulation read/write/create/delete/search files & directories, list info, permission management
Communications processes exchange information（同一部機或 network）via shared memory or message passing (packets moved by the OS)
Error detection errors may occur in CPU and memory, in I/O devices, in user programs；OS takes appropriate actions；debugging facilities

1.2 確保系統本身有效運作（efficient operation via resource sharing）

Service 解釋
Resource allocation 多 user/job 同時跑 → 分配 CPU cycles, memory, file storage, I/O devices
Logging (accounting) keep track of which users use how much and what kinds of computer resources
Protection and security Protection: ensure all access to system resources is controlled；Security: defend from outsiders → user authentication, defend external I/O devices from invalid access

⚠️ S26 MC5 陷阱：「Errors only occur in memory and I/O devices」→ False（CPU、memory、I/O devices、user programs 都可以出錯）。 ✔「Logging service should keep track of computer resources that each user uses」→ True。

  • Summary 補充：OS services 兩大類 —（1）enforce protection among concurrent processes（process 只可以存取自己 address space；唔可以直接存取 hardware）；（2）provide functionalities not directly supported by hardware（memory management、file systems）。
  • [2.7/2.13 圖] user & system programs → user interfaces (GUI/touch/CLI) → system calls → services (program execution, I/O, file systems, communication, resource allocation, accounting, error detection, protection & security) → OS → hardware。

2. User Interfaces [2.8–2.12]

  • CLI / command interpreter — allows direct command entry。
    • Sometimes implemented in kernel, sometimes by system programs.
    • 有多個 flavor → shells（C shell, Bourne-Again shell (bash), Korn shell）。
    • Shell 嘅主要功能：(1) fetches a command from user, (2) interprets it, (3) executes it. ⭐（PA1 myshell 就係做呢樣）
  • GUI — user-friendly desktop metaphor；icons；mouse；invented at Xerox PARC (early 1970s)，first widely used in Apple Macintosh (1984)。Windows = GUI + CLI "command" shell；Mac OS X = "Aqua" GUI + UNIX kernel；Unix/Linux = CLI + optional GUI（KDE, GNOME）。
  • Touchscreen — gestures, virtual keyboard, voice command（Siri）。

3. System Calls ⭐⭐ [2.13–2.26]

3.1 定義

  • System calls provide the programming interface to the services provided by the OS.（Sp21 MC：「___ provide an interface to the services provided by an OS」→ System calls）
  • 通常以 high-level language (C/C++) functions 提供；certain low-level tasks written in assembly for accessing hardware。
  • 例：cp in.txt out.txt 涉及一連串 system calls（acquire input file name、open input file、create output file、loop read/write、close、write completion message、terminate normally）。

3.2 API (Application Programming Interface) [2.15]

  • API specifies a set of functions available to application programmers, including the parameters passed to each function and the return values it may expect.
  • The functions that make up an API invoke the actual system calls on behalf of the programmer.
    • 例：Windows CreateProcess() invokes NTCreateProcess() system call。
  • Programmer 經 OS 提供嘅 library 用 API；UNIX/Linux C → libc。
  • 三大 API：Win32 API（Windows）、POSIX API（UNIX, Linux, Mac OS X）、Java API（JVM）。
  • [2.16] ssize_t read(int fd, void *buf, size_t count) — fd file descriptor；buf buffer；count max bytes；return 0 = end of file；−1 = error。

3.3 點解用 API 而唔直接 call system call？⭐（F19 Q1.3、S26 MC6）[2.17]

📝 Model answer：(1) Program portability — a program using an API can compile and run on any system that supports the same API, even though the implementation of system calls varies from machine to machine. (2) Hide the complex details of system calls — actual system calls are often more detailed and difficult to work with; the caller only needs to obey the API and understand what the OS will do, not how the system call is implemented.

  • S26 MC6 答案：APIs provide a more convenient programming interface。
  • ✘「APIs always execute faster than system calls」、✘「APIs can bypass kernel protection」、✘「APIs eliminate the need for linkers and loaders」。
  • F20 MC4：「Programmers interface with ___ to obtain services from the OS」→ APIs（best answer）。

3.4 System Call Implementation [2.18–2.19]

  • 大部分語言嘅 run-time environment (RTE)（a set of functions built into libraries）提供 system-call interface，作為連接 OS system calls 嘅 link。
  • Each system call is associated with an identity number；system-call interface maintains a table indexed by these numbers。
  • System-call interface 做三件事：intercepts function calls in the API → invokes the necessary system call within the OS → returns the status of the system call and return value(s)。
  • The caller knows nothing about how a system call is implemented.（只需要 obey API）
  • [2.19 圖]：user application 呼叫 open() → (user mode) system call interface → (kernel mode) 用 index i 查 table → implementation of open() → return。

📝 HW1 Q2.4：user program call read() 點樣 user→kernel→user？（三步）

  1. The program calls the read() API in libc (user mode); the library/system-call interface puts the system-call number and parameters in registers (or a block/stack) and executes a trap (system-call) instruction.
  2. The trap switches the CPU to kernel mode (mode bit 1→0); the kernel uses the system-call number to index the system-call table and runs the read() service routine (which may block waiting for the device).
  3. When finished, the kernel puts the return value/status in a register, executes a return-from-trap which resets the mode to user mode, and control returns to the instruction after the call.
  • HW1 MC6 答案：The API and run-time environment use the system-call interface to invoke the corresponding kernel service, and control later returns to the application.

3.5 System Call Parameter Passing ⭐ [2.20–2.21]

三種方法（F20 MC5：All of the above）：

  1. Registers — simplest；但可能 parameters 多過 registers。
  2. Block (table) method — parameters stored in a block/table in memory, address of the block passed in a register（Linux & Solaris 用）。
  3. Stack method — parameters pushed onto the stack by the program and popped off by the OS。
  • Block and stack methods do not limit the number or length of parameters.

3.6 Types of System Calls（六類）⭐ [2.22–2.25]

類別 例子 UNIX 例 Windows 例
Process control create/terminate process, end/abort, load/execute, get/set attributes, wait for time, wait/signal event, allocate/free memory, dump memory, debugger, locks fork(), exit(), wait() CreateProcess(), ExitProcess(), WaitForSingleObject()
File management create/delete, open/close, read/write/reposition, get/set attributes open(), read(), write(), close() CreateFile(), ReadFile(), WriteFile(), CloseHandle()
Device management request/release device, read/write, get/set attributes, attach/detach ioctl(), read(), write() SetConsoleMode(), ReadConsole(), WriteConsole()
Information maintenance get/set time/date, system data, attributes getpid(), alarm(), sleep() GetCurrentProcessID(), SetTimer(), Sleep()
Communications create/delete connection, send/receive messages (message passing), shared-memory create/access regions pipe(), shm_open(), mmap() CreatePipe(), CreateFileMapping(), MapViewOfFile()
Protection control access, get/set permissions, allow/deny user access chmod(), umask(), chown() SetFileSecurity(), ...

3.7 Standard C Library Example ⭐（S26 MC8）[2.26]

  • C program invoking printf() library call, which calls the write() system call.
  • ✔ "printf() invokes write() system call to get service from OS"
  • ✘ 「printf() and write() are both system calls」（printf 係 library function）
  • ✘ 「program enters kernel mode immediately when printf() is called」（printf 喺 user mode 處理 formatting/buffering，到 write() 先 trap 入 kernel）
  • ✘ 「write() invokes printf()」

4. System Programs（系統程式）[2.27–2.30]

  • System services / system programs / system utilities provide a convenient environment for program development and execution.
  • 有啲只係 user interfaces to system calls；有啲複雜得多。
  • The view of the OS seen by most users is defined by the application and system programs, not the actual system calls.（GUI 同 CLI 用同一套 system calls，但 user 睇落好唔同）
  • 類別：
    • File management — create, delete, copy, rename, print, list files/directories
    • Status information — date, time, available memory, disk space, number of users；performance, logging, debugging info
    • File modification — text editors；search/transform file contents
    • Programming-language support — compilers, assemblers, debuggers, interpreters
    • Program loading and execution — absolute loaders, relocatable loaders, linkage editors, overlay loaders, debugging systems
    • Communications — virtual connections among processes/users/systems（message, web, email, remote login, file transfer）
    • Background services — launch at boot；有啲跑到系統關機 → services, subsystems, daemons（disk checking, process scheduling, error logging）
  • Application programs — not part of the OS（browsers, word processors, spreadsheets, databases, games）。

5. Linkers and Loaders ⭐⭐（F19 Q1.1、F20 Q2.2 都考過）[2.31–2.32]

main.c --(compiler: gcc -c main.c)--> main.o (relocatable object file)
main.o + other object files + libraries --(linker: gcc -o main main.o -lm)--> main (executable file)
main --(loader: ./main)--> program in memory  (+ dynamically linked libraries)
  • Source code is compiled into relocatable object files（designed to be loaded into any physical memory location）。
  • Linker combines these relocatable object files into a single binary executable file（along with libraries if needed, e.g. -lm math library）。
  • Programs reside on secondary storage as binary executables, which must be brought into memory by the loader to be executed.
  • Relocation assigns final addresses to program parts and adjusts code & data to match those addresses。
  • Modern OS don't link libraries statically；而係用 dynamically linked libraries (DLLs in Windows) — loaded when needed, shared by all programs using the same version (loaded only once) → 慳 memory，避免 link 用唔到嘅 library。
  • Object files / executables 有 standard formats（machine code + symbol table；Linux = ELF）→ OS 知點 load 同 start。

📝 Model answer：The linker combines several relocatable object files (generated by the compiler), together with any needed libraries, into a single binary executable file stored on disk. The loader loads the binary executable file into memory (possibly together with dynamically linked libraries) so that it can be run on a CPU.

6. OS Design and Implementation [2.33–2.37]

6.1 Design goals [2.33]

  • Design 受 choice of hardware, type of system（desktop, mobile, distributed, real-time）影響；先定 goals and specifications。
  • User goals：convenient to use, easy to learn and use, reliable, safe, fast。
  • System goals：easy to design, implement, maintain; flexible, reliable, error-free, efficient。

6.2 Policy vs Mechanism ⭐ [2.34]

  • Policy: What will be done?　Mechanism: How to do it?
  • 例：A timer is a mechanism for ensuring CPU protection, but deciding how long the timer is set for a particular user is a policy decision.
  • Separation of policy from mechanism allows flexibility if policy decisions are changed later（同一個 mechanism 可以支持「I/O-intensive 優先」或者相反嘅 policy）。
  • 總結：goals → determine policies → implemented through mechanisms。

6.3 Implementation [2.35]

  • 早期用 assembly；而家多數用 C/C++；最底層 kernel 可能仍用 assembly。Android：system libraries C/C++，frameworks Java。
  • Advantages of high-level languages：easier to port to other hardware；code written faster, more compact, easier to understand and debug；compiler 進步 → recompile 就變好。
  • Disadvantages：reduced speed、increased storage requirements（今日唔係大問題）。

6.4 Examples [2.36–2.37]

  • MS-DOS：single-tasking；開機 invoke shell；no process created；single memory space；load program overwriting all but the kernel；program exit → shell reloaded。
  • FreeBSD（Unix variant）：multitasking；user login → shell；shell executes fork() to create a new process, exec() to load a new program into the process；shell waits for the process to terminate or continues with user commands（background）；需要 CPU scheduling、process coordination、memory management。

7. Operating System Structure ⭐⭐⭐（必考比較題）[2.38–2.53]

7.0 一覽表

Structure 核心概念 優點 缺點 例子
Simple structure 無 well-defined structure most functionality in least space interfaces/levels 唔分明；app 可直接 access I/O；no dual mode/no hardware protection MS-DOS
Monolithic all kernel functionality in a single, static binary file running in a single address space performance：system-call interface overhead 好少，kernel 內部溝通快 difficult to implement, debug, maintain/modify；tightly coupled Original UNIX, Linux, Windows（largely）
Layered layer 0 = hardware … layer N = user interface；每層只用下層服務 simplicity of construction and debugging；information hiding 難定義 layers；performance overhead（traverse 多層） TCP/IP networks；(pure layered OS 好少)
Microkernel remove nonessential components from kernel, implement them as user-level programs in separate address spaces；靠 message passing easier to extend、easier to port、more secure and reliable（less code in kernel mode） performance suffers due to increased system-function overhead / user–kernel communication / message copying Mach, Darwin（macOS/iOS 部分）, Windows NT（layered microkernel）
Loadable kernel modules (LKMs) core kernel + dynamically loaded modules（boot time or run time） 唔使 recompile/reboot；any module can call any other module；no message passing → more efficient than microkernel （仍喺 kernel mode，module 出錯可影響 kernel） Linux（device drivers, file systems）, Solaris
Hybrid 混合多種結構 performance + security + usability 複雜 Linux（monolithic + modular）, Windows（monolithic + microkernel-like personalities + LKMs）, macOS/iOS（Darwin = Mach + BSD）

7.1 Simple Structure — MS-DOS [2.39]

  • Written to provide the most functionality in the least space；not carefully divided into modules。
  • Application programs can access I/O directly（interfaces/levels 唔分開）。
  • Written for Intel 8088 — no dual mode and no hardware protection。

7.2 Monolithic Structure — Original UNIX ⭐ [2.40–2.42]

  • Traditional UNIX kernel = everything below the system-call interface and above the physical hardware.
  • Place all functionality of the kernel into a single, static binary file that runs in a single address space.
  • UNIX = two separable parts: kernel + system programs；kernel 再分成 interfaces 同 device drivers。
  • Drawback：enormous functionalities combined into one level → difficult to implement, debug, and maintain。
  • Advantage：distinct performance advantage — very little overhead in the system-call interface, and communication within the kernel is fast. → 所以 UNIX、Linux、Windows 仍然用。
  • Sp21 MC：Original UNIX 採用 → Monolithic structure。
  • Linux [2.42]：based on UNIX；monolithic — runs entirely in kernel mode in a single address space；apps 用 glibc 同 system call interface 溝通；modular design 可以 run time 改 kernel（LKM）。

7.3 Modular Design [2.43]

  • Monolithic = tightly coupled system（改一部分影響其他部分）。
  • Loosely coupled system：分成細 components，每個有 specific/limited 功能 → changes in one component affect only that component → 更自由靈活。
  • Layered approach 係 modular design 嘅其中一種。

7.4 Layered Approach ⭐ [2.44–2.45]

  • OS divided into layers, each built on top of lower layers. Bottom layer (layer 0) = hardware; highest (layer N) = user interface.
  • 每層有 data structures + functions，可被上層調用、可調用下層。
  • Information hiding：上層唔需要知下層點 implement，只需知 what these operations do（interface）；每層隱藏自己嘅 data structures、operations、hardware。
  • Main advantage：simplicity of construction and debugging（由最底層開始逐層 debug）。
  • 成功例子：computer networks（TCP/IP）、web applications。
  • Few OSes use a pure layered approach because：
    1. Significant challenges in appropriately defining layers and their functionalities
    2. Overall performance affected by the overhead of traversing multiple layers to obtain a service
  • Trend：fewer layers with more functionality，modularized code。
  • S26 MC9 ✔：The interfaces between adjacent layers are usually well defined。✘ any layer may directly access any other layer；✘ eliminates all performance overhead；✘ pure layered is dominant。

7.5 Microkernel ⭐⭐ [2.46–2.48]

  • Kernel 變大難管理 → removing all nonessential components from the kernel and implementing them as system or user-level programs in separate address spaces → much smaller kernel。
  • Mach（CMU, mid-1980s）；best-known：Darwin（Mac OS X/iOS，包含 Mach microkernel）。
  • 微核心通常提供：minimal process and memory management + communication facility (IPC)。
  • Main function：provide communication between client programs and services running in user space through message passing. 例：app 要讀 file → 同 file server 經 microkernel exchange messages，兩者唔直接 interact。
  • Advantages：
    • Easier to extend — new services added to user space without modifying the kernel
    • Kernel 要改時改動較少（kernel 細）
    • Easier to port to new architectures
    • More secure and reliable — less code in kernel mode；if a service fails, the rest of the OS remains untouched
  • Drawbacks：
    • Performance can suffer due to increased system-function overhead (user space ↔ kernel space communication)
    • Two user-level services communicate → messages must be copied between services in separate address spaces
    • Windows NT（layered microkernel）比 Windows 95 慢 → Windows XP 變返 more monolithic。

📝 F20 Q2.1 Model answer：Main idea: a microkernel removes all nonessential components from the kernel and implements them as system or user-level programs in separate address spaces; the kernel keeps only minimal process/memory management and IPC (message passing). Advantage: easier to extend/port, more secure and reliable since less code runs in kernel mode. Disadvantage: performance suffers due to increased system-function overhead — message passing / copying between user space and kernel space.

⚠️ S26 MC7：「microkernel typically has better performance than monolithic」→ False。（「runs memory management in user space」亦被接受為 false，因為 minimal memory management 喺 kernel 入面。）HW1 MC5 ✔「microkernel keeps only essential functions in the kernel and provides other services outside the kernel through message passing」。

7.6 Loadable Kernel Modules (LKMs) — Modular Approach ⭐⭐ [2.49–2.51]

  • Best current methodology for OS design.
  • Kernel has a set of core components and links in additional services via modules, either at boot time or during run time.
  • 好處：dynamic linking is preferable to adding features directly to the kernel, which would require recompiling the entire kernel every time。例：kernel 內有 CPU scheduling + memory management，file systems 經 loadable modules 加入。
  • Similar to layered：each kernel section has well-defined, protected interfaces；but more flexible — any module can call any other module（無上下層限制）。
  • Similar to microkernel：primary module 只有 core functions + 知點 load/communicate 其他 modules；but more efficient — modules do not need message passing to communicate。
  • Linux uses LKMs primarily for device drivers and file systems；可以 boot/run time insert 或 remove。例：插 USB，kernel 冇 driver → dynamic load。
  • → dynamic and modular kernel while keeping the performance benefits of a monolithic system。
  • [2.51] Solaris：core kernel + scheduling classes, file systems, loadable system calls, executable formats, STREAMS modules, misc modules, device & bus drivers。
  • F20 MC6：「core components in kernel + adds services via modules during run time」→ LKMs。

📝 F19 Q1.4 Model answer（LKM vs layered）：Similarity: like a layered design, OS functionality is divided into modules, each with a well-defined (protected) interface. Difference: LKM is much more flexible — any module can call any other module directly, with no higher/lower layer restriction (and modules can be loaded/removed at run time).

7.7 Hybrid Systems [2.52–2.59]

  • Very few OSes adopt a single strictly defined structure → hybrid systems combine approaches for performance, security, usability。
  • Linux：monolithic（single address space → efficient）+ modular（LKM）。
  • Windows：largely monolithic，但保留 microkernel 特色 — separate subsystems (personalities) running as user-mode processes；亦支援 dynamically loadable kernel modules。
  • macOS / iOS [2.54–2.57]：kernel environment Darwin = Mach microkernel + BSD UNIX kernel（XNU kernel，hybrid）。
    • Two system-call interfaces：Mach system calls (traps) + BSD system calls (POSIX)。
    • Mach → memory management, CPU scheduling, IPC；BSD → POSIX API, networking, file system, security。
    • I/O kit for device drivers；dynamically loadable modules = kernel extensions (kexts)。
    • Mac：Aqua UI（mouse/trackpad）、Cocoa（Objective-C API）、Core frameworks（QuickTime, OpenGL）。
    • iOS：Springboard UI（touch）、Cocoa Touch、Media services、Core services（cloud, databases）；ARM（vs Intel）；不能原生跑 Mac apps。
  • Android [2.58–2.59]：Open Handset Alliance（Google）；open-source（iOS closed-source）；layered；Android API for Java → 跑喺 Android RunTime (ART)（VM optimized for mobile）；JNI (Java Native Interface) bypass VM 存取 hardware（not portable）；libraries：webkit, SQLite, SSL；HAL (hardware abstraction layer) → consistent view；Bionic C library（唔係 glibc）；modified Linux kernel（power management）。
  • iOS vs Android（summary）：相同 — 都基於現有 kernel、software stack、developer frameworks；唔同 — iOS closed / Android open；Objective-C vs Java；Android uses a VM, iOS executes natively。

7.8 Summary in OS Design [2.53]（原句，MC 用）

  • Monolithic: no structure; all functionality in a single static binary in a single address space; difficult to modify; primary benefit is efficiency.
  • Layered: discrete layers (bottom = hardware interface, top = user interface); not ideal for OS due to performance problems and difficulty in defining layers.
  • Microkernel: minimal kernel; most services run as user-level applications; communication via message passing.
  • LKM: services through modules loaded and removed during runtime.
  • Many contemporary OSes are hybrid: monolithic kernel + modules.

8. History of OS [2.60]

  • 硬件貴、人平：Hand programmed (1945–55) → Batch processing (1955–65) → Multiprogramming (1965–80)
  • 硬件平、人貴：Time sharing → Personal computer → Distributed OS → Real-time OS

9. 補充：Protection & Security（Chapter 2 summary）

  • Protection — controlling access of users/processes to resources。Access right = permission to perform operations on an object；Domain = a set of access rights；process 喺 domain 入面執行。
  • Security — defend system against internal/external attacks（DoS, worms, viruses）。

10. 常見陷阱 & MC 速查

敘述 真/假
printf() is a system call ✘（library function，calls write()）
API always faster than system calls ✘
API can bypass kernel protection ✘
Parameters can be passed by registers, block/table, stack ✔（all of the above）
Block & stack methods limit number of parameters ✘（do not limit）
Shell may be implemented in kernel or as system program ✔
Linker loads program into memory ✘（loader 做）
DLLs are loaded once and shared ✔
Microkernel has better performance than monolithic ✘
Microkernel: services communicate by message passing ✔
Microkernel easier to port and extend ✔
Layered: any layer may call any other layer ✘（LKM 先可以 any module calls any module）
Layered: main advantage = simplicity of construction & debugging ✔
LKM requires recompiling entire kernel ✘
LKM more efficient than microkernel (no message passing) ✔
Monolithic: fast but hard to maintain ✔
Original UNIX = monolithic ✔
Linux = monolithic + modular (hybrid) ✔
Windows NT = layered microkernel ✔
Policy = how; mechanism = what ✘（policy = what，mechanism = how）
Darwin = Mach + BSD ✔
Android apps run on ART VM; iOS runs natively ✔

11. Past-paper 題目（Ch2）

  1. S26 MC5 false about OS services → "Errors only occur in memory and I/O devices"
  2. S26 MC6 advantage of API → more convenient programming interface
  3. S26 MC7 false about microkernel → better performance than monolithic（C 亦接受）
  4. S26 MC8 printf/write → printf() invokes write() system call
  5. S26 MC9 layered → interfaces between adjacent layers are well defined
  6. F19 Q1.1 / F20 Q2.2 linker vs loader（§5）
  7. F19 Q1.3 點解分開 API 同 system call（§3.3）
  8. F19 Q1.4 LKM vs layered（§7.6）
  9. F20 MC4 APIs；MC5 all of the above；MC6 LKMs；Q2.1 microkernel（§7.5）
  10. Sp21 MC：two modes → user mode and kernel mode；interface to OS services → system calls；original UNIX → monolithic
  11. HW1 MC5 microkernel true statement；MC6 read() path；MC7 privileged instruction in user mode → trap to OS；Q2.4 read() user→kernel→user

12. 自我測驗

  1. 寫出 system call 六大類別。
  2. 點解 block/stack parameter passing 優於 registers？（no limit on number/length）
  3. LKM 同 microkernel 有咩相似同唔同？（both: core kernel small; LKM no message passing → efficient）
  4. 舉例說明 policy vs mechanism。（timer = mechanism; how long = policy）
  5. FreeBSD shell 點樣 run 一個 program？（fork → exec → wait）

3Ch3 Processes進程・fork・IPC・interrupt

📌 考試範圍提示：Ch3 係分數最多嘅章節之一。必考：

  1. fork() 計數 / process tree / possible outputs（每年 2–3 題，8–13 分一題）
  2. fork + exec + wait 成功/失敗情況、pipe / dup 填空（S26 Q3.3）
  3. MC：process state、PCB、context switch、schedulers、zombie/orphan、pipes（ordinary vs named）、interrupt、DMA、dual mode ⚠️ 今個 term interrupt / DMA / dual-mode 喺 Ch3 slides（3.21–3.31）。

0. Computer-System Operation：I/O、Interrupt、DMA、Dual Mode [3.21–3.31]

0.1 I/O 基本運作 [3.21]

  • I/O devices and the CPU execute concurrently and asynchronously.
  • Each device controller is in charge of a particular device type and has a local buffer.
  • Device controller moves data between the peripheral devices and its local buffer storage.（I/O = device ↔ controller local buffer）
  • CPU moves data from/to main memory to/from local buffers（typically for slow devices: keyboard, mouse）。
  • Device controller informs the CPU that it has finished an operation by causing an interrupt.
    • Input device：data available in local buffer；Output device：I/O operation completed。

0.2 Interrupts ⭐⭐ [3.22–3.24]

  • An I/O device may trigger an interrupt by sending a signal to the CPU（經 interrupt-request line）。CPU handles the interrupt and then returns to the interrupted instruction。
  • Interrupts are widely used to handle asynchronous events — device controllers and hardware faults.
  • Interrupt transfers control to an interrupt service routine (ISR) or interrupt handler — part of kernel code.
  • Interrupt vector（summary）：a table/array holding the addresses of the interrupt handlers；每個 interrupt 有 unique interrupt number 作為 index。
  • 運作（summary）：CPU 每執行完一條 instruction 就 sense interrupt-request line → 有 signal → 讀 interrupt number → 用佢做 index 入 interrupt vector → jump 去 handler。
  • 兩條 interrupt request lines（summary）：
    • Nonmaskable interrupt — reserved for events like unrecoverable memory errors, power failure
    • Maskable interrupt — can be turned off by the CPU before executing critical instruction sequences；device controllers 用（可以 delay）
  • Interrupt priority levels ⭐ — a high-priority interrupt can preempt the execution of a low-priority interrupt；CPU 可以 defer low-priority interrupts 而唔使 mask 全部。
  • A trap or exception is a software-generated interrupt caused either by an error (e.g., arithmetic error / divide by zero) or a user request (a system call). ⭐
  • All modern operating systems are interrupt-driven；每秒幾百個 interrupts。

⚠️ MC 陷阱：

  • S26 MC1 ✗「All interrupts are serviced in exactly the same way, with no difference in priority」（有 priority levels）
  • HW1 MC2 ✗「Interrupts can only be generated by I/O devices」（software trap/exception 都係）；✔ hardware interrupts asynchronous；✔ trap caused by error or system call；✔ different priority levels
  • F20 MC3 ✗「Interrupt allows software to access hardware including the CPU」（嗰個係 system call 嘅作用）
  • Sp21 MC2：直接 trigger interrupt 嘅 event → I/O job completion

0.3 DMA (Direct Memory Access) ⭐⭐ [3.25–3.26]

  • Programmed I/O — CPU runs special I/O instructions to move one byte at a time between memory and slow devices（keyboard, mouse）。
  • DMA — for fast devices and large amounts of data：DMA controller bypasses the CPU to transfer data between I/O device and memory directly；CPU/OS 只係 initialize DMA controller。→ relieves the CPU from slow data movement。
  • 步驟：OS writes DMA command block into memory（source and destination addresses, read or write mode, number of bytes）→ writes location of command block to DMA controller → bus mastering（DMA controller grabs bus from CPU）→ when done, sends an interrupt to the CPU to signal completion。
  • Six-step DMA transfer [3.26]：
    1. Device driver is told to transfer disk data to buffer at address X
    2. Device driver tells disk controller to transfer C bytes from disk to buffer at address X
    3. Disk controller initiates DMA transfer
    4. Disk controller sends each byte to DMA controller
    5. DMA controller transfers bytes to buffer X, increasing memory address and decreasing C until C = 0
    6. When C = 0, DMA interrupts CPU to signal transfer completion

⚠️ MC 陷阱：

  • HW1 MC1 ✗「DMA controller sends an interrupt after every byte」→ 只係 block 完成後 interrupt 一次
  • S26 MC2 ✔「DMA allows data transfer between device and memory with less CPU involvement」
  • ✗「DMA eliminates interrupts completely」；✗「CPU and DMA can't execute concurrently」；✗「DMA for slow devices, CPU for fast devices」（相反）

0.4 Dual-mode Operation ⭐⭐ [3.27–3.31]

  • Dual-mode operation allows the OS to protect itself and other system components.
  • User mode vs kernel mode（亦叫 supervisor / system / privileged mode）— distinguish execution of OS code and user code。
  • Provides the basic means of protecting the OS from errant users, and errant users from one another.
  • Mode bit provided by hardware：user mode = 1，kernel mode = 0. ⭐
  • Privileged instructions — can only be executed in kernel mode，例：I/O control, timer management, interrupt management（summary：set timer value, clear memory, disable interrupts, modify device-status table, access I/O devices）。
  • If a privileged instruction is attempted in user mode, the hardware treats it as illegal and traps it to the OS.（HW1 MC7 答案 A）
  • Beyond two modes：Intel — four protection rings（ring 0 = kernel, ring 3 = user；rings 1, 2 rarely used）；ARMv8 — seven modes；CPUs supporting virtualization have a separate VMM mode（more privileges than user, fewer than kernel）。VMM 做 machine switch（save entire machine state 包括 privileged hardware state）。
  • Initial control in kernel mode → set to user mode when giving control to user app → back to kernel via interrupt, trap, or system call.
  • Timer — ensures a user program always returns control to the OS（防止 infinite loop 霸住 CPU）；設定 timer 係 privileged。
  • System call：user mode → kernel mode to fulfil request；return from system call resets it to user mode。

0.5 Three Types of Mode Transfer（user → kernel）⭐ [3.31]

Type 性質 例子
System call user process requests a service from OS；"like a function call but outside the user process" exit(), write(), read()
Interrupt external, asynchronous events；independent of user process timer, I/O completion
Trap / Exception internal, synchronous events in the process protection violation (segmentation fault), divide by zero

1. Process Concept（進程概念）[3.4–3.8]

1.1 Four Fundamental OS Concepts [3.4]

  1. Process — an instance of an executing program；consists of an address space and one or more threads of control。
  2. Thread — a single unique execution context；fully describes program state — program counter, registers, execution flags, stack。
  3. Address space — program 喺自己嘅 address space 執行，distinct from other programs'（logical/virtual address，Ch9–10）。
  4. Dual-mode operation — basic protection（見 §0.4）。

1.2 Process 嘅組成部分 [3.5] ⭐

  • Text section — the program code
  • Current activity — program counter, processor registers
  • Stack — temporary data when invoking functions: function parameters, return addresses, local variables ⭐（Sp21 MC 答案：stack）
  • Data section — global variables
  • Heap — memory dynamically allocated during run time（malloc() / free()）
  • Program = passive entity（executable file on disk）；Process = active entity（有 PC 指住下一條 instruction + associated resources）。
  • A program becomes a process when an executable file is loaded into memory（double-click icon / shell command）。
  • One program can be executed by multiple processes.
  • A process can be an execution environment for other programs（例：JVM 執行 Java program）。

1.3 Loading a program into memory [3.7]

  1. Load code and static data（initialized variables）into the address space
  2. Allocate the runtime stack；fill in main() parameters argc, argv
  3. Allocate heap（malloc() / free()）
  4. Other initialization (I/O) — UNIX：每個 process 預設有 three open file descriptors: stdin (0), stdout (1), stderr (2) ⭐

1.4 Memory Layout of a C Program [3.8]

 high address ┌──────────────────────┐
              │ argc, argv           │  (separate section for main() params)
              ├──────────────────────┤
              │ stack   ↓            │  local vars, params, return addr
              │                      │
              │ heap    ↑            │  malloc()
              ├──────────────────────┤
              │ uninitialized data   │  (bss)  ┐ global data
              │ initialized data     │         ┘
              ├──────────────────────┤
 low address  │ text (code)          │
              └──────────────────────┘
  • Global data section is divided into (1) initialized data and (2) uninitialized data.

2. Process State（進程狀態）⭐⭐ [3.9]

State 意思
New process is being created — allocating resources
Ready waiting to be assigned to a CPU core
Running instructions are being executed on a CPU
Waiting waiting for some event(s) to occur（I/O completion, signal）
Terminated finished execution — deallocating resources 
   new --admitted--> ready <--interrupt-- running --exit--> terminated
                       |  ----scheduler dispatch---->   |
                       ^                                v
                       +--I/O or event completion-- waiting <--I/O or event wait--+

轉換（F19 Q2.1 答案）：

  • New → Ready：admitted
  • Ready → Running：scheduler dispatch（CPU scheduling）
  • Running → Ready：interrupt（e.g. timer / time slice expired / preempted）
  • Running → Waiting：I/O or event wait
  • Waiting → Ready：I/O or event completion
  • Running → Terminated：exit

📝 HW1 Q2.3：admitted → dispatched → disk I/O → I/O completes → timer interrupt → dispatched → exits New → Ready → Running → Waiting → Ready → Running → Ready → Running → Terminated 離開 Running 時 OS 喺 PCB 記錄：process state, program counter, CPU registers（context），以及更新 scheduling info、accounting info（CPU time used）、I/O status（e.g. pending I/O request）。

⚠️ 注意：Waiting → Running 係冇嘅（要先返 Ready）；Ready → Waiting 亦冇。 S26 MC14：令 process 離開 running state 嘅 event → It issues an I/O request（call function / 加 local variable / read cache 都唔會）。 Sp21 MC6：might force a process to leave the CPU → all of the above（fork system call、I/O request、interrupt）。

3. Process Control Block (PCB) ⭐ [3.10, 3.13]

PCB（又叫 task control block）= information associated with each process（kernel data structure）：

Field 內容
Process state running, waiting, etc.
Program counter location of the next instruction to execute
CPU registers accumulators, index registers, stack pointers, general-purpose registers, condition codes
CPU-scheduling information priorities, pointers to scheduling queues, scheduling parameters
Memory-management information memory allocated（page tables / segment tables）
Accounting information CPU used, clock time elapsed, time limits
I/O status information I/O devices allocated, list of open files
  • 每個 process 有 unique PID (process identifier)（UNIX/Linux 係 positive integer；PID 1 = init / systemd，root process）。
  • Process table / process list — kernel data structure keeping track of all processes（indexed by PID, pointing to PCBs）。
  • Linux [3.13]：task list = circular doubly linked list of struct task_struct（process descriptor = PCB）：pid_t pid; long state; unsigned int time_slice; struct task_struct *parent; struct list_head children; struct files_struct *files; struct mm_struct *mm;

4. Threads（簡介，詳見 Ch4）[3.11–3.12]

  • A thread is represented by a program counter, registers, execution flags, and a stack — part of a process.
  • 一個 thread 喺 CPU 執行 = 佢嘅 state 喺 CPU hardware registers。
  • Multiple threads of a process run within the same address space (share code, data, files); each thread has its own thread state.（multicore 上可以 parallel）
  • 例：web browser 一個 thread display、一個 thread 由 network 拎 data。
  • 傳統 process（single thread）= heavy-weight process。

5. Process Scheduling（進程排程）⭐ [3.14–3.18]

5.1 基本

  • Objective of multiprogramming：have some process running at all times to maximize CPU utilization.
  • Process scheduler — selects an available process (in memory) for execution on a CPU core。
  • Single CPU core → never more than one process running at a time.
  • Degree of multiprogramming = number of processes currently residing in memory. ⭐（Sp21 MC）
  • I/O-bound process — more time doing I/O；many short CPU bursts。
  • CPU-bound process — infrequent I/O；few very long CPU bursts。

5.2 Scheduling Queues [3.15–3.16]

  • Ready queue — processes in main memory, ready and waiting to execute
  • Wait queues — processes waiting for an event（e.g. I/O completion）
  • Processes migrate among the various queues during their lifetime.
  • Queueing diagram [3.16]：ready queue → CPU → 出口有：I/O request（→ I/O wait queue → I/O → ready）、time slice expired（→ ready）、create child process（→ child termination wait queue）、wait for an interrupt（→ interrupt wait queue → interrupt occurs → ready）。
  • Summary：the state of a process can be determined by which queue it resides on（running 嗰個例外）。

5.3 Schedulers ⭐⭐ [3.17–3.18]

Scheduler 又叫 做咩 頻率
Long-term scheduler job scheduler selects which processes should be brought into memory（from job queue, after resource allocation）；dictates the degree of multiprogramming；aims for a good process mix of I/O-bound and CPU-bound；mainframes/minicomputers 用，PC 冇 very infrequent（seconds, minutes）→ may be slow
Short-term scheduler CPU scheduler selects a process from the ready queue to execute next and allocates a CPU core very frequent（milliseconds）→ must be fast
Medium-term scheduler — reduces degree of multiprogramming — removes a process from memory, stores it on disk, brings it back later = swapping（swap space） 需要時
  • F20 MC10：「___ swaps a process out of memory and temporarily stores it on disk」→ medium-term scheduler。
  • Sp21 MC：「selects from the ready queue to execute on the CPU」→ short-term scheduler。

6. Context Switch（上下文切換）⭐⭐ [3.19–3.20]

  • When the CPU switches to another process, the OS must save the state of the old process and load the saved state of the new process via a context switch.
  • Context of a process is represented in the PCB（or thread）：registers, program counter, stack pointer。
  • Context-switch time is pure overhead — the system does no useful work while switching.
  • The more complex the OS and the PCB, the longer the context switch.
  • 速度取決於 memory speed, number of registers to copy, special instructions (load/store all registers) → highly dependent on hardware。
  • Some processors provide multiple sets of registers（SUN UltraSPARC）→ context switch = changing a pointer to another register set.

⚠️ S26 MC11 ✔「During context switch, OS saves the state of one process/thread and restores another」。 ✗「Context switch means switching from user mode to kernel mode」（嗰個係 mode switch，唔係 context switch） ✗「requires same hardware on all architectures」；✗「shorter if OS is more complex」

7. Process Creation（建立進程）⭐⭐⭐ [3.32–3.46]

7.1 概念

  • Parent process creates child processes → tree of processes. 每個 process 有 unique pid。
  • Linux tree [3.33]：systemd (pid 1) 有三個 children：logind (8415)、python (2808)、sshd (3028)；logind → bash (8416) → ps (9298)、vim (9204)；sshd (3028) → sshd (3610) → tcsh (4005)。
  • Child 需要 resources（CPU time, memory, files, I/O devices）；parent 可傳 initialization data。
  • Resource sharing 三種選擇 [3.34]：
    1. Parent and children share (almost) all resources — fork()
    2. Children share a subset of parent's resources — clone()（Ch4）
    3. Parent and child share no resources
  • Execution 兩種選擇：
    1. Parent continues to execute concurrently with its children（例：a.out &）
    2. Parent waits until some or all of its children have terminated（例：shell 等 a.out 完成）
  • Address space 兩種選擇 [3.35]：
    1. Child is a duplicate of the parent (same program and data) — UNIX/Linux fork()
    2. Child has a new program loaded into it — exec()

7.2 fork() ⭐⭐⭐ [3.35–3.39]

  • fork() creates a new process that duplicates the entire address space of the parent. Both processes continue execution at the instruction after fork().
  • Return value（each process receives exactly one return value）[3.36–3.37]：
Return value 喺邊個 process 意思
> 0 parent 值 = PID of the newly created child
= 0 child 新建嘅 child
< 0（−1） parent (original) fork failed，no child created，errno set
  • All state of the parent is duplicated in the child：memory (program, data), file descriptors…；每個 process 有自己一份 program 同 data（time consuming）。
  • Steps to implement fork() [3.38]：
    1. Create and initialize the PCB in the kernel
    2. Create a new address space（allocate memory）
    3. Initialize the address space with a copy of the parent's entire address space（time consuming）
    4. Inherit the execution context of the parent（e.g. open files）
    5. Inform the CPU scheduler that the child is ready to run
  • Parent vs Child after fork() [3.39]：
    • Duplicated：address space, global & local variables, current working directory, root directory, process resources, resource limits, program counter (PC)…
    • Different：PID, return value from fork(), running time, running state
  • ⚠️ Child 改 variable 唔會影響 parent（各自一份 copy）— HW1 MC9 ✗「If the child changes a global variable, the parent's copy changes automatically」。
  • ⚠️ Execution order of parent and child depends on the scheduler unless synchronization（wait()）is used。
  • F20 MC7 ✗「The parent process waits until the completion of the child process」（fork 本身唔會等，要 wait()）。
  • Sp21 MC ✗「The child process can call wait() in order to finish earlier than the parent」。

7.3 Copy-on-Write (COW) & clone() [3.46]

  • Process creation in UNIX is unique — separated into two system calls: fork() and exec().（其他 OS：create new address space + read executable + start 一次過）
  • Linux implements fork() with copy-on-write (COW) pages — parent and child share a single copy；copying is delayed until a page is written。若 fork() 後即刻 exec()，pages never need to be copied → greatly speeds up process creation。
  • Linux also implements fork() via clone()（more general；flags 指定 share 邊啲 resources）。

7.4 exec / wait / exit / signal [3.41–3.43]

  • exec() / execlp() — changes the program being run by the current process；creates a new process image from a regular executable file（replaces the address space；成功就唔會 return，fail 先 return −1 並繼續執行下一行）。
  • wait() — wait for a (child) process to finish；pid = wait(&status); 返回 terminated child 嘅 PID，status 存 exit status。
  • exit() — terminate the current process and free all its resources。
  • signal() — send a notification to another process（slide 3.41 原文）。
    • ⚠️ slides 前後唔一致：slide 4.31 話真正 deliver signal 畀 process 嘅 UNIX function 係 kill(pid, signal)；而實際 UNIX 嘅 signal(SIGINT, handler) 係裝 handler（PA1 就係咁用）。MC 照題目用字對返邊張 slide 作答。
  • 經典 code [3.42]：
pid = fork();
if (pid < 0) { fprintf(stderr, "Fork Failed"); exit(-1); }   /* error */
else if (pid == 0) { execlp("/bin/ls", "ls", NULL); }        /* child */
else { wait(NULL); printf("Child Complete"); exit(0); }      /* parent */
  • Implementing a shell [3.43]：loop readAndParseCmdLine → fork() → child exec(prog, args)（NOT REACHED after）→ parent wait(child_pid)。
  • F19 Q2.4：shell 要 start new process 必須用嘅兩個 system calls → fork()（clones the current process）+ exec()（overlays a new program onto the calling process）。

8. Process Termination（終止）⭐⭐ [3.47–3.50]

  • Process 執行完最後一句 → exit() 要求 OS 刪除佢；可以經 wait() 回傳 status value 畀 parent。
  • Resources (memory, open files, I/O buffers) are deallocated and reclaimed by the OS.
  • Parent can terminate children：abort()（UNIX/Linux）、TerminateProcess()（Windows）；parent 需要知 child PID（由 fork() 返回）。理由：child exceeded allocated resources；task no longer required；parent is exiting（有啲 OS 唔准 child 冇 parent）。
  • Cascading termination — if a process terminates, all its children must also be terminated（initiated by OS）。
  • Normal termination：exit() 直接或間接被 call（C runtime library 預設 call exit()）。

8.1 Zombie ⭐⭐

  • A process that has terminated, but whose parent has not yet called wait(), is a zombie process.
  • 佢嘅 entry in the process table, PID, and PCB still exist（因為 process table entry 存住 exit status）；其他 resources 已釋放；not runnable（冇 address space）。
  • All processes transition into the zombie state before termination, but generally only briefly.
  • Once the parent calls wait(), the zombie's PID, process-table entry and PCB are released.
  • S26 MC13 ✔「A zombie process has terminated but its parent has not yet collected its status」；✗ still executing user code；✗ created when exec() fails；✗ owns no PID。

8.2 Orphan ⭐

  • If a parent terminates without invoking wait(), its child becomes an orphan（如果冇 cascading，可能仍 runnable，最終變 zombie）。
  • init / systemd is assigned as the new parent and periodically calls wait() → 收集 exit status，釋放 PID 同 process-table entry。

9. fork() 解題大法 ⭐⭐⭐（考試必用）

9.1 基本規則

  1. 每 call 一次 fork()，「每個執行到嗰句嘅 process」都會變兩個：parent（return child PID > 0 → true）、child（return 0 → false）。
  2. Child 由 fork() 下一句開始行，有 parent 所有 variables 嘅 copy（包括 loop counter i）。
  3. n 句連續 fork()（無條件）→ 2ⁿ processes。
  4. Short-circuit evaluation ⭐：
    • A && B：A false (0 → child) 就唔會執行 B；A true (parent) 先執行 B。
    • A || B：A true (parent) 就唔會執行 B；A false (child) 先執行 B。
  5. printf 次數 = 執行到該句 printf 嘅 process 數目（每個 printf 位置分開計）。
  6. return / exit() / break 會令 process 停止繼續 loop 或者唔再執行後面。
  7. exec() 成功 → 之後嘅 code 唔會執行；失敗 → return −1 繼續。

9.2 常見 pattern 公式（全部已用真 C 程式驗證 ✔）

Code（每個 iteration，所有 process 都繼續 loop） 每 iteration 每個 process 變成 n 次後 processes
fork(); 2 2ⁿ
fork(); fork(); [3.44] 4 4ⁿ（slide：10 次 → 4¹⁰）
if (fork()) fork(); [3.45] 3（parent 再 fork 一次） 3ⁿ（slide：3¹⁰）
if (fork() == 0) fork();（Sp21 MC8） 3（child 再 fork） 3ⁿ（n=3 → 27）
if (fork() && fork()) .../fork() || fork() 3 3ⁿ

9.3 有 break / return 嘅 loop（追蹤「仲 active」嘅 process）

S26 Q3.1：

for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    if (fork()) fork();   // parent: fork 多一次
    else break;            // 第一個 child: break（停 loop）
}
  • 每個 active process 每 iteration：第一個 fork 產生 child（→ else → break，停）；parent 再 fork 一次，新 child（第二個）繼續 loop（佢喺 if branch 入面，return 0 但已經喺 if 入面）。
  • 所以 1 個 active → 3 個 processes，其中 2 個繼續 active、1 個 stopped。
  • A_k = 2^k（iteration k 開始前 active 數）；每 iteration 新增 2^k 個 break。
  • Total processes = 2ⁿ (active) + (2ⁿ − 1) (stopped) = 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 1；break 次數 = 2ⁿ − 1。
  • n = 2：7 processes，3 breaks ✔；n = 3：15 processes，7 breaks ✔。
  • Process tree（n=2）：P0 → P1 (break), P2 → {P5 (break), P6}, P3 (break), P4… （P0 第 0 輪產生 P1(break)、P2；第 1 輪產生 P3(break)、P4；P2 第 1 輪產生 P5(break)、P6）
P0
├── P1   (break at i=0)
├── P2   (continues)
│   ├── P5 (break at i=1)
│   └── P6
├── P3   (break at i=1)
└── P4

S26 MC10：

for (i = 0; i < 3; i++) {
    if (fork() == 0) { fork(); printf("X "); return 1; }
}
printf("Y ");
  • 只有 original parent 繼續 loop；每 iteration 產生 1 個 child，child 再 fork → 2 個 process 各印 "X " 後 return。
  • 3 iterations → 3 × 2 = 6 個 process 印 X；original 印 1 個 Y。Total = 7 processes, X = 6, Y = 1 → 答案 C ✔

9.4 Short-circuit 題

Sp21 MC7：if (fork() || fork()) { fork(); }

  • P0：fork1 → P0 得 >0（true）→ 跳過第二個 fork → 入 if → fork → 2 個
  • C1（fork1 得 0）→ 執行 fork2：C1 得 >0（true）→ 入 if → fork → 2 個；C2 得 0 → false → 唔入 if → 1 個
  • Total = 2 + 2 + 1 = 5 ✔

F19 Q2.7：if (fork() == fork() || fork() != fork()) fork(); printf("Fork!\n");

  • fork() == fork()：4 個 processes，返回值組合 (x,y)：P0 (c1,c2) 唔等；C2 (c1,0) 唔等；C1 (0,c3) 唔等；C3 (0,0) 相等 → true（只有收到兩次 0 嗰個 grandchild）。
  • C3：|| 短路 → 執行 body fork() → 2 個 process 印。
  • 其餘 3 個 process 執行 fork() != fork() → 每個變 4 個 = 12；每組 4 個入面 3 個 true（(c,c')、(0,c)、(c,0)），1 個 false (0,0)。
  • 9 個 true → fork → 18；3 個 false → 3。
  • Total = 2 + 18 + 3 = 23 lines ✔

9.5 Nested / combined

Sp21 Q1.1：

pid1 = fork(); pid2 = fork();
if (pid1 > 0) if (pid2 == 0) fork();
  • 兩個 fork 後 4 個 processes；只有「pid1 > 0（P0 或 P0 嘅第二個 child）且 pid2 == 0」嘅 P0 第二個 fork 生出嘅 child 符合 → 再 fork 1 次 → 5 processes ✔

Sp21 Q1.2：

if (fork() == 0) { for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) if (fork() == 0) fork(); }
fork();
  • 第一個 fork：child 入 loop；loop 5 次，每次每個 process 變 3 個 → 3⁵ = 243（包括 child 自己）。
  • 加 original parent = 243 + 1 = 244；最後 fork 全部 ×2 → (3⁵ + 1) × 2 = 488 ✔

F20 Q3.2：

if (!fork()) { for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) if (fork()) fork(); }
fork(); printf("a");
  • 同上：child branch 變 3ⁿ，加 parent → 3ⁿ + 1，最後 fork ×2 → "a" 數 = 2(3ⁿ + 1) ✔（n=1 → 8；n=2 → 20）

F19 Q2.6（loop 入面 parent 同 child 都 printf）：

for (i = 0; i < n; ++i) { if (fork()) printf("Fork!\n"); else printf("Fork!\n"); }
  • 第 i 次 iteration（由 1 數）有 2^i 個 process 各印一行 → 2 + 4 + … + 2ⁿ = 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 2 ✔

9.6 Possible output orderings（輸出次序題）⭐

方法：

  1. 畫 process tree，寫低每個 process 印咩、依咩次序（同一 process 內次序固定）。
  2. 加 wait() 約束：parent wait() 之後嘅 print 一定喺 child 全部 print 之後。
  3. Fork 之前印嘅一定最先（假設 flushed）。
  4. 列出所有符合約束嘅 interleavings（topological orders）。

F20 Q3.1：

if (fork() == 0) {            // child C
    if (fork() > 0) { printf("1"); wait(NULL); printf("2"); }  // C
    else printf("3");         // grandchild G
} else printf("4");           // parent P
  • 約束：C 印 "1" 然後等 G（印 "3"）→ 再印 "2"。即 "2" 喺 "1" 同 "3" 之後；"4" 隨意。
  • "2" 最後：1、3、4 任意排 = 3! = 6 種：1342, 1432, 3142, 4132, 3412, 4312
  • "4" 喺 "2" 之後：1324, 3124
  • Total = 8（unbuffered）。若 buffered（"1" 同 "2" 同一 buffer 一齊 flush）→ 只剩 4312, 3412, 3124（3 種）。

Sp21 Q1.3：printf("A\n"); if (fork()==0) printf("B\n"); else { printf("C\n"); ___A___ } printf("D\n");

  • (a) A 空白：ABDCD, ABCDD, ACBDD, ACDBD（4 種；A 一定最先；child 印 B,D；parent 印 C,D）
  • (b) A = wait(NULL)：parent 嘅 D 一定最後 → ABDCD, ABCDD, ACBDD
  • (c) A = printf("E\n")：parent 印 C,E,D；child 印 B,D → 7 種：ABDCED, ABCDED, ABCEDD, ACBDED, ACBEDD, ACEBDD, ACEDBD

HW1 Q3.1（x 值）：

int x = 1; pid_t c1 = fork();
if (c1 == 0) { x += 2; printf("C1: %d\n", x); return 0; }   // 3
x *= 2; pid_t c2 = fork();
