if (c2 == 0) { x += 3; printf("C2: %d\n", x); return 0; }   // 2+3 = 5
wait(NULL); wait(NULL); printf("P: %d\n", x);                // 2
  • C1: 3, C2: 5, P: 2 — 因為 each process has its own copy of x（fork 時複製）。
  • Possible outputs：C1:3 → C2:5 → P:2 或 C2:5 → C1:3 → P:2（P 一定最後，因為 wait 兩次）。
  • 若將第一個 wait(NULL) 移到 x *= 2 前：parent 先等 C1 完，先 fork C2 → 保證 C1: 3, C2: 5, P: 2；數值不變（wait 唔改 x）。

HW1 Q3.2（exec）：

printf("A\n"); pid_t p = fork();
if (p == 0) { pid_t q = fork();
    if (q == 0) { execlp("./worker","worker",(char*)NULL); printf("X\n"); return 1; }
    wait(NULL); printf("C\n"); return 0; }
printf("P\n"); wait(NULL); printf("D\n");
  • 3 processes：parent → child → grandchild（grandchild exec worker，印 W）。
  • X 唔會印：execlp 成功 → address space 被取代，never returns。
  • 約束：A 最先；W < C < D；P < D。→ A P W C D、A W P C D、A W C P D（3 種）。

9.7 fork + exec 成功/失敗情況（S26 Q3.2）⭐

int ret = fork();
if (ret == 0) { printf("Hello1\n"); exec("some_executable"); printf("Hello2\n"); }
else if (ret > 0) { wait(NULL); printf("Hello3\n"); }
else { printf("Hello4\n"); }
Case Output
fork 成功、exec 成功 Hello1, Hello3（Hello2 唔印：image replaced）
fork 成功、exec 失敗 Hello1, Hello2, Hello3（exec 返回 −1，繼續）
fork 失敗 Hello4（only parent, ret < 0）
  • 因為有 wait(NULL)，Hello3 一定喺 child 嘅輸出之後。

9.8 Buffering 陷阱

  • printf 冇 \n 或者 output 去 file/pipe → stdout buffer 未 flush 就 fork → buffer 被複製 → 重複印。
  • 考試通常講明「stdout is flushed after each printf」或「printf not buffered」。如果冇講，可以寫一句 assumption。

10. Interprocess Communication (IPC) ⭐⭐ [3.51–3.64]

10.1 Independent vs Cooperating

  • Cooperating processes can affect or be affected by other processes（e.g. share data）。
  • Reasons for cooperation：information sharing（copy & paste）、computation speedup（parallel subtasks）、modularity（+ convenience）。
  • 需要 IPC mechanism 交換 data。兩大模型：
    1. Shared memory — a region of memory is shared；processes read/write the shared region（implicit communication；快，但要自己 synchronize）
    2. Message passing — explicit messages exchanged（useful in distributed environment；Mach OS 主要用；唔需要 share address space）

10.2 Producer–Consumer Problem（Bounded Buffer, Shared Memory）[3.53–3.56]

  • Unbounded buffer：no practical limit；bounded buffer：fixed size。
#define BUFFER_SIZE 10
item buffer[BUFFER_SIZE]; int in = 0, out = 0;
/* Producer */
while (true) {
    while (((in + 1) % BUFFER_SIZE) == out) ;   /* buffer full → do nothing */
    buffer[in] = next_produced;
    in = (in + 1) % BUFFER_SIZE;
}
/* Consumer */
while (true) {
    while (in == out) ;                          /* buffer empty → do nothing */
    next_consumed = buffer[out];
    out = (out + 1) % BUFFER_SIZE;
}
  • ⭐ This solution only allows at most BUFFER_SIZE − 1 items in the buffer at the same time（因為 in == out 表示 empty，要留一格分辨 full）。

10.3 Message Passing [3.57–3.64]

  • Mechanism for processes to communicate and synchronize without sharing the address space.
  • 兩個 operations：send(message)、receive(message)；message size fixed or variable。
  • P、Q 要通訊：establish a communication link → exchange via send/receive。
  • Implementation 選擇：direct or indirect；synchronous or asynchronous；automatic or explicit buffering。

Direct communication [3.59]：

  • Processes must name each other explicitly：send(P, message)、receive(Q, message)。
  • Link established automatically；associated with exactly one pair；between each pair exactly one link。
  • Disadvantage：limited modularity — 改一個 process 嘅 identifier 要檢查所有其他 processes。

Indirect communication [3.60–3.61]：

  • Messages sent to and received from mailboxes (ports)；每個 mailbox 有 unique id；share a mailbox 先可以通訊。
  • A link may be associated with more than two processes；each pair may have several links（each = one mailbox）。
  • Operations：create mailbox、send/receive through mailbox、destroy mailbox；send(A, msg)、receive(A, msg)。
  • Mailbox sharing problem（P1 send，P2 P3 都 receive，邊個收？）解決：
    1. Allow a link to be associated with at most two processes
    2. Allow only one process at a time to execute receive()
    3. Allow the system to select the receiver arbitrarily; sender is notified who the receiver was

Synchronization (blocking vs non-blocking) [3.62–3.63] ⭐：

Blocking (synchronous) Non-blocking (asynchronous)
Send sender blocked until the message is received (by receiver or mailbox) sender sends and resumes
Receive receiver blocks until a message is available receiver gets a valid message or null
  • Both send and receive blocking → rendezvous；producer–consumer 變得 trivial（send(next_produced) / receive(next_consumed)）。

Buffering（message queue） [3.64] ⭐：

  1. Zero capacity — no messages queued；sender must wait for receiver (rendezvous)
  2. Bounded capacity — finite length n；sender must wait if the link is full
  3. Unbounded capacity — infinite；sender never waits

11. Pipes ⭐⭐⭐ [3.65–3.69]

11.1 設計問題（4 個）

  1. Unidirectional or bidirectional?
  2. If bidirectional：half-duplex（一次一個方向）or full-duplex（同時雙向）？
  3. Must there be a relationship (parent–child)?
  4. Can pipes be used over a network, or only on the same machine?

11.2 Ordinary Pipes vs Named Pipes（必背表）

Ordinary pipe Named pipe
方向 Unidirectional（one-way） Bidirectional（UNIX FIFO：half-duplex；Windows：full-duplex）
關係 Requires parent–child relationship No parent–child relationship required
存取 Cannot be accessed from outside the process that creates it Several processes can use it（multiple writers）
生命週期 Ceases to exist after processes finish communicating and terminate Continues to exist after processes finish, until explicitly deleted
機器 Same machine UNIX：same machine；Windows：same or different machines
UNIX API pipe(int fd[2])：fd[0] = read end，fd[1] = write end mkfifo()；appear as typical files in the file system（FIFOs）
Windows anonymous pipes（ReadFile()/WriteFile()） CreateNamedPipe()
  • UNIX treats a pipe as a special type of file → standard read() and write()；child inherits the pipe from the parent.
  • Parent calls pipe() first, then fork() → child inherits both ends。
  • If the parent writes and the child reads, the parent must close the read end, and the child must close the write end. ⭐
  • ls | grep x：shell 用 pipe 將 ls 嘅 output 接到 grep 嘅 input（tools 唔使特別設計都可以組合）。

⚠️ MC 陷阱：S26 MC12 ✗「ordinary pipes can be accessed by arbitrary processes」；Sp21 MC5 ✗「named pipes cease to exist after the communicating processes have finished」；HW1 MC10 同樣 ✗。F20 MC9：「multiple processes, no parent-child relationship, independent from network」→ named pipes（socket/RPC 需要 network）。

11.3 Pipe example（Parent → Child）[3.68]

int pfds[2]; char buf[30];
pipe(pfds);                       /* 1. create pipe BEFORE fork */
pid_t pid = fork();               /* 2. child inherits pfds[0], pfds[1] */
if (pid != 0) {                   /* parent = writer */
    close(pfds[0]);               /* close unused READ end */
    write(pfds[1], "test", 5);
    wait(NULL);
} else {                          /* child = reader */
    close(pfds[1]);               /* close unused WRITE end */
    read(pfds[0], buf, 5);
    printf("CHILD: read \"%s\"\n", buf);
}

📝 HW1 Q2.5 Model answer：(1) The parent calls pipe(fd) before fork() so that both processes share the same pipe (fd[0] read end, fd[1] write end). (2) Call fork(); the child inherits both file descriptors. (3) Parent (writer) closes fd[0] and writes data with write(fd[1], ...), then closes fd[1]. (4) Child (reader) closes fd[1] and reads with read(fd[0], ...), then closes fd[0]. Data flows parent → child (one-way). HW1 Q3.3(c)：如果 parent 同 child 各自喺 fork() 之後 call pipe() → 會建立兩條獨立 pipe，佢哋唔共享 file descriptors → cannot communicate。 HW1 Q3.3(b)：Child: 12 然後 Parent: done — 因為 child 嘅 read() blocks until data is written，而 parent wait(NULL) 等 child 結束先印。

11.4 dup / dup2 & I/O redirection（Lab 3 / PA1）⭐⭐

  • File descriptor table：0 = stdin，1 = stdout，2 = stderr。
  • dup(oldfd) — 複製 oldfd 到 lowest-numbered unused fd，返回新 fd。
  • dup2(oldfd, newfd) — 令 newfd 指向 oldfd 嘅 file（如 newfd 已開會先 close）。
  • Input redirection 經典 pattern：close(0); dup(fin); ⟹ fd 0 變成 file → scanf 由 file 讀。等價 dup2(fin, 0);
  • Output redirection：close(1); dup(fout); 或 dup2(fout, 1);
  • Pipe 接 stdout → stdin（ls | wc）：writer child：dup2(fd[1], 1); close(fd[0]); close(fd[1]); execlp("ls",...)；reader child：dup2(fd[0], 0); close(fd[1]); close(fd[0]); execlp("wc",...)；parent 要 close 兩端（否則 reader 永遠收唔到 EOF）。

S26 Q3.3（填空）：child 處理 1.txt、parent 處理 2.txt，數 even numbers，child 經 pipe 傳結果：

int fin = open(file_name[i], O_RDONLY);
close(0);                          // Blank 1
dup(fin);                          // Blank 2  → stdin now = file
scanf("%d", &length);
for (j = 0; j < length; j++) {
    scanf("%d", &number);
    if (number % 2 == 0) cnt++;    // Blank 3
}
if (pid == 0) { close(fd[0]); write(fd[1], &cnt, sizeof(int)); exit(0); }  // 4.1 fd[1], 4.2 &cnt
waitpid(pid, &status, 0); close(fd[1]);
read(fd[0], &child_cnt, sizeof(int));                                    // 5.1 fd[0], 5.2 &child_cnt

12. Client–Server Communication：Sockets & RPC ⭐ [3.70–3.72]

  • A socket is defined as an endpoint for communication. A pair of processes over a network uses a pair of sockets。
  • A socket is identified by an IP address and a port number.
  • Server always running，listens to a well-known port；接受 request → 建立 socket connection。
  • Well-known ports（< 1024）：SSH 22、FTP 21、HTTP 80。
  • Only clients can initiate communication. Client 揀一個 port number > 1024（未用緊）。
  • IP：IPv4 32-bit（128.32.244.172）、IPv6 128-bit。
  • ⭐ The 4-tuple (source IP address, source port number, destination IP address, destination port number) uniquely determines a pair of communication.（F19 Q2.2 答案）
  • RPC (Remote Procedure Call) — procedure-call mechanism between systems with network connections；message-based；常用於 distributed file systems（RPC daemons + clients）。

13. 常見陷阱 & MC 速查

敘述 真/假
Program = active, process = passive ✘（program passive；process active）
Stack holds function params, return addresses, local variables ✔
Heap holds global variables ✘（data section；heap = dynamic allocation）
Ready → Waiting transition exists ✘
Long-term scheduler controls degree of multiprogramming ✔
Short-term scheduler invoked infrequently ✘（very frequently, ms）
Medium-term scheduler does swapping ✔
Context switch = user→kernel mode switch ✘
Context-switch time is useful work ✘（pure overhead）
fork() returns 0 to parent ✘（0 to child；child PID to parent）
fork() fails → returns −1 to parent, no child ✔
After fork, child shares parent's variables ✘（copy；COW 只係 implementation 優化）
Parent automatically waits for child after fork ✘（需要 wait()）
exec() returns on success ✘（只有 failure 返回 −1）
Zombie still runs user code ✘
Zombie keeps PID & process-table entry ✔
Orphans are adopted by init/systemd ✔
Ordinary pipe: unidirectional, parent-child ✔
Named pipe ceases after processes end ✘
Named pipe (UNIX FIFO) full-duplex ✘（half-duplex；Windows named pipe 先 full-duplex）
Blocking send/receive = synchronous ✔
Zero-capacity buffer: sender never waits ✘（unbounded 先 never waits）
Bounded-buffer shared-memory solution holds BUFFER_SIZE items ✘（BUFFER_SIZE − 1）
Mode bit 1 = kernel ✘（1 = user，0 = kernel）
Privileged instruction in user mode → trap to OS ✔
DMA interrupts after every byte ✘
Trap = software-generated interrupt ✔
Interrupts all have same priority ✘

15. 自我測驗

  1. for (i=0;i<4;i++) fork(); 有幾多個 processes？（16）
  2. fork(); fork() && fork(); fork(); 有幾多？（2 × 3 × 2 = 12：第一個 fork → 2；fork() && fork() 每個變 3；最後 ×2）
  3. Zombie 同 orphan 分別？
  4. 點解 bounded buffer 只可以放 BUFFER_SIZE − 1 個 item？
  5. Named pipe 喺 UNIX 叫咩？點建立？（FIFO；mkfifo()）
  6. 寫出三種 mode transfer。（system call, interrupt, trap/exception）

4Ch4 Threads線程與並行

📌 考試範圍提示：Ch4 通常 3–4 題 MC + 有機會 1 題 short question。必考： thread 有乜自己私有 / 乜同其他 thread 共享、benefits（4 個）、concurrency vs parallelism、data vs task parallelism、Amdahl's Law 計算、multithreading models（many-to-one / one-to-one / many-to-many）、signals（sync vs async）、thread cancellation（asynchronous vs deferred）、fork()/exec() semantics、Linux clone()、heavyweight vs lightweight。

1. Motivation & Overview（動機）[4.4–4.6]

1.1 點解要 multithreading？

  • 好多 applications 本身係 multithreaded：
    • Web browser：一個 thread display images/text，另一個 thread 由 network retrieve data。
    • Word processor：display graphics、respond to keystrokes、background spelling & grammar checking。
  • Most OS kernels are multithreaded（Linux boot 時建立多個 kernel threads：managing devices、memory management、interrupt handling）。
  • Take advantage of processing capabilities on multicore systems（data mining、graphics、AI）。
  • ⭐ Process creation is heavy-weight while thread creation is light-weight — threads of the same process share code, data and other resources。
  • 其他例子 [4.5]：embedded systems（elevators, planes）、OS kernels、database servers、network servers（file/web server、airline reservation）、parallel programming。

1.2 Thread 定義 ⭐⭐ [4.6]

  • A thread is a basic unit of CPU utilization — independently scheduled and run（an instance of execution）。
  • 每個 thread 私有（per-thread）：thread ID, program counter (PC), register set, stack
  • 同一 process 嘅 threads 共享（shared）：code (text), data (global variables), heap, open files, signals（及其他 OS resources）
 single-threaded process        multithreaded process
 ┌──────┬──────┬──────┐        ┌──────┬──────┬──────┐
 │ code │ data │ files│        │ code │ data │ files│   ← shared
 ├──────┼──────┼──────┤        ├──────┼──────┼──────┤
 │ regs │  PC  │stack │        │ regs │ regs │ regs │   ← private
 └──────┴──────┴──────┘        │stack │stack │stack │      (per thread)
                               │  PC  │  PC  │  PC  │
                               └──────┴──────┴──────┘

📝 F19 Q2.3：unique to each thread → program counter, registers, stack, thread ID (thread state)；shared → code, data, files (heap)。 ⚠️ F20 MC11：NOT shared by threads in the same process → Stack。

2. Benefits（四大好處）⭐⭐ [4.7]

Benefit 解釋
Responsiveness may allow continued execution if part of a process (one thread) is blocked — 對 interactive / UI applications 特別重要
Resource sharing threads share the resources of a process by default — 比 shared memory / message passing 容易（同一 address space）
Economy thread creation is much cheaper than process creation（less time & memory）；context switching between threads is faster than between processes
Scalability process can take advantage of multicore architectures（threads run in parallel）

📝 HW2 MC1：app 喺一個 thread blocked 時仍然 respond user → Responsiveness。

2.1 Heavyweight vs Lightweight（F20 Q2.3）⭐

📝 Model answer：A process is called a heavyweight process because creating it requires allocating a new address space and duplicating the program, data and other resources (PCB, memory, open files) — time- and resource-consuming. A thread is lightweight because it only contains an execution unit (thread ID, PC, registers, stack, TCB) and shares the code, data and open files of its process; creating a thread needs no duplication of program and data, and context switching between threads of the same process is cheaper since the address space stays the same.

3. Multicore Programming（多核心編程）⭐ [4.8–4.11]

3.1 五大挑戰 [4.9]

  1. Dividing tasks — how to divide into separate, concurrent tasks
  2. Balance — each task performs an "equal" amount of work（value）
  3. Data splitting — data must be divided to run on separate cores
  4. Data dependency — if there is a data dependency, synchronization is required
  5. Testing and debugging — different execution paths make debugging difficult

3.2 Concurrency vs Parallelism ⭐⭐ [4.9–4.10]

  • Parallelism — a system can perform more than one task simultaneously（需要 multicore / multiprocessor）。
  • Concurrency — supports more than one task making progress；on a single core, the scheduler provides concurrency by multiplexing over time。
  • Single-core：only concurrency is possible；multicore：parallelism possible.
  • Concurrency without parallelism 係可以嘅；parallelism 一定 concurrent。
 Single core (concurrency):   T1 T2 T3 T4 T1 T2 T3 T4 T1 ...   (time →)
 Two cores (parallelism):     core 0: T1 T3 T1 T3 ...
                              core 1: T2 T4 T2 T4 ...

⚠️ S26 MC16：✔「Concurrency can be supported on a single processor core through time multiplexing by the scheduler」。 ✗「Concurrency means performing more than one task at exactly the same time」（嗰個係 parallelism） ✗「Parallelism means tasks only make progress over time on a single core」 ✗「Testing and debugging become easier in multicore systems」 Sp21 MC：multiple tasks make progress on a single processor → concurrency。

3.3 Data vs Task Parallelism ⭐ [4.11]

  • Data parallelism — distributes subsets of the same data across multiple cores, same operation on each core（e.g. 分開 sum 一個 array 嘅兩半；distributed ML）。
  • Task parallelism — distributes threads (tasks) across cores, each thread performing a unique operation。
  • Not mutually exclusive — an application may use both（hybrid）。

📝 HW2 MC2：「Threads on different cores perform the same operation on different subsets of an array」→ data parallelism。「One thread handles input, another does spell-check」→ task parallelism。

4. Amdahl's Law ⭐⭐⭐（計數題必考）[4.12–4.13]

  • S = serial portion，1 − S = parallel portion，N = number of processing cores。
  • As N → ∞, speedup → 1/S.
  • Serial portion has a disproportionate effect on performance gained by adding cores.
  • Amdahl's Law identifies theoretical speedup in latency at a fixed workload。

🧮 speedup ≤ 1 / ( S + (1 − S) / N )

4.1 例子（全部計算過）

題目 S N 計算 Speedup
Slide：75% parallel 0.25 2 1/(0.25 + 0.75/2) = 1/0.625 1.6
Sp21 MC：30% serial 0.3 7 1/(0.3 + 0.7/7) = 1/0.4 2.5
HW2 Q3.1(a)：20% serial 0.2 4 1/(0.2 + 0.8/4) = 1/0.4 2.5
HW2 Q3.1(b) 0.2 ∞ 1/S = 1/0.2 5
S26 MC17 A：81% parallel 0.19 3 1/(0.19 + 0.27) = 1/0.46 2.17
S26 MC17 B：81% parallel 0.19 5 1/(0.19 + 0.162) = 1/0.352 2.84
S26 MC17 C：61% parallel 0.39 3 1/(0.39 + 0.2033) = 1/0.5933 1.69 ✔（1.6–1.7）
S26 MC17 D：61% parallel 0.39 5 1/(0.39 + 0.122) = 1/0.512 1.95

4.2 解題 tips

  • 留意題目畀 parallel % 定 serial %（S = serial！）。
  • 反向題：要 speedup = X with N cores → 解 S：S = (1/X − 1/N) / (1 − 1/N)。
  • 例：想 4 cores 有 3× speedup → S = (1/3 − 1/4)/(1 − 1/4) = (1/12)/(3/4) = 1/9 ≈ 11.1% serial。
  • Speedup 上限永遠 ≤ N 同 ≤ 1/S。

5. Multithreaded Process & TCB ⭐ [4.14–4.18]

5.1 Multithreaded process [4.14]

  • More than one instance of execution，每個有自己 PC。
  • Threads share the same address space → can access the same data.
  • Each thread has its own private set of registers.
  • Context switch between two threads (T1 → T2) of the same process：save T1's register state, restore T2's；address space remains the same → overhead is much smaller。Switching to a thread of a different process → more overhead.
  • Overlap of I/O with other activities within a single program（一個 thread 用 CPU，另一個做 I/O）。

5.2 Thread Control Block (TCB) ⭐ [4.15]

  • Thread = single unique execution context = lightweight process：PC, registers, execution flags, stack。
  • Thread 喺 processor 執行 = resident in the registers；PC register holds the address of the executing instruction。
  • Each thread has a TCB：
    • Execution state：CPU registers, program counter, pointer to stack
    • Scheduling info：state, priority, CPU time
    • Accounting info
    • Various pointers（implementing scheduling queues）
    • Pointer to enclosing process (PCB)
  • ⚠️ Sp21 MC：NOT in TCB → Process identifier（PID 喺 PCB；TCB 只有 pointer to enclosing process）。

5.3 Shared vs Private state [4.16] ⭐

  • Threads encapsulate concurrency — "active" component；address space encapsulates protection — "passive" part.
  • Shared by all threads in a process：contents of memory (global variables, heap)；I/O state (file descriptors, network connections)
  • Private to each thread (kept in TCB)：CPU registers (including PC)；execution stack (parameters, temporary variables, saved PC)
  • If a process has multiple threads, there is no "process state", only the state of each thread（summary）。

5.4 Thread lifecycle [4.17–4.18]

  • new → ready → running → waiting → terminated（同 process 一樣）。
  • "Active" threads are represented by their TCBs; TCBs are organized into queues by state（ready queue、各 device queues：tape unit、disk unit、ethernet…，每個 queue 有自己 scheduling policy）。

📝 HW2 Q2.1：同一 process 兩個 threads 可唔可以有唔同 PC / register 值？→ Yes — 每個 thread 係獨立 flow of execution，有自己 PC、register set、stack；唔 running 時存喺 TCB (Thread Control Block)。

6. User Threads vs Kernel Threads ⭐⭐ [4.19–4.20]

  • User threads — independently executable entities within a program, created and managed by a user-level thread library；visible only to programmers and unknown to the kernel → cannot be scheduled on a CPU directly。
  • Kernel threads — supported and managed by the OS；can be scheduled and executed on a CPU（Windows, Linux, macOS, iOS, Android 都支援）。
  • ⭐ OS only manages and schedules kernel threads.（F19 Q3.1：OS 排程嘅係 kernel-level threads）
  • Three primary thread libraries：POSIX Pthreads、Windows threads、Java threads（對應 POSIX API、Win32 API、Java API）。
  • F20 MC12：provides API for creating and managing threads → Thread library (e.g. POSIX Pthreads)。
  • If a program defines no user threads, there is no kernel thread to schedule for it（user threads = concurrent & modular entities OS 可利用）。
  • TCB 只同 kernel thread 關聯；kernel thread is the only thing the CPU scheduler schedules（summary）。

7. Multithreading Models ⭐⭐⭐ [4.21–4.26]

Model Mapping 優點 缺點 例子
Many-to-One many user threads → 1 kernel thread thread management 喺 user space（efficient） one thread blocking (blocking system call) blocks the entire process；cannot run in parallel on multicore（only one kernel thread active at a time） Solaris Green Threads, GNU Portable Threads（few systems now）
One-to-One each user thread → its own kernel thread maximum concurrency（another thread runs when one blocks）；parallel on multicore creating a user thread requires creating a kernel thread → overhead；number of threads per process may be restricted Windows, Linux（most common now）
Many-to-Many many user threads → smaller or equal number of kernel threads flexible；OS 可預先建立足夠 kernel threads（thread pool）；user threads 數目唔受 kernel threads 限制 difficult to implement in practice Windows ThreadFiber package（otherwise uncommon）
Two-level many-to-many + allows a user thread to be bound to a kernel thread 更靈活 複雜 —
  • Many-to-one 同 many-to-many 要決定邊個 user thread map 去 kernel thread → process-contention scope (PCS)（Ch5）。
  • Concurrency 比較：many-to-one < many-to-many < one-to-one。
  • Most OSes now use the one-to-one model（cores 多咗，限制 kernel threads 數目冇咁重要）[4.26]。
  • LWP (lightweight process)（summary）：user 同 kernel threads 之間嘅 intermediate data structure；對 thread library 嚟講 LWP 好似一個 virtual processor，library 將 user thread schedule 落 LWP；每個 LWP attached to a kernel thread；kernel thread block → LWP 都 block。

⚠️ MC 陷阱：

  • F20 MC13 ✗「A user thread can be mapped to more than one kernel thread」（冇 one-to-many）；✔ kernel threads scheduled by OS；✔ users manage user threads；✔ one user thread → at most one kernel thread。
  • Sp21 MC ✗「The many-to-many model is easy to implement in practice」。✔ many-to-one = many user → single kernel；✔ one-to-one more concurrency than many-to-one；✔ most OS now one-to-one。
  • HW2 Q2.2：many-to-one 喺 4 cores 可唔可以 parallel？→ No，只有一個 kernel thread，OS 只 schedule kernel threads，一次只可以喺一個 core 上跑（只有 concurrency 冇 parallelism）；一個 thread blocking system call 仲會 block 成個 process。

8. Threading Issues ⭐⭐ [4.27–4.33]

8.1 fork() and exec() semantics [4.28]

  • Does fork() called by a thread duplicate only the calling thread or all threads?
  • Some UNIX systems have two versions of fork().
  • If exec() is called immediately after forking → duplicating only the calling thread is appropriate（exec 會取代成個 process）。
  • exec() works as normal — replaces the entire process including all threads.

8.2 Signal Handling ⭐⭐ [4.29–4.31]

  • Signals notify a process that a particular event has occurred.
  • Synchronous signals — illegal memory access, division by 0 → delivered to the same process that performed the operation causing the signal。
  • Asynchronous signals — generated by an event external to the running process：terminating with 、timer expires（kill）。
  • 處理：有啲 ignore，有啲（illegal memory access）terminate program。
  • Signal handling pattern：(1) generated by an event → (2) delivered to a process → (3) handled by a default or user-defined handler。
  • Every signal has a default handler (kernel); a user-defined handler can override the default.
  • Multithreaded process 要 deliver 去邊？四個選擇：
    1. To the thread to which the signal applies
    2. To every thread in the process
    3. To certain threads in the process
    4. Assign a specific thread to receive all signals for the process
  • Synchronous signals → delivered to the thread causing the signal（not others）。
  • Some asynchronous signals (e.g. terminate process, Ctrl-C) → sent to all threads.
  • UNIX：kill(pid_t pid, int signal)（deliver to a process）；Pthreads：pthread_kill(pthread_t tid, int signal)（deliver to a specific thread）。
  • ⚠️ 留意：slide 3.41 將 signal() 寫成「send a notification to another process」，但實際 deliver 用 kill()（4.31）；signal(SIGINT, handler) 係安裝 user-defined handler（PA1）。MC 照題目用字揀。

⚠️ S26 MC18 ✗「All signals should be delivered to all threads in a process」。✔ signals can be received sync or async；✔ users can define custom handlers；✔ different signals can have different handlers。 📝 HW2 MC3：division by zero → synchronous；timer expiration → asynchronous。 📝 HW2 Q2.3 (Ctrl+C)：Asynchronous — Ctrl+C makes the terminal send SIGINT, generated by an event external to the running process. Yes, the program can handle it differently: register a user-defined handler with signal(SIGINT, handler) (or sigaction), which overrides the default handler (terminate). (SIGKILL/SIGSTOP cannot be caught.) — PA1 myshell 就係用 signal(SIGINT, handle_sigint)。

8.3 Thread Cancellation ⭐ [4.32–4.33]

  • Terminating a thread before it has finished；the thread to be cancelled = target thread.
  • Asynchronous cancellation — terminates the target thread immediately（可能喺 critical update 中途 → OS 未必可以 reclaim 所有 resources）。
  • Deferred cancellation — the target thread periodically checks whether it should terminate, allowing it to terminate itself in an orderly fashion（preferred）。
  • Invoking cancellation only requests it; actual cancellation depends on thread state and type.
  • Pthreads：if cancellation is disabled, it remains pending until enabled；default type is deferred.
  • Code [4.33]：
#include <pthread.h>
pthread_t tid;
pthread_create(&tid, 0, worker, NULL);   /* worker = start routine */
pthread_cancel(tid);                     /* request cancellation */
pthread_join(tid, NULL);                 /* wait for thread to terminate */

📝 HW2 MC4：deferred cancellation → target thread periodically checks whether it should terminate。

8.4 Thread-Local Storage（課本補充，Sp21 MC 考過）

  • Thread-local storage (TLS) is data that is unique to each thread（類似 static data 但每個 thread 一份；同 local variables 唔同，local 只喺一個 function call 內存在）。

9. OS Examples [4.34–4.38]

9.1 Windows Threads

  • Windows application = separate process，每個 process 可有多個 threads；Windows uses the one-to-one mapping。
  • Thread 組成：thread ID, register set, program counter, separate user and kernel stacks, private data storage area（run-time libraries / DLLs 用）。
  • Register set + stacks + private storage area = context of the thread.
  • 數據結構：
    • ETHREAD (executive thread block) — pointer to process & to KTHREAD（kernel space）
    • KTHREAD (kernel thread block) — scheduling & synchronization info, kernel-mode stack, pointer to TEB（kernel space）
    • TEB (thread environment block) — thread id, user-mode stack, thread-local storage（user space）

9.2 Linux Threads ⭐ [4.37–4.38]

  • Linux does not differentiate between threads and processes — both are called tasks.
  • A thread in Linux is a process that may share certain resources with other threads；每個 thread 有自己 task_struct。
  • 對比 Windows：Windows 一個 PCB（shared resources）指向兩個 TCB；Linux 就係兩個 normal task_struct，set up to share resources。
  • clone() 用 flags 指定 share 咩：
clone(CLONE_VM | CLONE_FS | CLONE_FILES | CLONE_SIGHAND, 0);
  • CLONE_VM：share address space；CLONE_FS：share file-system info；CLONE_FILES：share open files；CLONE_SIGHAND：share signal handlers。
  • Instead of copying all data structures like fork(), the new task points to the parent's data structures depending on the flags.
  • A normal fork() can be implemented as clone(SIGCHLD, 0)（乜都唔 share → 等於 process）。

10. Pthreads API 速查（Lab 4 / Ch6 都用）

#include <pthread.h>
int pthread_create(pthread_t *tid, const pthread_attr_t *attr, void *(*start)(void *), void *arg);
int pthread_join(pthread_t tid, void **retval);     /* wait for thread tid */
void pthread_exit(void *retval);
int pthread_cancel(pthread_t tid);
pthread_t pthread_self(void);
/* mutex */
pthread_mutex_t m; pthread_mutex_init(&m, NULL);
pthread_mutex_lock(&m); /* critical section */ pthread_mutex_unlock(&m);
/* semaphore */
#include <semaphore.h>
sem_t s; sem_init(&s, 0 /*shared between threads*/, 1 /*initial value*/);
sem_wait(&s); /* P / wait: decrement or block */ sem_post(&s); /* V / signal: increment */
  • 編譯：gcc prog.c -lpthread（或 -pthread）。
  • 典型 pattern：
void *worker(void *arg) { int id = *(int *)arg; ...; return NULL; }
int main() {
    pthread_t t[4]; int ids[4];
    for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) { ids[i] = i; pthread_create(&t[i], NULL, worker, &ids[i]); }
    for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) pthread_join(t[i], NULL);
}
  • ⚠️ 傳 &i（loop variable）畀多個 thread 會 race（i 會變）→ 用 ids[i] array。

11. 常見陷阱 & MC 速查

敘述 真/假
Each thread has its own address space and file descriptors ✘（share）
Threads of the same process cannot run concurrently on multiple cores ✘（one-to-one 可以 parallel）
Thread creation is usually faster than process creation ✔（S26 MC15）
Multithreaded process can't use multicore ✘
Stack is shared among threads ✘
Heap / global data shared ✔
Concurrency possible on single core ✔
Parallelism possible on single core ✘
Data parallelism = same operation on different data subsets ✔
As N→∞, Amdahl speedup → 1/S ✔
Speedup can exceed N ✘
OS schedules user threads directly ✘（kernel threads）
Many-to-one allows parallelism on multicore ✘
Many-to-one: one blocking call blocks whole process ✔
One-to-one: maximum concurrency, but overhead of kernel threads ✔
Many-to-many easy to implement ✘
User thread can map to multiple kernel threads ✘
Most OS use one-to-one now ✔
Division by zero → asynchronous signal ✘（synchronous）
Ctrl-C → asynchronous ✔
All signals delivered to all threads ✘
User-defined handler can override default ✔
Asynchronous cancellation = target checks periodically ✘（嗰個係 deferred）
Pthreads default cancellation type = deferred ✔
Linux distinguishes threads and processes ✘（both tasks）
fork() = clone(SIGCHLD, 0) ✔
PID is in TCB ✘
Windows uses one-to-one ✔

12. Past-paper 題目（Ch4）

  • S26 MC15：true about multithreaded process → C. Thread creation is usually faster than process creation
  • S26 MC16：concurrency/parallelism → C
  • S26 MC17：Amdahl 1.6–1.7 → C (61%, 3 cores) = 1.69
  • S26 MC18：false about signals → C. All signals delivered to all threads
  • F19 Q2.3：unique vs shared（§1.2）；Q3.1：OS schedules kernel threads；user-thread → kernel-thread/LWP scheduling = PCS (process-contention scope)，within the same process, based on priority set by programmer；CPU scheduling (SCS) 喺全系統 kernel threads 之間揀
  • F20 MC11 stack；MC12 thread library；MC13 user thread → multiple kernel threads（false）；Q2.3 heavyweight vs lightweight
  • Sp21 MC：Amdahl 30% serial 7 cores → 2.5；TCB 唔包括 process identifier；single processor 多 task progress → concurrency；many-to-many easy to implement（false）；TLS → unique to each thread
  • HW2：MC1 responsiveness、MC2 data parallelism、MC3 sync/async、MC4 deferred、Q2.1 TCB、Q2.2 many-to-one、Q2.3 Ctrl+C、Q3.1 Amdahl（2.5；5）

  2. 50% serial 嘅 program 用 4 cores speedup？（1/(0.5+0.125) = 1.6）；無限 cores？（2）
  3. Many-to-one model 兩大缺點？

5Ch5 CPU SchedulingCPU 排程

📌 考試範圍提示：Ch5 係分數最重嘅一章（S26：27/100；F19：40/100；F20：27+；Sp21：26）。必考：

  1. Gantt chart + average waiting / turnaround / response time：FCFS、SJF、SRTF、RR、Priority（± RR）、MLFQ（每年都有！）、自創 policy（LUF、dynamic priority）
  2. RM / EDF real-time Gantt + missed deadlines
  3. MC / short：preemptive vs non-preemptive、convoy effect、starvation & aging、MLFQ 優點、RR vs FCFS、exponential averaging、Little's formula、PCS vs SCS、processor affinity、load balancing、hard vs soft real-time ⚠️ Response time 本科定義 = 第一次運行「完結」時間 − arrival time（slide 5.12），唔係課本「第一次開始」！

1. Basic Concepts（基本概念）[5.4–5.7]

1.1 CPU–I/O Burst Cycle

  • Objective of multiprogramming：have a process running at all times → maximize CPU utilization.
  • Process execution consists of cycles of CPU execution and I/O wait — CPU burst and I/O burst.
  • [5.5] Histogram of CPU-burst times：many short CPU bursts, few long ones (long-tail distribution)。I/O-bound → many short CPU bursts；CPU-bound → a few long CPU bursts. 呢個分佈對設計 scheduling algorithm 好重要。
  • CPU idle 時，OS 由 ready queue 揀一個 process（除非 ready queue empty）→ 由 CPU scheduler（process scheduler / short-term scheduler） 揀。

1.2 When does scheduling happen? ⭐⭐ [5.6]

CPU scheduling 可能喺四種情況發生：

  1. Running → waiting（e.g. I/O request, wait()）
  2. Process terminates
  3. Running → ready（e.g. interrupt / timer）
  4. Waiting → ready（I/O completion）或者 new → ready（新 process 到達，priority 更高）
  • Scheduling under 1 and 2 is non-preemptive (cooperative) — process gives up the CPU voluntarily。
  • Scheduling under 3 and 4 is preemptive.
  • Preemptive 要考慮：access to shared data (race condition, Ch6)、preemption while in kernel mode、interrupts during crucial OS activities。

📝 Sp21 MC：CPU scheduling decisions may take place in → all of the above（running→waiting、terminates、running→ready）。 📝 HW2 MC5：preemptive 例子 → time quantum expires, running process moved back to the ready queue（A terminate、B I/O、D wait() 都係自願 → non-preemptive）。

1.3 Dispatcher [5.7]

  • Dispatcher module gives control of the CPU to the process selected by the CPU scheduler：
    1. switching context from one process to another
    2. switching to user mode
    3. jumping to the proper location in the user program to restart it
  • Dispatch latency — time for the dispatcher to stop one process and start another（overhead）。 應該越快越好。
  • vmstat（Linux）可睇 context switches 數目（每秒幾百次）。

2. Scheduling Metrics（排程指標）⭐⭐⭐ [5.8, 5.12]

Metric 定義 目標
CPU utilization fraction of the time that the CPU is busy max
Throughput number of processes completed per unit time（favors short processes） max
Turnaround time (TAT) time to execute a particular process = completion time − arrival time min
Waiting time (WT) total time spent waiting in the ready queue = TAT − CPU burst min
Response time (RT) time from submission until the first response is produced min
Fairness adequate allocation for different jobs/processes —

2.1 公式（本科 convention，必背）⭐

  • Response time 嘅「first run」完結 = 第一次上 CPU 嘅嗰段（RR / MLFQ 就係 end of first quantum，或者 burst 細過 quantum 就係完成時間）[5.12]。
    • Slide 5.13 例：RR q=4，P1(24), P2(3), P3(3) 全部 t=0 到 → RT：P1 = 4, P2 = 7, P3 = 10，avg = 7。
    • S26 MLFQ 官方：P1 response = 4 − 0 = 4（P1 跑 0–4）。
    • 非 preemptive (FCFS/SJF) 嘅 RT = TAT（第一次 run 就跑完）。
  • Waiting time 另一個計法（gap method）：將 Gantt chart 上 process 唔喺 CPU 嘅時間（由 arrival 起）加埋。兩個方法結果一樣：
    • S26 P2：gap = (4−2)+(21−8)+(40−29)+(57−45)+(64−58) = 44 ✔；formula = 71 − 2 − 25 = 44 ✔。
  • S26 MC19 ✔「Response time is the amount of time from when a request is submitted until the first response is produced」。✗ WT = arrival 到完成（嗰個係 TAT）；✗ TAT = 等 ready queue 時間（嗰個係 WT）；✗ throughput = CPU busy fraction（嗰個係 utilization）。

🧮 Turnaround = Completion − Arrival

🧮 Waiting = Turnaround − CPU burst

🧮 Response = (completion of the FIRST run) − Arrival

3. FCFS — First-Come, First-Served ⭐ [5.9–5.10]

  • 按 arrival order 排；non-preemptive（跑完 current CPU burst 先放）。
  • 例：P1 = 24, P2 = 3, P3 = 3（全部 t=0）
    • 次序 P1, P2, P3：0 P1 24 P2 27 P3 30 → WT = 0, 24, 27 → avg 17；TAT avg = (24+27+30)/3 = 27
    • 次序 P2, P3, P1：0 P2 3 P3 6 P1 30 → WT = 6, 0, 3 → avg 3；TAT avg = 13
  • ⭐ Convoy effect — short processes stuck behind a long process，令 average waiting time 好長，完全取決於 arrival order。One CPU-bound + many I/O-bound → low I/O device utilization。（例：銀行排隊存張支票，前面有人開戶）

📝 HW2 MC6：convoy effect → several short processes wait behind a long process that arrived first。 📝 F19 Q3.2：FCFS 問題 = convoy effect（short process stuck behind a long process）。

4. RR — Round Robin ⭐⭐⭐ [5.11–5.16]

  • FCFS + preemption：每個 process 得 time quantum q（通常 10–100 ms）；用完 → preempted and added to the end (tail) of the ready queue。
  • n processes → each gets 1/n of CPU time in chunks of at most q；no process waits more than (n − 1)q time units ⭐（ignoring context switch）。
  • Timer interrupts every quantum；如果 burst（或 remaining）< q，process 自己完成就放 CPU。
  • 例 [5.13]：q = 4，P1 24, P2 3, P3 3 → 0 P1 4 P2 7 P3 10 P1 14 P1 18 P1 22 P1 26 P1 30
    • WT：P1 = 6, P2 = 4, P3 = 7 → avg 5.67；TAT avg = (30+7+10)/3 = 15.67；RT = 4, 7, 10 → avg 7
  • RR：average waiting time can be long, but more "fair"；better for short jobs than FCFS；better average response time → good for interactive jobs.

4.1 Time quantum 大細 [5.14, 5.16] ⭐

  • q large → RR degenerates to FCFS.（Sp21 MC 答案：FCFS）
  • q small → many context switches; q must be large w.r.t. context-switch time (< 10 µs), otherwise overhead too high.（q 極細 = processor sharing：好似 n 個 processor 每個 1/n 速度）
  • Average turnaround time does not necessarily improve as q increases.
  • TAT improves if most processes finish their CPU burst within a single quantum.
  • Rule of thumb：80% of CPU bursts should be shorter than q.

4.2 FCFS vs RR ⭐（F19 Q3.2 考過）[5.15]

  • Is RR always better than FCFS? No! 例：10 jobs start at the same time, each needs 100 s, q = 1 s → average turnaround time much worse under RR（全部差唔多 1000 s 先完；FCFS 平均 550 s），but average response time much better。
  • 一般而言 no clear winner between FCFS and RR in TAT/WT — 取決於 burst times、arrival pattern、quantum size。

📝 F19 Q3.2 Model answer：RR performs worse than FCFS in turnaround time when all processes have the same (equal) CPU burst times that are longer than the time quantum — the jobs are interleaved and all finish near the end, whereas FCFS finishes them one by one.

4.3 RR 做 Gantt chart 規則（考試用）

  1. 用 queue（寫低每個時刻 queue 內容！）。
  2. 新到達 process 加入 queue tail；quantum 用完嘅 process 都去 tail。
  3. ⚠️ 同一時刻「新 process 到達」同「running process quantum 用完」→ convention：新到達嗰個先入 queue，然後先放被 preempt 嗰個（寫低 assumption）。
  4. Process burst 完 → 即刻揀下一個（唔使等 quantum 完）。
  5. Queue 空而 running process quantum 用完 → 佢繼續跑（再得一個 quantum）。

5. SJF / SRTF — Shortest Job First ⭐⭐⭐ [5.17–5.24]

5.1 SJF（non-preemptive）

  • Associate each process with the length of its next CPU burst; schedule the shortest.（更準確叫 shortest-next-CPU-burst）
  • ⭐ SJF is optimal — gives the minimum average waiting time for a given set of processes.
  • Difficulty：knowing the length of the next CPU request.（所以 practical 難 implement；F20 MC15 答案 SJF difficult to implement）
  • Sketch proof：moving a short process before a long one decreases the waiting time of the short process more than it increases the waiting time of the long process → average waiting time decreases.
  • 例 [5.18]：P1 = 6, P2 = 8, P3 = 7, P4 = 3。Slide 個 chart 當全部 t = 0 到：0 P4 3 P1 9 P3 16 P2 24 → avg WT = (3 + 16 + 9 + 0)/4 = 7（slide 答案）。
    • ⚠️ 如果真係跟表入面 arrival（0, 2, 4, 5）：t=0 只有 P1 → 0 P1 6 P4 9 P3 16 P2 24 → WT = 0, 14, 5, 1 → avg 5。考試一定要睇清楚有冇 arrival time。

5.2 SRTF — Shortest-Remaining-Time-First（preemptive SJF）⭐

  • New arrival with burst shorter than the remaining time of the running process → preempts it.
  • Scheduling 喺 every arrival (condition 4) 都要 invoke。
  • 例 [5.23]：P1(0,8), P2(1,4), P3(2,9), P4(3,5) → 0 P1 1 P2 5 P4 10 P1 17 P3 26
    • avg WT = [(10−1) + (1−1) + (17−2) + (5−3)]/4 = 26/4 = 6.5

5.3 比較 [5.24] ⭐

  • SJF/SRTF are the best for minimizing average waiting time (and average turnaround time).
  • Provably optimal：SJF among non-preemptive, SRTF among preemptive. SRTF is always at least as good as SJF.
  • SJF/SRTF = FCFS if all processes have the same CPU burst.
  • Can lead to starvation of long processes if shorter processes keep arriving；fairness cannot be enforced.
  • F20 MC14：best (min) average waiting time → SJF（SRTF 亦接受）。

5.4 Exponential Averaging（預測下一個 CPU burst）⭐⭐ [5.19–5.22]

🧮 τ(n+1) = α · t(n) + (1 − α) · τ(n)，0 ≤ α ≤ 1

  • tₙ = actual length of the n-th CPU burst；τₙ₊₁ = predicted value for the next burst；通常 α = ½。
  • α = 0 → τₙ₊₁ = τₙ（recent history does not count）；α = 1 → τₙ₊₁ = tₙ（only the last burst counts）。
  • 展開：τₙ₊₁ = α tₙ + (1−α) α tₙ₋₁ + … + (1−α)ʲ α tₙ₋ⱼ + … + (1−α)ⁿ⁺¹ τ₀ → all past values count but recent ones weigh more；past effect diminishes exponentially（α=0.5，10 rounds 後 (1/2)¹⁰ = 1/1024 可忽略）。
  • 預測取決於 (1) all previous actual bursts with different weights, (2) the initial estimate τ₀。
  • Slide 5.20 例（α = ½, τ₀ = 10）：
i 0 1 2 3 4 5 6 7
CPU burst tᵢ 6 4 6 4 13 13 13 …
guess τᵢ 10 8 6 6 5 9 11 12
  • HW2 MC9：τ = 10, t = 6, α = 0.25 → 0.25×6 + 0.75×10 = 1.5 + 7.5 = 9 ms。

6. Priority Scheduling ⭐⭐ [5.25–5.27]

  • Each process has a priority number；CPU allocated to the highest priority（本科：smallest integer = highest priority，除非題目另講）。
  • Preemptive（higher-priority arrival preempts）或 non-preemptive。
  • Equal priority → FCFS order（或者 RR）。
  • SJF is a special case of priority scheduling where priority = inverse of predicted next CPU burst.
  • ⭐ Problem：Starvation (indefinite blocking) — low-priority processes may never execute.
  • ⭐ Solution：Aging — gradually increase the priority of a process as it waits（需要每個 process 喺 ready queue 有 timer）。
  • Sp21 MC：main problem of priority scheduling → some processes may never execute (starvation)；HW2 MC7：aging → address starvation by gradually increasing the priority of a waiting process。
  • 例 [5.26]（all t=0；P1 10/3, P2 1/1, P3 2/4, P4 1/5, P5 5/2）→ 0 P2 1 P5 6 P1 16 P3 18 P4 19，avg WT = (6+0+16+18+1)/5 = 8.2
  • Priority + RR [5.27]：run the highest priority；same priority → round-robin。例 q = 2（burst/priority：P1 4/3, P2 5/2, P3 8/2, P4 7/1, P5 3/3，全部 t=0）→ 0 P4 7 P2 9 P3 11 P2 13 P3 15 P2 16 P3 20 P1 22 P5 24 P1 26 P5 27
    • P4（priority 1）先跑完；P2、P3（priority 2）RR；P2 喺 16 完咗之後 P3 獨自跑到 20；最後 P1、P5（priority 3）RR。

7. Multilevel Queue（多級佇列）⭐ [5.28–5.29]

  • Priority scheduling combined with RR；priority assigned statically；a process remains in the same queue for its whole runtime.
  • Partition processes into queues based on process type（例：real-time → system → interactive → batch）。
  • Each queue can have its own scheduling algorithm.
  • Scheduling among queues：(1) fixed-priority preemptive scheduling（高 queue 空先輪到低 queue → 可能 starvation）或 (2) time-slice（each queue gets a certain % of CPU time，e.g. 60%, 20%, 10%, 10%）。
  • Sp21 MC：Multilevel queue（固定）有 starvation（free from starvation 嘅只有 FCFS 同 RR）。

8. MLFQ — Multilevel Feedback Queue ⭐⭐⭐（每年必考 Gantt）[5.30–5.35]

  • A process can move between queues；aging can be implemented this way → flexibility.
  • MLFQ 由以下 parameters 定義：
    1. number of queues
    2. scheduling algorithm for each queue
    3. method to upgrade a process（to higher queue）
    4. method to demote a process（to lower queue）
    5. method to determine which queue a process enters when it needs service

8.1 Slide example 規則（考試照用）[5.31] ⭐

  • Q0 – RR（slide 5.31: q = 8；例子 5.32–5.34 用 q = 4）；Q1 – RR（q = 16 / 8）；Q2 – FCFS
  • A new job always enters Q0 (RR, FCFS order)；it also preempts jobs from Q1 or Q2 currently running.
  • Gets q₀ in Q0；if not finished → moved to Q1. In Q1 gets q₁ additional；if still not complete → preempted and moved to Q2.
  • ⭐ If a job from Q1 or Q2 is preempted by a new job from Q0, it joins the HEAD of Q1 or Q2, with the remaining quantum (Q1) or remaining CPU burst (Q2).（唔會攞一個全新 quantum；唔會因為被 preempt 而 demote）
  • Q1 job 只會喺 Q0 空先跑；Q2 job 只會喺 Q0 和 Q1 都空先跑。
  • Q1 job 永遠唔會 preempt Q2 job（因為冇 process 直接 arrive 去 Q1）— summary 重點。
  • A job in Q0 is never preempted（Q0 內部只有 RR）。
  • Burst ≤ q₀ + q₁（e.g. 4 + 8 = 12）嘅 job 永遠唔會落 Q2。
  • This approximates SRTF：CPU-bound jobs drop like a rock；short-running I/O-bound jobs stay near the top.

8.2 Lecture example [5.32–5.34]（Q0 q=4, Q1 q=8, Q2 FCFS）

Process Arrival Burst
P1 0 10
P2 2 15
P3 5 2
P4 12 14
P5 18 6 
0 P1 4 P2 8 P3 10 P1 12 P4 16 P1 18 P5 22 P1 24 P2 32 P4 40 P5 42 P2 45 P4 47
 Q0   Q0   Q0   Q1   Q0   Q1   Q0   Q1   Q1   Q1   Q1   Q2   Q2
  • t=4 P1 用完 Q0 quantum → Q1；t=8 P2 → Q1；t=10 P3 完；P1 喺 Q1 跑；t=12 P4 到 → preempt P1（P1 返 Q1 head，剩 6 quantum）；t=16 P4 → Q1 tail；P1 繼續（剩 quantum 6）；t=18 P5 到 → preempt P1；t=22 P5 → Q1；P1 跑到 24 完成（Q1 總共用 2+2+2 = 6 ≤ 8）；P2 24–32 用完 Q1 quantum → Q2；P4 32–40 → Q2；P5 40–42 完成；P2 42–45 完；P4 45–47 完。
  • WT：P1 = 14, P2 = 28, P3 = 3, P4 = 21, P5 = 18 → avg = 84/5 = 16.8 ✔

8.3 MLFQ advantages ⭐⭐（F19 Q3.3、F20 Q2.5 必背）[5.35]

  • Used in BSD Unix, Solaris, Windows NT and later。
  1. It does not need prior knowledge of the next CPU burst time（SJF/SRTF 需要）
  2. It handles interactive jobs well — better response time than RR（short jobs 喺 top queue 完成）
  3. It produces similar performance to SJF/SRTF
  4. It is "fair" — makes progress on CPU-bound jobs
  • Starvation 可以用 reshuffling jobs periodically（e.g. 一段時間後將所有 jobs 搬返 top queue）解決。

📝 F20 Q2.5 Model answer：(1) MLFQ resembles the performance of SJF/SRTF without requiring prior knowledge of the next CPU burst time; (2) like RR, it gives good response time to interactive (short, I/O-bound) jobs since they finish in the top queues; (3) it is fair — all processes, long or short, make progress (and starvation can be avoided by periodically moving jobs up / aging). 📝 F19 Q3.3：MLFQ approximates SJF because short (interactive, I/O-bound) jobs finish within the top high-priority queue(s), while long CPU-bound jobs are demoted to lower queues — so short jobs get served first like SJF. Its major advantage over SJF is that it does not require knowing the CPU burst times in advance. ⚠️ S26 MC20 ✔「Each queue can use a different scheduling algorithm」；✗ processes never move；✗ requires burst times known；✗ non-preemptive。Sp21 MC ✔「handles interactive jobs well by delivering similar performance as SJF」；✗ needs prior knowledge；✗ classification permanent；✗ won't suffer starvation。

8.4 MLFQ 做題 checklist ✍️

  1. 每個時間點寫低：Q0、Q1、Q2 內容（同次序）、running process、佢喺邊個 queue、剩餘 quantum、剩餘 burst。
  2. 事件：arrival（入 Q0 tail；如果 running 係 Q1/Q2 → preempt，被 preempt 嘅返 head of own queue，保留剩餘 quantum）；quantum 用完（未完成 → 降一級，入 tail）；完成。
  3. Gantt 上標 process@Queue（HW2 要求 label queue）。
  4. 同一 process 由 Q0 用完 quantum 立即喺 Q1 繼續跑（因為 Q0 空）→ Gantt 可合併，但計 response time 要用 Q0 quantum 完結時間（S26 P5：49 − 45 = 4）。
  5. 題目規則永遠優先（S26 第 8 條、F20 aging timer、HW2 「retains unused quantum」）。

9. Thread Scheduling ⭐⭐ [5.36–5.37]

  • On modern OSes, kernel-level threads are scheduled (by the OS); user-level threads are managed by the thread library.
  • LWP (lightweight process) — intermediate data structure；appears as a virtual processor on which user threads are scheduled to run；each LWP attached to a kernel thread。
  • ⭐ Process-Contention Scope (PCS) — under many-to-one and many-to-many models, the thread library schedules user-level threads onto an LWP；competition among threads belonging to the same process；typically by priority set by programmers（thread library 通常唔會調整 priority）；PCS typically preempts the running thread in favour of a higher-priority user thread。
  • ⭐ System-Contention Scope (SCS) — the kernel schedules kernel threads onto an available CPU；competition among all threads in the system → 就係 CPU scheduling。
  • One-to-one systems (Windows, Linux, Solaris) schedule threads using only SCS.

📝 HW2 MC8：thread library 揀 user thread run on LWP → PCS, because the competing threads belong to the same process。 📝 F19 Q3.1：The OS schedules kernel-level threads. Mapping user threads onto kernel threads/LWPs (PCS) is done by the thread library, based on priorities set by the programmer, among the user threads of the same process; CPU scheduling (SCS) decides which kernel thread runs on a CPU among all kernel threads in the system.

10. Multiple-Processor Scheduling ⭐⭐ [5.38–5.46]

10.1 AMP vs SMP [5.38–5.39]

  • Multiprocessor 而家包括 multicore CPUs, multithreaded cores, NUMA systems, heterogeneous multiprocessing。
  • Asymmetric multiprocessing (AMP) — only one processor (the master server) accesses kernel data structures（唔使 data sharing）；all scheduling decisions, I/O processing, system activities handled by the master；other processors execute only user code；⭐ the master server can become a potential bottleneck。
  • Symmetric multiprocessing (SMP) — each processor is self-scheduling，with one common ready queue or a private ready queue per processor；每個 processor 嘅 scheduler examine ready queue 揀 thread。
    • Common queue：must ensure two processors don't choose the same thread（race condition） 同 processes 唔會 lost。
    • All modern OSes support SMP（Windows, Linux, macOS, Android, iOS）。

📝 F20 Q2.4 Model answer（common vs per-processor ready queue）：A single common ready queue achieves load balancing easily — no processor is idle while the queue is non-empty — but it is hard to keep processor affinity (a thread may run on a different CPU each time, losing its cache contents) and the shared queue needs locking (possible race condition/contention). Per-processor private ready queues naturally provide processor affinity (and no contention on a shared queue), but need extra mechanisms such as push/pull migration to balance the load.

10.2 Multicore & Multithreaded cores (CMT) ⭐ [5.40–5.44]

  • Multiple cores on the same chip — faster and consume less power, but complicate scheduling.
  • ⭐ Memory stall：when a processor accesses memory, it spends a significant amount of time waiting for data to become available（modern processors much faster than memory, esp. on cache miss）。（處理器可能 50% 時間喺等 memory）
  • Chip multithreading (CMT)（Intel = hyperthreading）：multiple hardware threads per core；each hardware thread has its own architectural state (PC, register set) and appears as a logical CPU。
  • If one thread stalls waiting for memory, the core switches to another hardware thread.
  • Dual-threaded dual-core → 4 logical processors；quad-core × 2 threads → 8 logical processors；UltraSPARC T3：16 cores × 8 threads = 128 logical processors。
  • Two levels of scheduling：
    1. OS decides which software (kernel) thread runs on each logical CPU（= CPU scheduling）
    2. Each core decides which hardware thread runs on the physical core（e.g. RR on UltraSPARC T3）
  • 三層 mapping [5.44]：user thread → LWP/kernel thread（PCS, thread library）；kernel (software) thread → logical CPU / hardware thread（CPU scheduling, OS）；hardware thread → core（core 自己，typically RR）。

📝 F19 Q2.5：multicore 優點 = faster + less power；CMT 原因 = memory stall — 一個 hardware thread 等 memory 時 core 可以跑另一個 thread（S26 MC22 ✔「another thread may run when one thread stalls waiting for memory」）。

10.3 Processor Affinity ⭐⭐ [5.45]

  • When a thread has run on a processor, that processor's cache holds its memory accesses → the thread has affinity for that processor.
  • High cost of invalidating and repopulating caches → most SMP systems try to avoid migrating processes between processors.
  • ⭐ Per-processor ready queues provide processor affinity for free!
  • Soft affinity — OS attempts to keep a process on the same processor but doesn't guarantee it（可以 migrate during load balancing）。
  • Hard affinity — a process specifies a subset of processors it may run on.（Linux：soft affinity + sched_setaffinity() for hard affinity）
  • NUMA：CPU 存取近佢嘅 memory 較快 → affinity to both processor and memory。

📝 HW2 Q2.4：hard affinity {CPU0, CPU1}，CPU2 idle → No，hard affinity must be respected；load balancing 只可以將 T 由 CPU0 搬去 CPU1。

10.4 Load Balancing ⭐ [5.46]

  • Keep workload evenly distributed（只喺 per-processor private queues 時需要）。
  • Push migration — a specific task periodically checks loads and pushes tasks from overloaded to idle/less-busy CPUs.
  • Pull migration — an idle processor pulls a waiting task from a busy processor.
  • Not mutually exclusive — often both implemented (Linux CFS).
  • ⭐ Load balancing often counteracts the benefits of processor affinity（natural tension）。
  • S26 MC22 ✗「The motivation of placing multiple cores on the same chip is to simplify the scheduler」（其實 complicate scheduling）；✔ AMP master bottleneck；✔ memory stall → run another thread；✔ affinity keeps process on same CPU for cache。

11. Real-Time CPU Scheduling ⭐⭐⭐ [5.47–5.52]

11.1 Hard vs Soft ⭐

  • Real-time scheduling demands performance guarantee — predictability.
  • Hard real-time — a task must be serviced by its deadline; service after the deadline is the same as no service.
  • Soft real-time — no guarantee when a critical real-time process will be scheduled; only guarantees it will be given preference over noncritical processes.
  • Real-time OS scheduler must support a priority-based algorithm with preemption（但 preemptive priority-based 只保證 soft real-time）。
  • Sp21 MC ✔「Soft real-time systems guarantee only that the process will be prioritized over non-critical processes」；✗ doesn't demand guarantee；✗ hard RT can service after deadline；✗ priority without preemption。

11.2 Periodic tasks [5.48]

  • 參數：processing time t, deadline d, period p，0 ≤ t ≤ d ≤ p；rate = 1/p。
  • Admission control：process announces deadline；scheduler admits only if it can guarantee completion by the deadline。
  • CPU utilization of task i = tᵢ / pᵢ；total U = Σ tᵢ/pᵢ（U > 1 → 一定有 miss）。

11.3 Rate-Monotonic (RM) ⭐⭐ [5.49–5.50]

  • Static priority = inverse of period：shorter period → higher priority（rationale：tasks requiring CPU more often get higher priority）。Preemptive.
  • RM is optimal among static-priority algorithms：if a set can't be scheduled by RM, no other static-priority algorithm can.
  • Only handles periodic processes with constant processing time.
  • 例 [5.49]：P1 (p=50, t=20), P2 (p=100, t=35)：U = 0.40 + 0.35 = 75% → OK：0 P1 20 P2 50 P1 70 P2 75 idle 100
  • 例 [5.50]：P1 (50, 25), P2 (80, 35)：U = 0.50 + 0.44 = 94% → 0 P1 25 P2 50 P1 75 P2 85 → P2 misses its deadline (80) by finishing at 85。
  • 課本 bound（slides 冇，但好用）：RM guaranteed if U ≤ N(2^{1/N} − 1)：N=1 → 1；N=2 → 0.828；N=3 → 0.780；N=4 → 0.757；N→∞ → ln 2 ≈ 0.693。超過 bound 唔一定 miss（要畫 Gantt 確認）。

11.4 EDF — Earliest Deadline First ⭐⭐ [5.51–5.52]

  • Dynamic priorities according to deadline：earlier (absolute) deadline → higher priority. Preemptive。
  • 例 [5.51]（P1 50/25, P2 80/35）：0 P1 25 P2 60 P1 85 P2 100 P1 125 P2 145 idle 150 → no deadline missed（t=50 時 P2 deadline 80 < P1 新 deadline 100 → P2 繼續）。
  • EDF does not require processes to be periodic, nor a constant CPU time per burst；only requirement：announce its deadline when it becomes runnable.
  • ⭐ EDF is theoretically optimal — can meet all deadlines with CPU utilization up to 100%（但實際上 context switching + interrupt handling 令佢做唔到 100%）。
  • S26 MC21：assigns priorities according to deadlines → EDF。

📝 Sp21 Q2.3(b)：EDF is better — unlike RM with static priorities, EDF uses dynamic priorities based on deadlines, which can increase the fraction of met deadlines (schedulable up to 100% utilization). 📝 HW2 Q2.5（A: p=50,t=25；B: p=80,t=35；t=50 時 A 新 instance deadline 100，B 剩 10 deadline 80）→ No：RM 揀 A（shorter period，static）→ B 75–85 miss 80；EDF 揀 B（deadline 80 < 100）→ B 50–60 ✓，A 60–85 ✓。

11.5 RM / EDF 做題方法 ✍️

  1. 列出每個 task 嘅 release times（0, p, 2p, …）同 absolute deadlines（release + d；冇講 d 就 d = p）。
  2. 每個 time unit（或者每個事件：release / completion）揀：
    • RM：ready jobs 入面 period 最短（固定）嗰個。
    • EDF：ready jobs 入面 absolute deadline 最早嗰個。
  3. Ties：題目有講就跟（S26：smaller process ID）；冇講：keep the running job（唔 preempt）（F19、Sp21 官方答案都係咁），再唔得就 smaller ID。寫低 assumption。
  4. Soft real-time：miss 咗 deadline 嘅 job 會繼續跑完（Sp21 hint；S26 官方亦係咁），但 count 為 missed。
  5. 數 missed deadlines：每個 deadline ≤ 時限範圍嘅 job，完成時間 > deadline（或者未完成）→ miss。
  6. 先計 U = Σt/p 做 sanity check。

S26 Q4.2（P1 t=1 d=3 p=4；P2 2/4/5；P3 2/6/7；P4 1/5/8；0–16，ties → smaller ID）：

  • RM（priority P1 > P2 > P3 > P4）：0 P1 1 P2 3 P3 4 P1 5 P2 7 P3 8 P1 9 P3 10 P2 12 P1 13 P3 14 P3 15 P2 16
    • P3 job (d=6) finishes 8 ✗；P4 never runs → misses d=5 ✗ and d=13 ✗；P3 job2 (d=13) finishes 14 ✗ → RM misses 4 deadlines（U = 0.25+0.4+0.286+0.125 = 1.06 > 1）
  • EDF：0 P1 1 P2 3 P4 4 P3 6 P1 7 P2 9 P1 10 P3 12 P4 13 P2 15 P1 16
    • P2 job3 (d=14) finishes 15 ✗；P1 job4 (d=15) finishes 16 ✗ → EDF misses 2 deadlines

Sp21 Q2.3（P1 1/3/3, P2 2/4/4，0–14）：

  • EDF：0 P1 1 P2 3 P1 4 P2 6 P1 7 idle 8 P2 10 P1 11 idle 12 P1 13 P2 14（t=9 tie deadline 12 → keep P2）
  • RM：0 P1 1 P2 3 P1 4 P2 6 P1 7 idle 8 P2 9 P1 10 P2 11 idle 12 P1 13 P2 14
  • 兩者都冇 miss（U = 0.833）。

F19 Q3.4（EDF；P1 p=d=10 t=4；P2 16/2；P3 20/8；到 38）：

  • 0 P1 4 P2 6 P3 14 P1 18 P2 20 P1 24 P3 32 P1 36 P2 38（t=10、t=30 deadline tie → keep P3 running）

12. Algorithm Evaluation [5.53–5.60]

  • 先定 criteria（e.g. maximize CPU utilization subject to max response time 300 ms；maximize throughput such that TAT ∝ execution time）。
  • Deterministic modeling — takes a particular predetermined workload and defines the performance of each algorithm for that workload；simple and fast, gives exact numbers, but requires exact input and answers apply only to those cases。
    • 例 [5.54–5.55]：5 processes at t=0：P1 10, P2 29, P3 3, P4 7, P5 12 → FCFS avg WT = 28；non-preemptive SJF = 13；RR (q=10) = 23。
  • Queueing models / analysis — CPU = server with ready queue；用 distributions（exponential, described by mean）；可計 utilization、average queue length、wait time；classes of algorithms and distributions handled are limited；assumptions often unrealistic。
    • ⭐ Little's Formula：n = λ × W（n = average queue length, λ = average arrival rate, W = average waiting time in queue）；valid for any scheduling algorithm and arrival distribution（steady state，arrival rate < service rate）。
    • 例：λ = 7/s，n = 14 → W = 2 s；HW2 MC10：λ = 3/s，n = 12 → W = 4 s。
  • Simulations — programming a model of the system；more accurate；data from random number generators (distributions) 或 trace files（real system 記錄）。
  • Implementation — 真系統測試，最準但 high cost & high risk；環境會變；可調 parameters；APIs 改 priority（Java, POSIX, Windows）。

13. Gantt Chart 萬用流程（考試步驟）✍️⭐⭐⭐

  1. 抄表：Process / Arrival / Burst（/ Priority）。
  2. 由 t = 0 開始逐個事件推進（events：arrival、completion、quantum expiry、check point）。每個事件寫低 ready queue（同 MLFQ 各層）。
  3. 揀 process 規則：
Algorithm 揀邊個 Preempt?
FCFS earliest arrival No
SJF shortest burst among arrived No
SRTF shortest remaining Yes, on arrival
RR head of queue Yes, quantum expiry
Priority (NP / P) highest priority P: on higher arrival
Priority + RR highest priority；same → RR Yes
MLFQ highest non-empty queue Yes（Q0 arrival preempts Q1/Q2）
RM shortest period Yes
EDF earliest absolute deadline Yes
  1. CPU idle：冇 process ready 就畫 idle（Sp21 FCFS 0 P1 2 idle 4 P2…）。
  2. 畫 Gantt：每段標 start time / process / end time；所有 boundary 時間都要寫（partial credit 靠呢個）。
  3. 計 table：Completion → TAT = C − A → WT = TAT − Burst → RT = end of first run − A。
  4. Average：寫出 (a+b+…)/n = x（S26：140/6 = 23.33）。
  5. Sanity check：總 burst 加 idle = 最後完成時間；每個 WT ≥ 0。

13.1 Tie-breaking（寫 assumption！）

  • 同時到達、同 burst/priority：FCFS → lower process ID。
  • SRTF：新到達 burst = running remaining → 一般 keep running（唔 preempt）；F19 官方 preempt 咗，但兩種 avg WT 一樣 2.5。
  • RR：新到達 + quantum expiry 同一時刻 → 新到達先入 queue。
  • EDF：deadline 相同 → keep running，否則 smaller ID（或者跟題目）。
  • Dynamic priority（S26 Q4.3）：題目講「same priority → larger ID first」— 跟題目！

14.2 S26 Q4.3 Dynamic priority（larger = higher；waiting rate α，running rate β；tie → larger ID）

  • P1 (0, 7), P2 (3, 5), P3 (6, 6), P4 (9, 4)
  • (a) α = 0, β = 3（running process priority 上升，waiting 不變 → 一旦 run 就霸住）：0 P1 7 P3 13 P4 17 P2 22
    • t=7：P2, P3 都係 0 → tie → larger ID → P3；t=13：P2 (0), P4 (0) → P4。
  • (b) α = 1, β = 0，只考慮 P1、P2，每 3 個 unit check：0 P1 3 P2 6 P1 9 P2 11 P1 12
    • t=3：P1 = 0 (ran 3), P2 = 0 → tie → P2；t=6：P1 = 3, P2 = 0 → P1；t=9：P1 = 3, P2 = 3 → tie → P2；t=11 P2 完 → P1。
  • (c) α = 1, β = −2，初始 30，每 3 unit check：0 P1 3 P2 6 P3 9 P4 12 P1 15 P2 17 P3 20 P4 21 P1 22
    • t=3：P1 = 30−6 = 24, P2 = 30 → P2；t=6：P1 27, P2 24, P3 30 → P3；t=9：P1 30, P2 27, P3 24, P4 30 → tie → P4；t=12：P1 33 → P1；t=15：P1 27, P2 33, P3 30, P4 27 → P2（17 完）；t=17：P1 29, P3 32, P4 29 → P3（20 完）；t=20：P1 32, P4 32 → tie → P4（21 完）；t=21：P1。

14.3 S26 Q2.3 自訂 policy（fᵢ = 1/(a·cᵢ + b)，揀 fᵢ 最大）

  • (a) Yes — I/O-bound processes have small average CPU bursts cᵢ → larger fᵢ → higher priority.
  • (b) Yes — a CPU-bound process (large cᵢ, low fᵢ) can starve if higher-priority I/O-bound processes keep arriving.

  • LUF vs SRTF：相似 — short jobs 較易有高 priority；分別 — LUF 令跑咗好耐嘅 process 易 starve；SRTF 令 long burst 嘅 process 一開始就 starve。

15. 常見陷阱 & MC 速查

敘述 真/假
Scheduling on running→waiting / terminate = non-preemptive ✔
Timer interrupt (quantum expiry) = preemptive ✔
FCFS is preemptive ✘（FCFS must be non-preemptive — Sp21 MC）
SJF gives minimum average waiting time ✔
SJF easy to implement ✘（need next burst length）
SRTF ≥ SJF in performance ✔
RR with very large q → FCFS ✔
RR always better TAT than FCFS ✘
RR: no process waits more than (n−1)q ✔
Small q → high context-switch overhead ✔
Priority scheduling problem = starvation；solution = aging ✔
Multilevel queue: processes move between queues ✘（MLFQ 先可以）
MLFQ requires known burst times ✘
MLFQ: each queue different algorithm ✔
Starvation-free: FCFS and RR ✔（Sp21 MC 答案 A：1, 4）
OS schedules user threads (SCS) ✘（PCS 由 thread library；SCS 係 kernel threads）
One-to-one systems only use SCS ✔
AMP: master can be a bottleneck ✔
Multicore simplifies scheduling ✘
Per-processor queues give affinity for free ✔
Load balancing may counteract affinity ✔
Hard RT: service after deadline acceptable ✘
RM: static priority by period ✔
EDF: dynamic priority by deadline ✔
EDF requires periodic tasks ✘
EDF optimal, up to 100% utilization (theory) ✔
Little's formula n = λW only for FCFS ✘（any algorithm）
Response time (course) = first start − arrival ✘（end of first run − arrival）
