6Ch6 Synchronization同步工具

📌 考試範圍提示：Midterm 包到 Ch6（Synchronization Tools）。每年都有 1 大題（8–25 分）+ 3–4 題 MC。題型：

  1. Race condition — 列出 shared variable 所有可能值（F19 Q4.3、F20 Q5：每個值 2.5 分！）
  2. Semaphore 次序控制填空（S26 Q5：wait/signal 填 blank）、semaphore 輸出序列判斷（F19 Q4.4）
  3. 用 atomic instruction 寫 spinlock（S26 Q2.4 swap；Sp21 Q3.1 compare_and_reset）
  4. Bounded-waiting test_and_set 算法分析 / 改錯（S26 MC25、Sp21 Q3.2）
  5. 定義：race condition、critical section 三個條件、spinlock 優缺點、binary semaphore vs mutex
  6. Pthread mutex + semaphore 程式輸出（S26 MC23）

1. Background（背景）[6.4–6.8]

  • Processes execute concurrently and may be interrupted at any time, partially completing execution.
  • Concurrent access to shared data may result in data inconsistency.
  • Maintaining data consistency requires mechanisms to ensure the orderly execution of cooperating processes.

1.1 Producer–Consumer with counter [6.5–6.6]

  • 用 counter 記 buffer 有幾多 item（可以用晒 BUFFER_SIZE 格）：
/* Producer */                          /* Consumer */
while (true) {                          while (true) {
  while (counter == BUFFER_SIZE) ;        while (counter == 0) ;
  buffer[in] = next_produced;             next_consumed = buffer[out];
  in = (in + 1) % BUFFER_SIZE;            out = (out + 1) % BUFFER_SIZE;
  counter++;                              counter--;
}                                       }
  • 問題：counter++ / counter-- 唔係 atomic。

1.2 Race Condition 例子 ⭐⭐ [6.7]

counter++  →  register1 = counter;  register1 = register1 + 1;  counter = register1
counter--  →  register2 = counter;  register2 = register2 - 1;  counter = register2

counter = 5 initially，interleaving：

Step Executed Result
S0 producer: register1 = counter register1 = 5
S1 producer: register1 = register1 + 1 register1 = 6
S2 consumer: register2 = counter register2 = 5
S3 consumer: register2 = register2 − 1 register2 = 4
S4 producer: counter = register1 counter = 6
S5 consumer: counter = register2 counter = 4（正確應該係 5！）
  • 調轉 S4、S5 → counter = 6。結果取決於執行次序 → race condition。
  • Kernel race example [6.8]：P0、P1 同時 fork() → race on kernel variable next_available_pid → same pid could be assigned to two different processes unless mutual exclusion。

2. Race Condition & Critical-Section Problem ⭐⭐⭐ [6.9–6.12]

2.1 Race condition 定義（S26 Q2.1 原題，4 分）

  • Sp21 MC：「several processes access and manipulate the same data concurrently and the outcome depends on the order」→ race condition。
  • 根本原因（summary）：high-level statements (count++) translate into multiple machine instructions, which can be interleaved arbitrarily.

📝 Model answer：A race condition is an undesirable situation where several processes (or threads) access and manipulate shared data concurrently, and the final outcome depends on the particular order (timing) in which the accesses take place. Because the execution order is unpredictable (e.g., context switches at untimely points), the result can be incorrect / non-deterministic. Example: two threads execute counter++ on the same shared counter without synchronization; each loads the old value into a register, increments it and stores it back, so one update can be lost and the final value is wrong (e.g., 5 + 1 − 1 gives 4 or 6 instead of 5).

2.2 Critical Section

  • Critical section：a segment of code in which a process may be changing shared variables, updating a table, writing a file, etc.
  • When one process is in its critical section, no other may be in its critical section.（mutual exclusion and critical section imply the same thing）
  • Critical-section problem：design a protocol that processes can use to cooperate.
  • General structure [6.10]：
do {
    entry section        /* request permission */
        critical section
    exit section
        remainder section
} while (true);

2.3 三個 requirements ⭐⭐⭐（F19 Q4.1 原題）[6.11]

  1. Mutual exclusion — if process Pᵢ is executing in its critical section, no other processes can be executing in their critical sections.
  2. Progress — if no process is executing in its critical section and some processes wish to enter, the selection of the process that will enter next cannot be postponed indefinitely（只有唔喺 remainder section 嘅 process 先參與決定）。
  3. Bounded waiting — a bound must exist on the number of times other processes are allowed to enter their critical sections after a process has made a request to enter its critical section and before that request is granted.（i.e. 等待時間有上限，no starvation）
  • Assumptions：each process executes at a nonzero speed；no assumption about the relative speed of the processes.
  • Summary：bounded waiting also implies progress.
  • Sp21 MC：CS solution 唔需要滿足 → synchronization（三個係 mutual exclusion, progress, bounded waiting）；mutual exclusion 定義 → A。

2.4 Critical sections in the kernel [6.12]

  • Kernel data structures（list of open files, memory allocation, process table, interrupt handling）都有 race condition。
  • Preemptive kernel — allows preemption of a process running in kernel mode；not free from race conditions，SMP 上更難；但 more responsive（適合 real-time）。
  • Non-preemptive kernel — runs until it exits kernel mode, blocks, or voluntarily yields → essentially free of race conditions in kernel mode（single-processor）。

3. Synchronization Hardware ⭐⭐ [6.13–6.21]

3.1 Disabling interrupts [6.13]

  • Uniprocessor：could simply disable interrupts → current code runs without preemption。
  • Generally inefficient on multiprocessor/SMP systems（要 disable 所有 CPU 嘅 interrupts，費時；影響 system clock）。
  • 層次：Hardware（load/store, disable interrupts, test&set, compare&swap）→ High-level APIs（locks, semaphores）→ programs。

3.2 Atomic instructions [6.14]

  • Atomic = non-interruptible；保證 instruction 唔會被中途打斷 → no race condition。
  • 即使兩個 CPU 同時執行，they are forced to execute sequentially (serialization)。
  • Building blocks for more sophisticated synchronization tools.
  • 兩個常用：test_and_set()（test a memory word and set a value）、compare_and_swap()（swap contents of two words）。
  • S26 MC24：commonly used hardware primitive → test_and_set()。

3.3 test_and_set ⭐⭐ [6.15–6.16]

boolean test_and_set(boolean *target) {   /* executed atomically */
    boolean rv = *target;
    *target = TRUE;
    return rv;                              /* returns the OLD value */
}
/* lock initialized to FALSE */
do {
    while (test_and_set(&lock)) ;   /* spin while old value was TRUE (lock held) */
        /* critical section */
    lock = false;                   /* release */
        /* remainder section */
} while (true);
  • 第一個 call 嘅 process 攞到 old = FALSE → 入 CS，同時 lock 變 TRUE；其他人攞到 TRUE → spin。
  • ✔ mutual exclusion；✘ bounded waiting（冇保證輪到邊個）。
  • Sp21 MC4（lock init 0）：entry section → while (test_and_set(&lock));（C）。✗ while (!test_and_set(&lock))。

3.4 compare_and_swap (CAS) ⭐⭐ [6.17–6.18]

int compare_and_swap(int *value, int expected, int new_value) {  /* atomic */
    int temp = *value;
    if (*value == expected)
        *value = new_value;
    return temp;                           /* returns the OLD value */
}
/* lock initialized to 0 */
do {
    while (compare_and_swap(&lock, 0, 1) != 0) ;   /* spin until old value was 0 */
        /* critical section */
    lock = 0;
        /* remainder section */
} while (true);
  • ⚠️ 留意 return 值：CAS 返回 old value → 用 != 0 判斷。Sp21 MC4 ✗ while (compare_and_swap(&lock, 0, 1) == 0)（相反）、✗ compare_and_swap(&lock, 1, 0) != 0。

3.5 Bounded-waiting Mutual Exclusion with test_and_set ⭐⭐⭐ [6.19–6.20]

/* shared: boolean waiting[n] (all false), boolean lock = false */
do {
    waiting[i] = true;
    key = true;
    while (waiting[i] && key)
        key = test_and_set(&lock);
    waiting[i] = false;
        /* critical section */
    j = (i + 1) % n;
    while ((j != i) && !waiting[j])        /* scan in cyclic order */
        j = (j + 1) % n;
    if (j == i)
        lock = false;          /* no one is waiting, so release the lock */
    else
        waiting[j] = false;    /* unblock process j (hand the lock over directly) */
        /* remainder section */
} while (true);

Sketch proof [6.20]：

  • Mutual exclusion：Pᵢ enters CS only if waiting[i] == false or key == false. key becomes false only via test_and_set() — only the first process to execute test_and_set finds key == false；others wait. waiting[i] becomes false only when another process leaves its CS, and only one waiting[i] is set to false → mutual exclusion。
  • Progress：a process exiting its CS either sets lock = false or sets waiting[j] = false — both let a waiting process proceed。
  • Bounded waiting：the exiting process scans waiting[] in cyclic order (i+1, i+2, …, n−1, 0, …, i−1) and designates the first waiting one → any waiting process enters within n − 1 turns。

⚠️ S26 MC25（which is NOT correct）→ A.「test_and_set(&lock) releases the lock after the critical section」（錯：釋放係 lock = false 或者 waiting[j] = false；test_and_set 只會 set lock 為 true）。✔ waiting[i] 表示 Pᵢ 想入 CS；✔ waiting[j] = false 表示 Pᵢ 將 lock 交畀 Pⱼ（lock 冇真正釋放）；✔ 執行 lock = false 後 Pᵢ 可以再入 CS。

📝 Sp21 Q3.2（改錯題）：版本用 j = i + 1; while (j < n && !waiting[j]) j++; if (j < n) waiting[j] = false; Problems：(1) 只 scan index 大過 i 嘅 process → index 細過 i 嘅 process 可能 unbounded waiting（violates bounded waiting）；(2) 冇 process 等嘅時候 lock 永遠唔會 release → 其他人之後永遠入唔到（violates progress / deadlock）。 Correction：改返 cyclic scan j = (i + 1) % n; while ((j != i) && !waiting[j]) j = (j + 1) % n; 同 if (j == i) lock = false; else waiting[j] = false;。

3.6 Atomic Variables [6.21]

  • Atomic variable：atomic (uninterruptible) updates on basic data types (integers, booleans)，用 CAS 實現：
void increment(atomic_int *v) {
    int temp;
    do {
        temp = *v;
    } while (temp != compare_and_swap(v, temp, temp + 1));   /* retry if someone changed v */
}

3.7 用其他 atomic instruction 寫 spinlock（考試題型）⭐⭐

S26 Q2.4 — swap(x, v)：atomically sets x = v and returns the old value of x（locked = 1 held / 0 free）：

while (swap(locked, 1) == 1) ;   /* acquire: spin while the old value was 1 */
/* critical section */
locked = 0;                       /* release (or swap(locked, 0)) */

Sp21 Q3.1 — compare_and_reset(int *a, int old, int new)：if (*a == old) { *a = new; return 1; } else return 0;

struct spinlock { int value = 0; };
void acquire(struct spinlock *lock) {
    while (compare_and_reset(&lock->value, 0, 1) == 0) ;   /* spin until we changed 0 → 1 */
}
void release(struct spinlock *lock) {
    lock->value = 0;
}

萬用套路：搵出「成功攞到 lock」時 instruction 嘅 return 值 → while (instruction(...) 表示失敗) ; → release 就 set 返 free value。

Instruction Acquire Release
test_and_set(&lock)（return old） while (test_and_set(&lock)) ; lock = false;
compare_and_swap(&lock, 0, 1)（return old） while (compare_and_swap(&lock, 0, 1) != 0) ; lock = 0;
swap(locked, 1)（return old） while (swap(locked, 1) == 1) ; locked = 0;
compare_and_reset(&v, 0, 1)（return 1 = success） while (compare_and_reset(&v, 0, 1) == 0) ; v = 0;
課本 swap(&lock, &key)（exchange） key = true; while (key == true) swap(&lock, &key); lock = false;

4. Mutex Locks ⭐⭐ [6.22–6.23]

  • Simplest software tool most OSes use.
  • Protect a critical region by first acquire() a lock, then release() it — both must be atomic（usually via hardware atomic instructions）。
  • Boolean variable available 表示 lock 有冇得用：
acquire() {
    while (!available) ;   /* busy wait */
    available = false;
}
release() {
    available = true;
}
do { acquire lock;  critical section;  release lock;  remainder section; } while (true);
  • This solution requires busy waiting → the lock is called a spinlock.
  • Wait if locked — 等耐咗應該 sleep / block 而唔係 spin。

4.1 Spinlock 優缺點 ⭐⭐⭐（S26 Q2.4b 原題）

  • 單 CPU 用 spinlock 冇乜意思（spin 緊嘅 thread 佔住 CPU，持有 lock 嘅 thread 跑唔到）。

📝 Model answer：Disadvantage: a spinlock wastes CPU cycles due to busy waiting (the waiting thread keeps looping). Advantage: no context switch is required when a process must wait on a lock, and a context switch may take considerable time. So spinlocks are useful when locks are expected to be held for short times, especially on multiprocessor systems, where one thread can spin on one processor while another thread executes its critical section on another processor.

5. Semaphores ⭐⭐⭐ [6.24–6.28]

5.1 定義 [6.24]

  • Semaphore S — a (non-negative) integer variable, a generalized lock；Dijkstra (late 1960s)；main synchronization primitive in original UNIX。
  • Accessed only through two atomic operations (apart from initialization)：wait() and signal()（originally P() = proberen, to test；V() = verhogen, to increment）。
  • Atomic → no more than one process can execute wait()/signal() on the same semaphore at the same time (serialization).
wait(S) {                  signal(S) {
    while (S <= 0) ;           S++;
    S--;                   }
}

5.2 Counting vs Binary [6.25] ⭐

  • Counting semaphore — value ranges over an unrestricted domain；control access to a resource with a finite number of instances（initialize to the number of available resources）；wait = 攞一個，signal = 還一個。
  • Binary semaphore — value only 0 or 1；can behave like a mutex lock，但亦可以用嚟做其他同步。
  • Ordering example（S1 喺 S2 之前執行）：synch = 0
P1:  S1;  signal(synch);          P2:  wait(synch);  S2;

📝 F19 Q4.2：Binary semaphore = mutex lock? → No. A binary semaphore initialized to 1 can be used as a mutex lock, but a binary semaphore can also be used for other synchronization purposes, e.g., initialized to 0 to enforce execution order / signaling between processes. (Also a mutex lock is owned — the thread that locks it must unlock it — whereas any process can signal a semaphore.)

5.3 Implementation with no busy waiting ⭐⭐ [6.26–6.27]

  • Each semaphore has a waiting queue（each entry：value + pointer to next record）。
  • block() — place the invoking process on the waiting queue（suspend）；wakeup(P) — remove a process from the waiting queue and put it in the ready queue。
typedef struct { int value; struct process *list; } semaphore;
wait(semaphore *S) {
    S->value--;
    if (S->value < 0) { add this process to S->list; block(); }
}
signal(semaphore *S) {
    S->value++;
    if (S->value <= 0) { remove a process P from S->list; wakeup(P); }
}
  • ⭐ Increment/decrement is done BEFORE checking the value（同 busy-waiting 版相反）。
  • ⭐ Semaphore value may become negative；if negative, its magnitude = number of processes waiting on it.（busy-waiting classical 版永遠唔會 negative）

5.4 Deadlock and Starvation ⭐ [6.28]

  • Deadlock — two or more processes waiting indefinitely for an event that can be caused only by one of the waiting processes.
S = Q = 1
P0: wait(S); wait(Q); ... signal(S); signal(Q);
P1: wait(Q); wait(S); ... signal(Q); signal(S);

P0 wait(S)、P1 wait(Q) 之後：P0 等 Q（要 P1 signal），P1 等 S（要 P0 signal）→ deadlock（extremely difficult to debug）。

  • Starvation (indefinite blocking) — a process may never be removed from the semaphore queue，例如用 LIFO order 或者按 priority 移除 queue 入面嘅 processes。

6. 做題方法 ✍️⭐⭐⭐

6.1 Race condition 所有可能值（F19 Q4.3、F20 Q5）

步驟：

  1. 將每個 thread 拆成 atomic reads/writes（c = b + a → read b, read a, write c；if 條件嘅 read 係另一個 read！）。
  2. 另一個 thread 嘅 writes 可以插喺任何 read 之間（次序保持）。
  3. 按 「條件 read 嗰陣見到嘅值」分 branch，再按「每個 read 見到新值定舊值」分 sub-case。
  4. 列出所有 distinct 值（唔好漏！每個值獨立計分）。

F19 Q4.3：a = 7, b = 2, c = 5；T1：if (a < 0) c = b - a; else c = b + a;；T2：b = 8; a = -6;

Case T1 見到 c
T2 全部喺 T1 之前 → a<0 branch b = 8, a = −6 8 − (−6) = 14
else branch；T2 喺 c 計之前完成 b = 8, a = −6 8 + (−6) = 2
else；T2 喺 T1 之後 b = 2, a = 7 9
else；write b 喺 read b 前，write a 喺 read a 後 b = 8, a = 7 15
else；read b 喺 write b 前，write a 喺 read a 前 b = 2, a = −6 −4

→ {14, 2, 9, 15, −4}

F20 Q5：a = 7, b = 2；T1：if (b < 0) c = b - a; else c = b + a;；T2：b = -8; a = 6;

  • b<0 branch（write b 喺條件 read 之前）：a 新 → −8 − 6 = −14；a 舊 → −8 − 7 = −15
  • else branch，第二次 read b 見到新值（−8）：a 新 → −8 + 6 = −2；a 舊 → −8 + 7 = −1
  • else branch，第二次 read b 見到舊值（2）：a 新 → 2 + 6 = 8；a 舊 → 2 + 7 = 9 → {−14, −15, −2, −1, 8, 9}

6.2 Semaphore 次序（dependency）題（S26 Q5）

規則：「X 必須喺 Y 之前完成」→ 用一個 init 0 嘅 semaphore s：X 完成後 signal(s)；Y 開始前 wait(s)。每個 dependency edge 一個 semaphore。

S26 Q5：A depends on B and C；B depends on C（即 C → B → A，且 C → A）；s0, s1, s2 全部 init 0。

workerA: /*BLANK1*/ wait(&s2);   wait(&s1);  printf("A");          // 等 B (s2) 同 C (s1)
workerB: /*BLANK2*/ wait(&s0);   printf("B");  signal(&s2);        // 等 C (s0)，完成通知 A
workerC: /*BLANK3*/ /* Nothing */ printf("C"); /*BLANK4*/ signal(&s0); signal(&s1);
  • BLANK1 = wait(&s2)，BLANK2 = wait(&s0)，BLANK3 = /* Nothing */，BLANK4 = signal(&s0) → 輸出一定係 C B A。

6.3 Semaphore 輸出序列題（F19 Q4.4）

semaphore L = 3, R = 0;
P1: while (true) { wait(L); putc('C'); signal(R); }
P2: while (true) { wait(R); putc('A'); putc('B'); signal(R); }
P3: while (true) { wait(R); putc('D'); }

方法：數 token。L = 3 → P1 最多印 3 個 C（冇人 signal L）。每個 C 產生 1 個 R token；P2 借 token 印 AB 後還返；P3 食咗 token 唔還。

  • (a) D 數目 = R token 總數 = 3
  • (b) A：最少 0（P3 搶晒 token），最多 infinite（P2 無限借還）
  • (c) CABABDCDABCABD → No：印到 C A B A B D C D 時 C 印咗 2 次、D 食咗 2 個 token → R = 0，下一個 A（P2 需要 wait(R)）冇 token，要等第 3 個 C 先得。
  • (d) CABACDBCABDD → Yes：C(R=1) A(P2 借→0) B(還→1) A(借→0) C(→1) D(食→0) B(還→1) C(→2) A(借→1) B(還→2) D(→1) D(→0) ✓
  • 技巧：逐個字元追蹤 semaphore 值同每個 process「做到邊」；任何時刻 token 唔可以 < 0。

6.4 Pthread 程式輸出題（S26 MC23）

int counter = 0; pthread_mutex_t mtx; sem_t sem;   /* sem_init(&sem, 0, 0) */
worker1: lock; counter += 2; printf("A %d;", counter); unlock; sem_post(&sem);
worker2: sem_wait(&sem); lock; counter += 3; printf("B %d;", counter); unlock;
  • sem 初始 0 → worker2 一定等 worker1 post → 輸出 A 2; B 5;（答案 A）。mutex 保證 counter 更新互斥。

7. 課本補充（slides 冇，但可能用到）

7.1 Peterson's Solution（two processes, software-only）

int turn; boolean flag[2];          /* Pi uses j = 1 - i */
do {
    flag[i] = true;  turn = j;
    while (flag[j] && turn == j) ;
        /* critical section */
    flag[i] = false;
        /* remainder section */
} while (true);
  • 滿足 mutual exclusion、progress、bounded waiting（兩個 process）；但 modern architectures may reorder instructions → not guaranteed to work without memory barriers。

7.2 Classic problems with semaphores（Ch7 預覽）

  • Bounded buffer：mutex = 1, full = 0, empty = n
Producer: wait(empty); wait(mutex); add item; signal(mutex); signal(full);
Consumer: wait(full);  wait(mutex); remove item; signal(mutex); signal(empty);
  • ⚠️ 調轉 wait(mutex) 同 wait(empty) 次序 → 可能 deadlock。
  • First readers–writers：rw_mutex = 1, mutex = 1, read_count = 0；第一個 reader wait(rw_mutex)，最後一個 reader signal(rw_mutex)；writers 可能 starve。
  • Condition variables (POSIX)：pthread_cond_wait(&cond, &mutex)（必須持有 mutex；wait 時釋放 mutex 並 sleep，醒後重新 acquire）；pthread_cond_signal(&cond)。

8. 常見陷阱 & MC 速查

敘述 真/假
Race condition: outcome depends on order of access ✔
counter++ is a single atomic machine instruction ✘
CS requirements: mutual exclusion, progress, bounded waiting ✔
"Synchronization" is one of the 3 CS requirements ✘
Bounded waiting implies progress ✔（summary）
Disabling interrupts works well on multiprocessors ✘（only uniprocessor）
Atomic = non-interruptible ✔
test_and_set returns the new value ✘（old value）
CAS returns old value ✔
Simple TAS lock satisfies bounded waiting ✘
Bounded-waiting TAS: waiting process enters within n−1 turns ✔
test_and_set releases the lock ✘
Spinlock wastes CPU but avoids context switch ✔
Spinlocks useful on multiprocessors for short critical sections ✔
Semaphore accessed only by wait/signal (except init) ✔
P() = wait, V() = signal ✔
Binary semaphore = mutex lock (equivalent) ✘（can be used as one, but more general）
Counting semaphore initialized to # of resources ✔
Blocking semaphore value can be negative = # waiting ✔
Non-preemptive kernel essentially free of race conditions (uniprocessor) ✔
LIFO semaphore queue may cause starvation ✔
Wrong order of wait() on two semaphores may deadlock ✔

10. 自我測驗

  1. 寫出 CS 三個 requirements 嘅定義（英文）。
  2. 用 test_and_set 寫 lock；點解唔滿足 bounded waiting？
  3. 一個 semaphore value = −3 代表咩？（3 個 processes 喺 waiting queue）
  4. x = 0；T1：x = x + 1；T2：x = x * 2（每句 read/write 分開）→ 可能值？（2: T1→T2；1: T2→T1；1: T1 read 0, T2 read 0 & write 0, T1 write 1；0: T2 read 0, T1 write 1, T2 write 0 → {0, 1, 2}）
  5. 三個 threads 要 A → B → C 次序，用最少 semaphores 寫出來。（s1 = s2 = 0；A; signal(s1) | wait(s1); B; signal(s2) | wait(s2); C）

QPast Papers Solved歷屆長題全解

Spring 2026 Midterm（lecturer 提供）

S26-Q2.1 Race condition (4 pts) CH6

What is a race condition? Give an example.

Answer.

A race condition occurs when two or more processes/threads access and manipulate shared data concurrently, and the final result depends on the order in which the accesses take place; since the order is unpredictable, the result may be incorrect or inconsistent. (2.5) Example: two threads increment the same counter without synchronization — counter++ is load/add/store, so both may load the same old value and one update is lost. (1.5)

S26-Q2.2 Caching (4 pts) CH1

What is the fundamental idea of caching? Is a high cache hit ratio alone enough to guarantee good performance? Explain by considering hit time and miss penalty.

Answer.

Caching temporarily copies a subset of information from a slower but larger storage into a faster but smaller storage so that frequently used data can be accessed more quickly. (2) No — hit ratio is a major criterion but not sufficient (1); performance is the average access time = hit ratio × hit time + miss ratio × miss time, which also depends on the hit time and the miss penalty (1). A slow cache or a huge miss penalty can still give poor performance.

S26-Q2.3 Custom policy fᵢ = 1/(a·cᵢ + b) (4 pts) CH5

The OS keeps cᵢ = average past CPU burst of Pᵢ and picks the process with the highest fᵢ = 1/(a·cᵢ + b) (a, b > 0), running it for one quantum or until it gives up the CPU. (a) Does this prioritize I/O-bound over CPU-bound processes? (b) Can it cause starvation?

Answer.

(a) Yes — I/O-bound processes have small CPU bursts cᵢ, hence a larger fᵢ and higher priority. (b) Yes — a CPU-bound process (large cᵢ, low fᵢ) can starve if higher-priority I/O-bound processes keep arriving.

S26-Q2.4 swap spinlock (4 pts) CH6

swap(x, v) atomically sets x to v and returns the old value of x. locked = 1 when held, 0 otherwise. (a) Write the line that acquires the spinlock with a busy-spin loop. (b) Advantage and disadvantage of a spinlock.

Answer.

(a) while (swap(locked, 1) == 1); (release: locked = 0;) (b) Disadvantage: wastes CPU cycles due to busy waiting. Advantage: no context switch is needed when a process must wait on a lock (a context switch can take considerable time) — good for short critical sections on multiprocessors.

S26-Q3.1 fork tree with break (8 pts) CH3
for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    if (fork()) fork();
    else break;
}

(a) n = 2: draw the process tree; total processes and number of breaks. (b) Relationship between total processes and n.

Answer.

(a) 7 processes, 3 breaks. Tree: P0 → P1 (break), P2, P3 (break), P4; P2 → P5 (break), P6. (b) Each active process per iteration creates 2 children: the first breaks, the second keeps looping → active count doubles: Aₖ = 2ᵏ. After n iterations: active = 2ⁿ, stopped = 1 + 2 + … + 2ⁿ⁻¹ = 2ⁿ − 1. Total = 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 1 (breaks = 2ⁿ − 1).

S26-Q3.2 fork + exec scenarios (8 pts) CH3
int ret = fork();
if (ret == 0) { printf("Hello1\n"); exec("some_executable"); printf("Hello2\n"); }
else if (ret > 0) { wait(NULL); printf("Hello3\n"); }
else { printf("Hello4\n"); }

List all possible scenarios and outputs.

Answer.
  • Case I — fork succeeds, exec succeeds: Hello1, Hello3 (exec replaces the image; Hello2 never runs).
  • Case II — fork succeeds, exec fails (returns −1): Hello1, Hello2, Hello3.
  • Case III — fork fails: Hello4. (Hello3 always last because the parent waits.)
S26-Q3.3 pipe + dup fill-in (8 pts) CH3

Child counts even numbers in 1.txt, parent in 2.txt (file: length then elements, read with scanf); child sends its count via pipe; parent prints the total.

Answer.
  • Blank 1: close(0) · Blank 2: dup(fin) (stdin now refers to the file)
  • Blank 3: if (number % 2 == 0) cnt++
  • Blank 4.1: fd[1] · 4.2: &cnt (child: write(fd[1], &cnt, sizeof(int)))
  • Blank 5.1: fd[0] · 5.2: &child_cnt (parent: read(fd[0], &child_cnt, sizeof(int)))
S26-Q4.1 MLFQ Q0 RR4 / Q1 RR8 / Q2 FCFS (8 pts) CH5

P1 (0, 9), P2 (2, 25), P3 (6, 7), P4 (14, 18), P5 (45, 15), P6 (58, 6). New jobs → Q0; full quantum → demote; Q0 arrival preempts Q1/Q2 immediately; preempted job → head of its queue; no demotion when preempted.

Answer.

Gantt: 0 P1 4 P2 8 P3 12 P1 14 P4 18 P1 21 P2 29 P3 32 P4 40 P2 45 P5 57 P2 58 P6 64 P2 71 P4 77 P5 80 (P5 45–49 Q0 then 49–57 Q1; P6 58–62 Q0 then 62–64 Q1). Waiting = completion − arrival − burst: P1 21−0−9 = 12; P2 71−2−25 = 44; P3 32−6−7 = 19; P4 77−14−18 = 45; P5 80−45−15 = 20; P6 64−58−6 = 0 → avg = 140/6 = 23.33. Response (end of first run − arrival): 4, 6, 6, 4, 4 (49−45), 4 (62−58) → avg = 28/6 = 4.67 (6.33 also accepted if P5/P6 merged).

S26-Q4.2 RM / EDF with D < T (10 pts) CH5

(t, d, p): P1 (1, 3, 4), P2 (2, 4, 5), P3 (2, 6, 7), P4 (1, 5, 8); all arrive at 0; draw 0–16; ties → smaller ID; count missed deadlines.

Answer.
  • RM (P1 > P2 > P3 > P4 by period): 0 P1 1 P2 3 P3 4 P1 5 P2 7 P3 8 P1 9 P3 10 P2 12 P1 13 P3 14 P3 15 P2 16 → missed: P3 (d 6, finished 8), P4 (d 5, never), P3 (d 13, finished 14), P4 (d 13, never) → 4 missed. (U = 1.06 > 1.)
  • EDF: 0 P1 1 P2 3 P4 4 P3 6 P1 7 P2 9 P1 10 P3 12 P4 13 P2 15 P1 16 → missed: P2 (d 14, finished 15), P1 (d 15, finished 16) → 2 missed.
S26-Q4.3 Dynamic priority (9 pts) CH5

Larger value = higher priority; waiting changes priority at rate α, running at rate β; tie → larger ID. P1 (0, 7), P2 (3, 5), P3 (6, 6), P4 (9, 4).

Answer.
  • (a) α = 0, β = 3, initial 0: 0 P1 7 P3 13 P4 17 P2 22
  • (b) α = 1, β = 0, only P1, P2, check every 3: 0 P1 3 P2 6 P1 9 P2 11 P1 12
  • (c) α = 1, β = −2, initial 30, check every 3: 0 P1 3 P2 6 P3 9 P4 12 P1 15 P2 17 P3 20 P4 21 P1 22
S26-Q5 Semaphore ordering (8 pts) CH6

A depends on B and C; B depends on C. s0, s1, s2 initialised to 0. workerA: BLANK1; wait(&s1); print A. workerB: BLANK2; print B; signal(&s2). workerC: BLANK3; print C; BLANK4; signal(&s1).

Answer.

BLANK1 wait(&s2) · BLANK2 wait(&s0) · BLANK3 /* Nothing */ · BLANK4 signal(&s0) → order always C, B, A.

Fall 2019 Midterm

F19-Q1.1 Linker and loader (2 pts) CH2

What do the linker and loader do, respectively?

Answer.

The linker combines relocatable object files (and libraries) into a single binary executable file; the loader loads the binary executable file into memory so it can run.

F19-Q1.2 Locality (3 pts) CH1

What are temporal locality and spatial locality?

Answer.

Temporal locality: a memory location accessed recently is likely to be accessed again in the near future. Spatial locality: locations near a recently accessed location are likely to be accessed soon.

F19-Q1.3 Why separate API and system calls (2 pts) CH2

Two reasons for separating APIs from the underlying system calls?

Answer.

(1) Program portability — the API stays the same across platforms. (2) Hide the complex details of system calls from programmers.

F19-Q1.4 LKM vs layered (3 pts) CH2

Similarity and difference between the loadable-kernel-module approach and the layered approach?

Answer.

Similarity: functionality is divided into modules, each with a well-defined interface. Difference: LKM is more flexible — any module can call any other module (no higher/lower layer restriction), and modules can be loaded/removed at run time.

F19-Q2.1 State transitions (3 pts) CH3

Fill in the actions for: running→ready, ready→running, running→waiting, waiting→ready.

Answer.

running→ready: interrupt; ready→running: scheduler dispatch (CPU scheduling); running→waiting: I/O or event wait; waiting→ready: I/O or event completion.

F19-Q2.2 Socket (2 pts) CH3

Four pieces of information that uniquely define a socket connection?

Answer.

Source IP address, source port number, destination IP address, destination port number.

F19-Q2.3 Thread unique vs shared (3 pts) CH4

Three elements unique to each thread and three shared.

Answer.

Unique: program counter, registers (state), stack (thread ID). Shared: code, data (globals/heap), open files.

F19-Q2.4 Shell system calls (3 pts) CH3

Two system calls a shell must execute to start a new process on UNIX?

Answer.

fork() — clones the calling process (creates a child); exec() — overlays/replaces the child's address space with a new program (loads the program to run).

F19-Q2.5 Multicore vs SMP, CMT (4 pts) CH5

Two advantages of multicore vs conventional SMP; reason for chip multithreading (hyper-threading).

Answer.

Multicore is faster (on-chip communication) and consumes less power than separate single-core chips. CMT exploits memory stall: processors are much faster than memory, so a core may spend up to ~50% of its time waiting for data; with multiple hardware threads the core switches to another thread while one waits.

F19-Q2.6 Lines printed vs n (5 pts) CH3
for (i = 0; i < n; ++i) { if (fork()) printf("Fork!\n"); else printf("Fork!\n"); }
Answer.

Each iteration doubles the processes and every process prints one line: iteration i prints 2ⁱ lines → total 2 + 4 + … + 2ⁿ = 2ⁿ⁺¹ − 2 ✔

F19-Q2.7 fork()==fork() || fork()!=fork() (5 pts) CH3
if (fork() == fork() || fork() != fork()) fork();
printf("Fork!\n");
Answer.

23. After fork()==fork() there are 4 processes; only the one that got 0 twice is true → it skips the rest of || and forks → 2 lines. The other 3 run fork()!=fork() → 12 processes; 9 are true and fork again → 18; 3 false → 3. Total 2 + 18 + 3 = 23 ✔

F19-Q3.1 Which threads does the OS schedule (4 pts) CH5

Which threads are scheduled by the OS? How does mapping user threads onto kernel threads/LWPs differ from CPU scheduling?

Answer.

The OS schedules kernel-level threads. Scheduling user threads onto LWPs/kernel threads (PCS) is done by the thread library, usually by programmer-set priority, among threads of the same process; CPU scheduling (SCS) chooses which kernel thread runs on a CPU among all kernel threads in the system.

F19-Q3.2 FCFS problem; RR worse than FCFS (3 pts) CH5
Answer.

FCFS: convoy effect — short processes stuck behind a long one. RR has worse turnaround than FCFS when all processes have the same CPU burst length that is larger than the quantum (they all finish near the end).

F19-Q3.3 MLFQ approximates SJF (3 pts) CH5
Answer.

Short interactive jobs finish in the top (high-priority) queue, while long CPU-bound jobs sink to lower queues — so short jobs are served first, like SJF. Advantage: no prior knowledge of CPU burst times is needed.

F19-Q3.4 EDF to t = 38 (6 pts) CH5

P1 (period = deadline 10, t 4), P2 (16, 2), P3 (20, 8).

Answer.

0 P1 4 P2 6 P3 14 P1 18 P2 20 P1 24 P3 32 P1 36 P2 38 (deadline ties at t = 10 and 30 → the running P3 continues). No deadline missed.

F19-Q3.5 Least Used First (12 pts) CH5

LUF picks the process that has used the fewest CPU cycles; tie → FCFS; preempted process goes to the end of the queue; scheduler runs on completion and on new arrival. P1 (0, 25), P2 (15, 25), P3 (25, 5), P4 (40, 5).

Answer.

(a) 0 P1 15 P2 25 P3 30 P2 40 P4 45 P1 55 P2 60 (b) TAT / RT / WT: P1 55 / 15 / 30; P2 45 / 10 / 20; P3 5 / 5 / 0; P4 5 / 5 / 0. (c) Similar to SRTF: short jobs tend to get high priority. Different: under LUF a process that has already run a long time is likely to starve; under SRTF a process with a long burst is likely to starve from the beginning.

F19-Q3.6 FCFS / SJF / RR4 / SRTF (12 pts) CH5

P1 (0, 8), P2 (7, 13), P3 (9, 1), P4 (11, 5), P5 (20, 7), P6 (27, 1).

Answer.
  • FCFS 0 P1 8 P2 21 P3 22 P4 27 P5 34 P6 35 — WT 0,1,12,11,7,7 → 6.33
  • SJF 0 P1 8 P2 21 P3 22 P4 27 P6 28 P5 35 — WT 0,1,12,11,8,0 → 5.33
  • RR q=4 0 P1 8 P2 12 P3 13 P4 17 P2 21 P4 22 P5 26 P2 30 P5 33 P6 34 P2 35 — WT 0,15,3,6,6,6 → 6.0 (corrected official)
  • SRTF 0 P1 8 P2 9 P3 10 P2 11 P4 16 P2 20 P5 27 P6 28 P2 35 — WT 0,15,0,0,0,0 → 2.5 (official; keeping P2 on the tie at t=20 also gives 2.5)
F19-Q4.1 CS requirements (4 pts) CH6
Answer.

(1) Mutual exclusion — if a process is executing in its CS, no other process can be in its CS. (2) Progress — if no process is in its CS and some want to enter, the selection of the next one cannot be postponed indefinitely. (3) Bounded waiting — there is a bound on the number of times other processes may enter their CS after a process has requested entry and before that request is granted.

F19-Q4.2 Binary semaphore vs mutex (3 pts) CH6
Answer.

No. A binary semaphore initialised to 1 can be used as a mutex lock, but it can also be used for other synchronization purposes (e.g., initialised to 0 for ordering/signalling).

F19-Q4.3 Possible values of c (8 pts) CH6

a = 7, b = 2, c = 5. T1: if (a < 0) c = b - a; else c = b + a; T2: b = 8; a = -6;

Answer.

14, 2, 9, 15, −4 — (a<0 branch: 8−(−6) = 14); else-branch: T2 done before c computed: 8+(−6) = 2; T2 after: 2+7 = 9; b written before read but a after: 8+7 = 15; b read before write, a written before read: 2+(−6) = −4.

F19-Q4.4 Semaphores L = 3, R = 0 with putc (10 pts) CH6

P1: wait(L); putc('C'); signal(R). P2: wait(R); putc('A'); putc('B'); signal(R). P3: wait(R); putc('D').

Answer.

(a) 3 D's. (b) A's: min 0, max infinite. (c) CABABDCDABCABD — No (after "CABABDCD", R = 0, so P2 cannot print the next A before a third C). (d) CABACDBCABDD — Yes.

Fall 2020 Midterm

F20-Q2.1 Microkernel (4 pts) CH2
Answer.

Main idea: remove all nonessential components from the kernel and implement them as system/user-level programs in separate address spaces; communicate by message passing. Advantage: small kernel, easier to extend and port, more secure and reliable. Disadvantage: performance suffers due to increased system-function overhead (user↔kernel message passing).

F20-Q2.2 Linker and loader (4 pts) CH2
Answer.

Linker: combines several relocatable object modules into a single binary executable image (stored on disk). Loader: loads the executable into memory for execution.

F20-Q2.3 Heavyweight vs lightweight (4 pts) CH4
Answer.

A process is heavyweight because it owns an address space and resources; creating one duplicates program and data. A thread is lightweight — only an execution unit (PC, registers, stack) that shares code, data and files with other threads of its process, so creating it needs no duplication.

F20-Q2.4 Common vs per-processor ready queue (4 pts) CH5
Answer.

Common queue: easy load balancing (no CPU idle while the queue is non-empty) but hard to keep processor affinity (and needs locking). Per-processor queues: natural processor affinity but need push/pull migration for load balancing.

F20-Q2.5 MLFQ benefits (4 pts) CH5
Answer.

(1) Resembles SJF/SRTF performance without prior knowledge of the next CPU burst (2 pts); (2) RR-like good response time for interactive jobs (1); (3) fair — all processes make progress (1).

F20-Q3.1 Possible outputs (10 pts) CH3
if (fork() == 0) {
    if (fork() > 0) { printf("1"); wait(NULL); printf("2"); }
    else printf("3");
} else printf("4");
Answer.

Unbuffered: 8 outputs — "2" after "1" and "3"; "4" anywhere: 1342, 1432, 3142, 4132, 3412, 4312, 1324, 3124. Buffered ("1" and "2" flushed together): 3 outputs — 4312, 3412, 3124.

F20-Q3.2 Count "a" (13 pts) CH3
if (!fork()) { for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) if (fork()) fork(); }
fork(); printf("a");
Answer.

First fork: the child enters the loop; each iteration every process becomes 3 → 3ⁿ processes from the child branch. Plus the original → 3ⁿ + 1. The final fork doubles → 2(3ⁿ + 1) "a"s ✔

F20-Q4.1 FCFS / SJF / SRTF (9 pts) CH5

P1 (0, 9), P2 (3, 3), P3 (8, 2), P4 (10, 6), P5 (16, 8), P6 (20, 5).

Answer.
  • FCFS 0 P1 9 P2 12 P3 14 P4 20 P5 28 P6 33 → (0+6+4+4+4+8)/6 = 4.33
  • SJF 0 P1 9 P3 11 P2 14 P4 20 P6 25 P5 33 → (0+8+1+4+9+0)/6 = 3.67
  • SRTF 0 P1 3 P2 6 P1 8 P3 10 P1 14 P4 20 P6 25 P5 33 → (5+0+0+4+9+0)/6 = 3.0
F20-Q4.2 Two-level feedback queue with aging (10 pts) CH5

Q0 RR 4 ms (high), Q1 FCFS; new → Q0; Q0 arrival preempts Q1; preempted Q1 process → head of Q1 with timer reset; a Q1 process whose timer reaches 8 ms rejoins Q0. P1 (0, 9), P2 (3, 3), P3 (8, 2), P4 (10, 9), P5 (13, 4), P6 (18, 5).

Answer.

0 P1 4 P2 7 P1 8 P3 10 P4 14 P5 18 P1 22 P6 26 P4 30 P6 31 P4 32 → WT (13+1+0+13+1+8)/6 = 6. (P1 ages back to Q0 at 16; P4 at 22.)

F20-Q4.3 Priority + RR (8 pts) CH5

Smaller number = higher priority; preemptive; same priority → RR q = 4. P1 (0, 9, 2), P2 (3, 3, 1), P3 (8, 5, 4), P4 (10, 2, 4), P5 (16, 8, 5), P6 (20, 5, 3).

Answer.

0 P1 3 P2 6 P1 12 P3 16 P4 18 P3 19 P5 20 P6 25 P5 32 → (3+0+6+6+8+0)/6 = 3.83

F20-Q5 Possible values of c (15 pts) CH6

a = 7, b = 2. T1: if (b < 0) c = b - a; else c = b + a; T2: b = -8; a = 6;

Answer.

−14, −15, −2, −1, 8, 9 (2.5 marks each).

Spring 2021 Midterm

Sp21-Q1.1 pid1/pid2 (5 pts) CH3
pid1 = fork(); pid2 = fork();
if (pid1 > 0) if (pid2 == 0) fork();
Answer.

5 — 4 processes after two forks; only the child created by the parent's second fork has pid1 > 0 and pid2 == 0, so one more process ✔

Sp21-Q1.2 Nested loop (5 pts) CH3
if (fork() == 0) { for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) if (fork() == 0) fork(); }
fork();
Answer.

(3⁵ + 1) × 2 = 488 ✔

Sp21-Q1.3 Output orderings (8 pts) CH3
printf("A\n");
if (fork() == 0) printf("B\n");
else { printf("C\n"); ___A___ }
printf("D\n");
Answer.

(a) empty: ABDCD, ABCDD, ACBDD, ACDBD. (b) wait(NULL): ABDCD, ABCDD, ACBDD. (c) printf("E\n"): ABDCED, ABCDED, ABCEDD, ACBDED, ACBEDD, ACEBDD, ACEDBD.

Sp21-Q2.1 FCFS / SJF / RR8 / SRTF (10 pts) CH5

P1 (0, 2), P2 (4, 12), P3 (6, 6), P4 (9, 4), P5 (13, 5).

Answer.

FCFS 7.2 · SJF 6.6 · RR(8) 7.6 · SRTF 4.2 (Gantt charts in Ch5 §14.9; note the idle gap 2–4).

Sp21-Q2.2 MLFQ q=2, q=4, FCFS (8 pts) CH5

P1 (0, 10), P2 (10, 8), P3 (8, 30), P4 (16, 2), P5 (22, 8), P6 (6, 22).

Answer.

0 P1 2 P1 6 P6 8 P3 10 P2 12 P6 16 P4 18 P3 22 P5 24 P2 28 P5 32 P1 36 P6 52 P3 76 P2 78 P5 80 → WT 26, 60, 38, 0, 50, 24 → 33.

Sp21-Q2.3 EDF vs RM (8 pts) CH5

P1 (1, 3, 3), P2 (2, 4, 4), 0–14, soft real-time.

Answer.

EDF 0 P1 1 P2 3 P1 4 P2 6 P1 7 idle 8 P2 10 P1 11 idle 12 P1 13 P2 14; RM 0 P1 1 P2 3 P1 4 P2 6 P1 7 idle 8 P2 9 P1 10 P2 11 idle 12 P1 13 P2 14. EDF is better: dynamic deadline-based priorities can meet more deadlines (up to 100% utilization) than RM's static priorities.

Sp21-Q3.1 compare_and_reset spinlock (4 pts) CH6
Answer.

struct spinlock { int value = 0; }; · acquire: while (compare_and_reset(&lock->value, 0, 1) == 0) ; · release: lock->value = 0;

Sp21-Q3.2 Fix the bounded-waiting algorithm (7 pts) CH6
Answer.

Problems: (1) only processes with index > i are checked → lower-index processes can wait unboundedly; (2) the lock is never released when nobody is waiting. Fix: cyclic scan j = (i + 1) % n; while ((j != i) && !waiting[j]) j = (j + 1) % n; if (j == i) lock = 0; else waiting[j] = false;

Homework 1（Fall 2026）

HW1-Q2.1 AMAT + locality (6 pts) CH1

Hit ratio 90%, hit time 2 ns, miss time 50 ns.

Answer.

0.9 × 2 + 0.1 × 50 = 6.8 ns. Temporal: recently accessed items are likely accessed again (e.g., loop counter, loop body instructions). Spatial: items near recent accesses are likely accessed soon (e.g., sequential array elements a[i], a[i+1]).

HW1-Q2.2 Multiprogramming vs time sharing (6 pts) CH1
Answer.

Multiprogramming keeps several jobs in memory; when one waits for I/O, the CPU switches to another, so the CPU always has work → higher utilization. Time sharing switches among jobs so frequently (timer + CPU scheduling) that users can interact with each program while it runs → short response time, interactive computing.

HW1-Q2.3 State sequence (6 pts) CH3
Answer.

New → Ready → Running → Waiting → Ready → Running → Ready → Running → Terminated. When leaving Running, the PCB saves the process state, program counter, CPU registers, plus updated scheduling, accounting and I/O-status information.

HW1-Q2.4 read(): user → kernel → user (6 pts) CH2
Answer.

(1) read() in libc puts the system-call number and parameters in registers and executes a trap instruction. (2) The trap switches to kernel mode; the kernel indexes the system-call table and runs the read service. (3) The kernel stores the return value and executes return-from-trap, which switches back to user mode and resumes after the call.

HW1-Q2.5 Parent → child through a pipe (6 pts) CH3
Answer.

Call pipe(fd) before fork(); after fork, the parent (writer) closes fd[0] and writes to fd[1]; the child (reader) closes fd[1] and reads from fd[0]; close the used ends when done.

HW1-Q3.1 Values of x (15 pts) CH3
Answer.

C1: 3, C2: 5, P: 2 (each process has its own copy of x). Outputs: "C1: 3, C2: 5, P: 2" or "C2: 5, C1: 3, P: 2". Moving the first wait before x *= 2 guarantees C1, C2, P order; values unchanged.

HW1-Q3.2 exec worker (15 pts) CH3
Answer.

3 processes (parent → child → grandchild; the grandchild execs worker). X is not printed (successful exec never returns). Outputs: A P W C D · A W P C D · A W C P D.

HW1-Q3.3 Pipe parent → child (10 pts) CH3
Answer.

(a) Parent closes fd[0], writes to fd[1]; child closes fd[1], reads fd[0]; parent → child. (b) "Child: 12" then "Parent: done" — read blocks until data arrives, and the parent waits for the child. (c) No — separate pipe() calls after fork create two unrelated pipes, with no shared descriptors.

Homework 2（Fall 2026）

HW2-Q2.1 Different PC/registers (6 pts) CH4
Answer.

Yes — each thread has its own PC, register set and stack; saved in its TCB when not running.

HW2-Q2.2 Many-to-one on 4 cores (6 pts) CH4
Answer.

No — all user threads map to one kernel thread, which runs on one core at a time; only concurrency, no parallelism (and one blocking call blocks all).

HW2-Q2.3 Ctrl+C (6 pts) CH4
Answer.

Asynchronous (generated by an event external to the process). Yes — install a user-defined handler with signal(SIGINT, handler) to override the default (terminate).

HW2-Q2.4 Hard affinity (6 pts) CH5
Answer.

No — hard affinity restricts T to CPU 0 and CPU 1; load balancing may move it between those two only.

HW2-Q2.5 RM vs EDF at t = 50 (6 pts) CH5
Answer.

No. RM picks A (shorter period, static) → B finishes at 85 and misses 80. EDF picks B (deadline 80 < 100) → B finishes at 60, A at 85; both meet deadlines.

HW2-Q3.1 Amdahl (10 pts) CH4
Answer.

(a) 1/(0.2 + 0.8/4) = 2.5. (b) N → ∞: 1/0.2 = 5.

HW2-Q3.2 SRTF and RR q=3 (20 pts) CH5

P1 (0, 7), P2 (1, 3), P3 (4, 5), P4 (10, 2).

Answer.

SRTF 0 P1 1 P2 4 P3 9 P1 10 P4 12 P1 17 → avg WT 2.5, avg TAT 6.75. RR(3) 0 P1 3 P2 6 P1 9 P3 12 P1 13 P4 15 P3 17 → avg WT 4.75, avg TAT 9.

HW2-Q3.3 MLFQ (10 pts) CH5

Q0 RR4, Q1 RR8, Q2 FCFS; P1 (0, 20), P2 (1, 5), P3 (11, 2); preempted Q1 job keeps the unused part of its quantum.

Answer.

0 P1(Q0) 4 P2(Q0) 8 P1(Q1) 11 P3(Q0) 13 P1(Q1) 18 P2(Q1) 19 P1(Q2) 27. At t = 11, P1 goes to the head of Q1 (ahead of P2) with 5 ms of its Q1 quantum left.

MMC Answer KeyMC 題庫 + 答案

歷屆 MC（S26 / F20 / Sp21 / HW1 / HW2）

S26-MC1 Interrupts → C

Which of the following statements about interrupts is false?

A. Interrupts may be generated by hardware devices B. Interrupts may be generated by software exceptions or traps C. All interrupts are serviced in exactly the same way, with no difference in priority D. Interrupts are handled by the kernel
interrupt 有 priority levels，high-priority 可以 preempt low-priority interrupt [3.24]。
S26-MC2 DMA → B

Which of the following statements is true about DMA?

A. DMA is used to move data for slow devices, while CPU is used for fast devices B. DMA allows data transfer between device and memory with less CPU involvement C. DMA eliminates interrupts completely D. CPU and DMA can't execute concurrently
DMA 用於 fast devices / large data（A 相反）；完成後仍然 interrupt CPU（C 錯）；CPU 同 DMA 可以 concurrent（D 錯）[3.25]。
S26-MC3 Storage hierarchy → D

Which of the following statements is true about storage hierarchy?

A. Registers are slower than main memory B. Main memory is nonvolatile C. Hard-disk drives are smaller than volatile memory D. Main memory is byte addressable
each address refers to one byte [1.30]。Registers 最快；main memory volatile；HDD 大過 memory。
S26-MC4 von Neumann → B

Which of the following is a core characteristic of the von Neumann architecture?

A. Instructions and data are stored in separate physical memories B. Instructions and data are stored in the same memory space, accessed by address C. The CPU does not contain an ALU; all computations are done by an external co-processor D. The Program Counter (PC) is stored in external mass storage devices
stored-program architecture [1.26]。A 係 Harvard architecture。
S26-MC5 OS services → C

Which of the following is false about services of operating systems?

A. User interface can be CLI or GUI B. The logging service of OS should keep track of computer resources that each user uses C. Errors only occur in memory and I/O devices D. Resource allocation service should allocate resources like CPU cycles, memory, file storage
errors 可以喺 CPU and memory, I/O devices, user programs [2.5]。
S26-MC6 API → A

Which of the following is an advantage of using APIs instead of directly invoking system calls?

A. APIs provide a more convenient programming interface B. APIs always execute faster than system calls C. APIs can bypass kernel protection D. APIs eliminate the need for linkers and loaders
API 好處 = portability + hide complex details（convenient） [2.17]。
S26-MC7 Microkernel → B（C 亦接受）

Which is false about microkernel system structure?

A. It is easier to extend a microkernel operating system B. It typically has better performance than a monolithic kernel C. It runs memory management in user space D. It is easier to port a microkernel operating system to new architectures
microkernel performance suffers（message passing overhead）[2.48]；microkernel 保留 minimal memory management 喺 kernel，所以 C 都算錯。
S26-MC8 printf vs write → B

What's the relationship between printf() and write()?

A. Both printf() and write() are system calls provided directly by the kernel B. printf() invokes write() system call to get service from operating system C. The program enters kernel mode immediately when printf() is called D. write() system call invokes printf() to perform the print function
printf() 係 C library function，佢 call write() system call [2.26]。
S26-MC9 Layered design → B

Which of the following statements about a layered operating-system design is correct?

A. Any layer may directly access any other layer B. The interfaces between adjacent layers are usually well defined C. Layered design eliminates all performance overhead D. Pure layered design is the dominant structure in all modern operating systems
每層只用下層服務、interface well defined（information hiding）[2.44–2.45]。A 係 LKM 特色；layered 有 overhead；好少 pure layered。
S26-MC10 fork count → C
int main() {
   int i;
   for (i = 0; i < 3; i++) {
      if (fork() == 0) {
         fork();
         printf("X ");
         return 1;
      }
   }
   printf("Y ");
   return 0;
}

What is the total number of processes (including the original), the number of "X " printed, and the number of "Y " printed?

A. Total processes = 4, "X " = 3, "Y " = 1 B. Total processes = 4, "X " = 1, "Y " = 3 C. Total processes = 7, "X " = 6, "Y " = 1 D. Total processes = 7, "X " = 1, "Y " = 6
只有 original 繼續 loop；每輪生 1 個 child，child 再 fork → 2 個印 X 然後 return。3 輪 × 2 = 6 X；original 印 1 Y；total = 1 + 6 = 7（已用 C 程式驗證）。
S26-MC11 Context switch → B

Which statement is true about context switch?

A. Context switch means switching from user mode to kernel mode B. During context switch, OS saves the state of one process/thread and restores another C. Context switch requires the same hardware on all CPU architectures D. Context switch is shorter if the OS is more complex
[3.20]。A 係 mode switch；越複雜 OS/PCB → context switch 越長。
S26-MC12 Ordinary pipes → B

Which statement about ordinary pipes is false?

A. They can be accessed like file descriptors B. They can be accessed by arbitrary processes C. They disappear after the communicating processes terminate D. They are typically used between parent and child processes
ordinary pipe cannot be accessed from outside the process that creates it（只限 parent–child）[3.65–3.66]。
S26-MC13 Zombie → B

Which statement about zombie processes is correct?

A. A zombie process is still executing user code normally B. A zombie process has terminated but its parent has not yet collected its status C. A zombie process is created when exec() fails D. A zombie process owns no process identifier
[3.49] terminated 但 parent 未 call wait()；PID、process-table entry、PCB 仍然存在。
S26-MC14 Leaving running state → A

Which event typically causes a process to leave the running state?

A. It issues an I/O request B. It calls a function C. It increases a local variable D. It reads from cache
I/O request → running → waiting [3.9]。
S26-MC15 Multithreaded process → C

Which of the following is true about the multithreaded process?

A. Each thread has its own address space and file descriptors B. Threads can't run concurrently on multiple cores if they belong to the same process C. Thread creation is usually faster than process creation D. It can't take advantage of processing capabilities on multicore systems
Economy — thread creation cheaper [4.4, 4.7]；threads share address space & files。
S26-MC16 Concurrency vs parallelism → C

Which statement is correct about concurrency and parallelism in multicore systems?

A. Concurrency means a system can perform more than one task at exactly the same time B. Parallelism means tasks only make progress over time on a single processor core C. Concurrency can be supported on a single processor core through time multiplexing by the scheduler D. Testing and debugging become easier in multicore systems because execution paths are fixed
[4.9]。A 係 parallelism 定義；debugging 變難。
S26-MC17 Amdahl's Law → C

Which combination gives a speedup between 1.6 and 1.7?

A. parallel 81%, 3 cores B. parallel 81%, 5 cores C. parallel 61%, 3 cores D. parallel 61%, 5 cores
speedup = 1/(S + (1−S)/N)。A：1/(0.19+0.27) = 2.17；B：2.84；C：1/(0.39+0.2033) = 1.69；D：1.95。
S26-MC18 Signals → C

Which statement about signal handling is false?

A. Signals can be received synchronously or asynchronously B. Users can define custom signal handlers for certain signals C. All signals should be delivered to all threads in a process D. Different signals can have different handlers in the same process
synchronous signals 只 deliver 畀 causing thread；只有部分 asynchronous（e.g. Ctrl-C terminate）先畀所有 threads [4.30–4.31]。
S26-MC19 Scheduling criteria → C

Which statement about scheduling criteria is correct?

A. Waiting time is the amount of time from process arrival until the process finishes execution B. Turnaround time is the amount of time a process spends waiting in the ready queue C. Response time is the amount of time from when a request is submitted until the first response is produced D. Throughput is the fraction of time the CPU is busy
A 係 turnaround；B 係 waiting；D 係 CPU utilization [5.8, 5.12]。
S26-MC20 MLFQ → B

Which statement about MLFQ is correct?

A. Processes never move between queues B. Each queue can use a different scheduling algorithm C. It requires burst times to be known exactly in advance D. It is a non-preemptive algorithm
[5.30–5.31]。MLFQ 可以搬 queue、唔需要知 burst、preemptive。
S26-MC21 EDF → B

Which scheduling algorithm assigns priorities according to deadlines?

A. RM B. EDF C. FCFS D. RR
EDF = dynamic priority by deadline；RM = static by period [5.51]。
S26-MC22 Multiprocessor scheduling → C

Which statement about multiprocessor scheduling is false?

A. For asymmetric multiprocessing, the master server can become a potential bottleneck B. In a multithreaded system, another thread may run when one thread stalls while waiting for memory C. The motivation of placing multiple processor cores on the same physical chip is to simplify the scheduler D. Processor affinity tries to keep a process on the same CPU to improve cache performance
multicore 係為咗 faster + less power，而且令 scheduling 更複雜 [5.40]。
S26-MC23 Mutex + semaphore output → A
int counter = 0; pthread_mutex_t mtx; sem_t sem;   /* sem_init(&sem, 0, 0) */
void *worker1(void *arg) { pthread_mutex_lock(&mtx); counter = counter + 2;
    printf("A %d;", counter); pthread_mutex_unlock(&mtx); sem_post(&sem); return NULL; }
void *worker2(void *arg) { sem_wait(&sem); pthread_mutex_lock(&mtx); counter = counter + 3;
    printf("B %d;", counter); pthread_mutex_unlock(&mtx); return NULL; }
/* main creates t1 (worker1) and t2 (worker2), then joins both */

What is the output?

A. A 2; B 5; B. B 3; A 5; C. A 3; B 5; D. B 2; A 4;
sem 初始 0 → worker2 一定等 worker1 sem_post → 先 A（0+2=2）後 B（2+3=5）。
S26-MC24 Hardware primitive → B

Which hardware primitive is commonly used to build synchronization tools?

A. exec() B. test_and_set() C. fork() D. open()
atomic hardware instructions：test_and_set()、compare_and_swap() [6.14]。
S26-MC25 Bounded-waiting TAS → A

In the bounded-waiting mutual exclusion algorithm using test_and_set() (waiting[], key, lock; exit section scans j = (i+1)%n … and either sets lock = false or waiting[j] = false), which statement is not correct?

A. test_and_set(&lock) releases the lock after the critical section B. waiting[i] is used to indicate that process i wants to enter the critical section C. waiting[j] = false indicates that process i releases the lock (hands it to j) D. Process i can re-enter the critical section after executing lock = false
test_and_set 只會 set lock = true（acquire）；release 係 lock = false 或者 waiting[j] = false [6.19]。
F20-MC1 Locality → B

Locality is essential to ____

A. Memory B. Cache C. Hard disks D. Non-volatile memory (NVM)
caching works because of temporal/spatial locality [1.33]。
F20-MC2 Virtualization → D

____ allows a guest OS to run as an application on a native OS.

A. Operating system B. Emulation C. User interface D. Virtualization
[1.43] "allows an OS to run as an application within another OS"。
F20-MC3 Interrupt → C

Which of the following statements about interrupts is NOT true?

A. Interrupts enable hardware to interact with the operating system B. Interrupt alerts the CPU that some event requires attention C. Interrupt allows software to access hardware including the CPU D. Interrupt can be caused by both hardware and software
C 講緊 system call 嘅功能。
F20-MC4 API → C

Programmers interface with ____ to obtain services from the OS.

A. Shell B. System call interfaces C. APIs (application programming interfaces) D. System calls
programmers 用 API（best answer）[2.15]。
F20-MC5 Parameter passing → D

How can parameters be passed to the OS in system calls?

A. Registers B. Stack C. Block or table D. All of the above
三種方法 [2.20]。
F20-MC6 LKM → B

____ refers to the OS design that maintains a set of core components in the kernel and adds additional services via modules during run time.

A. Layered approach B. Loadable kernel modules (LKMs) C. Microkernel D. Monolithic
[2.49]。
F20-MC7 fork → A

Which of the following statements on fork() is NOT true?

A. The parent process waits until the completion of the child process B. The child process duplicates the entire address space of the parent process C. The parent and child processes run the same program D. The parent and child processes run concurrently in the system
等 child 係 wait() 嘅工作，fork 本身唔會等。
F20-MC8 Address space → D

Which of the following is contained in the address space of a process?

A. Stack B. Code C. Data D. All of the above
text (code)、data、heap、stack [3.5]。
F20-MC9 Named pipes → C

____ enables communication for multiple processes with no parent-child relationship, independent from network.

A. RPCs B. Ordinary pipes C. Named pipes D. Socket
named pipes 唔需要 parent-child、多 processes 用 [3.69]；socket/RPC 要 network。
F20-MC10 Medium-term scheduler → D

____ swaps a process out of memory and temporarily stores it on disk.

A. The long-term scheduler B. A device scheduler C. The CPU scheduler D. The medium-term scheduler
swapping [3.18]。
F20-MC11 Not shared by threads → B

Which item below is NOT shared by threads of the same process?

A. Code B. Stack C. Files D. Data
每個 thread 有自己 stack, registers, PC [4.6]。
F20-MC12 Thread library → A

____ provides the programmer with an API for creating and managing threads.

A. Thread library (such as POSIX Pthreads) B. Signals C. System calls D. Inter-process communications
[4.20]。
F20-MC13 Multithreading models → C

Which of the following statements is NOT true?

A. Kernel threads are scheduled by the OS B. Programmers or users manage user threads C. A user thread can be mapped to more than one kernel thread D. One user thread can be mapped to at most one kernel thread
冇 one-to-many mapping；many-to-many 係 many user → smaller or equal kernel threads。
F20-MC14 Minimum average waiting time → C（D 亦接受）

Given a set of processes, which algorithm produces the minimum average waiting time?

A. FCFS B. RR C. SJF D. SRTF
SJF optimal among non-preemptive；SRTF optimal among preemptive [5.17, 5.24]。
F20-MC15 Hard to implement → A

Which of the following scheduling algorithms is difficult to implement in practice?

A. SJF B. RR C. MLFQ D. FCFS
好難知道 next CPU burst length [5.17]。
Sp21-MC1 von Neumann → A

In a von Neumann architecture, a processing unit contains a/an ____ and processor registers.

A. arithmetic logic unit (ALU) B. instruction register (IR) C. program counter (PC) D. all of the mentioned
[1.26] "a CPU that contains an ALU and processor registers"；IR 屬 control unit。
Sp21-MC2 Interrupt trigger → D

Which of the following events will directly trigger an interrupt?

A. kernel code execution B. I/O job execution C. user code execution D. I/O job completion
device controller 完成操作 → interrupt CPU [3.21]。
Sp21-MC3 Dual mode → C

The two separate modes of operation in an operating system are ____.

A. supervisor mode and system mode B. physical mode and logical mode C. user mode and kernel mode D. kernel mode and privileged mode
[3.27]。
Sp21-MC4 System calls → C

____ provide an interface to the services provided by an operating system.

A. Inter-process communications B. Remote procedure calls C. System calls D. User interfaces
[2.14]。
Sp21-MC5 Original UNIX → C

Which operating system structure is adopted by the original UNIX?

A. layered structure B. microkernel C. monolithic structure D. modular approach
[2.40–2.41]。
Sp21-MC6 Degree of multiprogramming → B

The ____ refers to the number of processes in the memory.

A. program counter B. degree of multi-programming C. long-term scheduler D. CPU scheduler
[3.14]。
Sp21-MC7 Amdahl → B

Using Amdahl's Law, what is the speedup for an application that is 30% serial on 7 processing cores?

A. 2 B. 2.5 C. 3 D. 3.5
1/(0.3 + 0.7/7) = 1/0.4 = 2.5。
Sp21-MC8 Process owns → D

In an operating system, each process has its own ____.

A. address space B. local/global variables and open files C. pending alarms, signals and signal handlers D. all of the above
process = address space + resources。
Sp21-MC9 Stack → D

Which section of a process contains temporary data like function parameters, return addresses and local variables?

A. text section B. data section C. heap D. stack
[3.5]。
Sp21-MC10 Short-term scheduler → A

Which of the following selects from among the processes in the ready queue to execute on the CPU?

A. Short-term scheduler B. Medium-term scheduler C. Long-term scheduler D. Context switcher
= CPU scheduler [3.17]。
Sp21-MC11 fork incorrect → B

During process creation with fork(), which of the following is incorrect?

A. The child process can run concurrently with the parent B. The child process can call wait() in order to finish earlier than the parent C. The child process can call exec() to load a new program into it D. The child can be an exact duplicate of the parent
wait() 係等 child 完成，唔係令自己早完。
Sp21-MC12 Pipes → C

Which of the following statements about pipes is not true?

A. Named pipes allow bi-directional communication B. Only the parent and child processes can use ordinary pipes for communication C. Named pipes cease to exist after the communicating processes have finished D. Reading and writing to ordinary pipes on both UNIX and Windows can be performed like ordinary file I/O
named pipes continue to exist until explicitly deleted [3.69]。
Sp21-MC13 Leave the CPU → D

Which of the following events might force a process to leave the CPU?

A. make a fork system call B. make an I/O request C. interrupt D. all of the above
system call / I/O / interrupt 都可以令 process 離開 CPU。
Sp21-MC14 fork with || → C
if (fork() || fork()) {
   fork();
}

How many processes are created in total (including the original)?

A. 3 B. 4 C. 5 D. 6
P0（true，跳過第二個 fork）→ fork → 2；C1（false）→ fork2：C1 true → fork → 2；C2 false → 1。2 + 2 + 1 = 5 ✔
Sp21-MC15 fork in loop → C
for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
   if (fork() == 0) fork();
}

How many processes in total?

A. 3 B. 8 C. 27 D. 64
每輪每個 process 變 3 個 → 3³ = 27 ✔
Sp21-MC16 TCB → D

Which of the following is not in the Thread Control Block (TCB)?

A. Execution state B. Scheduling information C. Pointer to enclosing process D. Process identifier
TCB 有 execution state、scheduling info、accounting、pointers、pointer to enclosing PCB [4.15]；PID 喺 PCB。
Sp21-MC17 Concurrency → B

Which refers to the capability to allow multiple tasks to make progress on a single processor?

A. parallelism B. concurrency C. data parallelism D. task parallelism
[4.9]。
Sp21-MC18 Multithreading models → C

Which of the following is not true about multithreading models?

A. Many-to-one means many user-level threads are mapped to a single kernel thread B. One-to-one model provides more concurrency than many-to-one model C. The many-to-many model is easy to implement in practice D. Most operating systems now use the one-to-one model
many-to-many difficult to implement [4.26]。
Sp21-MC19 Thread-local storage → D

Thread-local storage is data that ____.

A. is not associated with any process B. has been modified by the thread, but not yet updated to the parent process C. is generated by the thread independent of the thread's process D. is unique to each thread
TLS = unique to each thread（Windows TEB 入面有 TLS [4.36]）。
Sp21-MC20 When scheduling occurs → D

CPU scheduling decisions may take place when ____.

A. a process switches from running to waiting state B. a process terminates C. a process switches from running to ready state D. all of the above
四種情況 [5.6]。
Sp21-MC21 Priority problem → B

The main problem with a priority scheduling algorithm is ____.

A. how to set the priority of a process B. some processes may never execute C. how to determine the length of the next CPU burst D. how to determine the length of the time quantum
starvation；solution = aging [5.25]。
Sp21-MC22 RR large quantum → B

For RR scheduling, when the time quantum q gets too large, it degenerates to ____.

A. SJF B. FCFS C. Shortest-remaining-time-first D. Multilevel queue
[5.14, 5.16]。
Sp21-MC23 Must be non-preemptive → A

Which of the following scheduling algorithms must be non-preemptive?

A. FCFS B. SJF C. Priority scheduling D. Multi-level feedback queue
FCFS 一定 non-preemptive；SJF / priority 有 preemptive 版本；MLFQ preemptive [5.11]。
Sp21-MC24 MLFQ true statement → C

In the multilevel feedback queue algorithm, which statement is true?

A. Prior knowledge on the next CPU burst time is required for scheduling B. Classification of the ready queue is permanent C. It handles interactive jobs well by delivering similar performance as SJF D. It won't suffer from starvation problem
[5.35]。B 係 multilevel queue（冇 feedback）；MLFQ 都可能 starvation（要 reshuffle/aging）。
Sp21-MC25 Real-time → C

Which statement about real-time CPU scheduling is true?

A. Real-time scheduling doesn't demand performance guarantee B. In hard real-time systems, a task can be serviced after its deadline C. Soft real-time systems guarantee only that the process will be prioritized over non-critical processes D. The scheduler for a real-time system must support a priority-based algorithm without preemption
[5.47]。RT scheduler 要 priority-based with preemption。
Sp21-MC26 Starvation-free → A

Which algorithms are free from starvation? 1. FCFS 2. Non-preemptive SJF 3. Preemptive SJF 4. Round Robin 5. Multi-level Queue

A. 1 4 B. 1 2 4 C. 1 3 4 D. 1 2 4 5
SJF/SRTF 長 job 可能 starve；multilevel queue（fixed priority）低 queue 可能 starve；FCFS、RR 唔會。
Sp21-MC27 Mutual exclusion → A

Mutual exclusion implies that ____.

A. if a process is executing in its critical section, then no other process can be executing in their critical sections B. if a process is executing in its critical section, then only one of the other processes can be executing in its critical section C. if a process is executing in its critical section, then all the resources of the system must be blocked until it finishes execution D. none of the above
[6.11]。
Sp21-MC28 CS requirements → C

A solution to the critical section problem does not have to satisfy which requirement?

A. mutual exclusion B. progress C. synchronization D. bounded waiting
三個 requirements = ME + progress + bounded waiting。
Sp21-MC29 Race condition → B

A situation where several processes access and manipulate the same data concurrently and the outcome depends on the particular order of access is called ____.

A. data consistency B. race condition C. aging D. starvation
[6.9]。
Sp21-MC30 Entry section → C

Suppose the binary variable lock is initialized to 0. Which can be an implementation of the entry section to solve the critical-section problem?

A. while (compare_and_swap(&lock, 0, 1) == 0) ; B. while (compare_and_swap(&lock, 1, 0) != 0) ; C. while (test_and_set(&lock)) ; D. while (!test_and_set(&lock)) ;
TAS 返回 old value；lock = 0 時第一個 process 得 0 → 跳出 loop 入 CS [6.16]。A 嘅條件相反（應該 != 0）。
HW1-MC1 DMA → C

Which statement about I/O operations and DMA is NOT true?

A. In programmed I/O, the CPU executes special I/O instructions to move data between a device controller and memory B. A DMA controller can transfer a block of data between an I/O device and memory without continuous CPU involvement C. During a DMA transfer, the DMA controller sends an interrupt to the CPU after every byte is transferred D. After a DMA block transfer completes, the DMA controller can interrupt the CPU
DMA block 完成先 interrupt 一次 [3.25–3.26]。
HW1-MC2 Interrupts and traps → B

Which statement about interrupts and traps is NOT true?

A. Hardware interrupts are generally asynchronous to the instruction currently being executed B. Interrupts can only be generated by I/O devices C. A trap or exception may be caused by an error or by a user request such as a system call D. Interrupts may have different priority levels
trap/exception = software-generated interrupt [3.24]。
HW1-MC3 Resource allocator → C

Several jobs are ready to run on a shared computer. The OS decides which job receives CPU time and how memory is allocated. Which role does this illustrate?

A. The OS provides a user interface B. The OS provides a communication service C. The OS acts as a resource allocator D. The OS acts as an application program
[1.23]。
HW1-MC4 NUMA → D

Which statement about multiprocessor, multicore, and NUMA systems is NOT true?

A. In SMP, processors have their own registers and local caches but share physical memory B. A multicore system places multiple computing cores on a single physical chip C. In NUMA, a CPU can access its local memory faster than remote memory connected to another CPU group D. In NUMA, every CPU accesses every part of memory with exactly the same latency
NUMA = non-uniform latency [1.40]。
HW1-MC5 OS structures → B

Which statement about operating-system structures is true?

A. A monolithic kernel moves most OS services into user-space processes that communicate by message passing B. A microkernel keeps only essential functions in the kernel and provides other services outside the kernel through communication mechanisms such as message passing C. In a layered OS, each layer may directly call any other layer without restriction D. A loadable kernel module always requires the entire kernel to be recompiled and the machine to be rebooted
[2.46–2.47]。A 描述 microkernel 唔係 monolithic；C 錯；LKM 唔使 recompile/reboot。
HW1-MC6 read() path → C

A C program calls read() to obtain data from a file. Which sequence best describes how the request reaches the OS?

A. The application directly executes the device's privileged I/O instructions while remaining in user mode B. The read() API completes the entire file operation in user mode without invoking the kernel C. The API and run-time environment use the system-call interface to invoke the corresponding kernel service, and control later returns to the application D. The OS creates a new process to execute every read() request
[2.18–2.19]。
HW1-MC7 Privileged instruction → A

What normally happens if a user program attempts to execute a privileged instruction in user mode?

A. The hardware treats the instruction as illegal and traps to the operating system B. The CPU automatically changes the instruction into a non-privileged instruction C. The user program changes the mode bit by itself and continues in kernel mode D. The instruction is always executed, but its result is discarded
[3.28]。
HW1-MC8 Virtualization → B

Which statement about virtualization is NOT true?

A. A virtual machine manager creates and manages virtual machines B. An application always runs faster inside a virtual machine than directly on the physical machine C. Each guest OS runs in its own VM and behaves as if it has access to hardware D. Virtualization allows multiple operating systems to run on one physical machine
VM 只會 minor performance decrease [1.46]。
HW1-MC9 fork → C

Which statement about fork() is NOT true?

A. After a successful fork(), the child receives 0 and the parent receives the child's PID B. The parent and child both continue execution at the instruction following fork() C. If the child changes a global variable after fork(), the parent's copy changes automatically D. The execution order of parent and child depends on scheduling unless synchronization is used
各自有 own copy。
HW1-MC10 Pipes → D

Which statement about pipes is NOT true?

A. An ordinary pipe is typically used between processes with a parent-child relationship B. A named pipe can be used by processes that do not have a parent-child relationship C. An ordinary pipe is typically unidirectional D. A named pipe ceases to exist as soon as the communicating processes terminate
named pipe 要 explicitly deleted。
HW2-MC1 Responsiveness → B

An interactive application continues responding to the user while one of its threads is blocked. Which benefit does this illustrate?

A. Economy B. Responsiveness C. Resource sharing D. Scalability
[4.7]。
HW2-MC2 Data parallelism → D

Which arrangement is an example of data parallelism?

A. Threads on different cores each perform a different operation B. One thread processes all elements of an array sequentially C. One thread handles user input while another thread performs spelling checks D. Threads on different cores perform the same operation on different subsets of an array
A、C = task parallelism；B = serial [4.11]。
HW2-MC3 Signal classification → A

Which statement correctly classifies signals?

A. Division by zero can generate a synchronous signal, whereas timer expiration can generate an asynchronous signal B. Division by zero can generate an asynchronous signal, whereas timer expiration can generate a synchronous signal C. Both generate synchronous signals D. Both generate asynchronous signals
[4.29]。
HW2-MC4 Deferred cancellation → C

Which statement describes deferred thread cancellation?

A. The target thread is terminated immediately when cancellation is requested B. Requesting cancellation terminates every thread in its process C. The target thread periodically checks whether it should terminate, allowing it to terminate in an orderly manner D. The target thread can be canceled only after it has finished its normal execution
A 係 asynchronous cancellation [4.32]。
HW2-MC5 Preemptive scheduling → C

Which event is an example of preemptive CPU scheduling?

A. A running process completes its execution and terminates B. A running process requests I/O and enters the waiting state C. A time quantum expires, and the running process is moved back to the ready queue D. A running process calls wait() and enters the waiting state
running → ready（involuntary）= preemptive [5.6]。
HW2-MC6 Convoy effect → B

Which situation illustrates the convoy effect in FCFS?

A. A waiting process gradually gains a higher priority B. Several short processes wait behind a long process that arrived first C. A process moves to a lower-priority queue after using its time quantum D. A running process is preempted when its RR quantum expires
[5.10]。A = aging；C = MLFQ demotion。
HW2-MC7 Aging → A

What is the purpose of aging in priority scheduling?

A. To address starvation by gradually increasing the priority of a waiting process B. To keep each process permanently in the same priority queue C. To predict the length of a process's next CPU burst D. To give higher priority to periodic tasks with shorter periods
[5.25]。D = RM。
HW2-MC8 PCS vs SCS → D

In a many-to-many model, a thread library selects a user thread to run on an available LWP. Which scheduling scope applies?

A. SCS, because several user threads are competing B. SCS, because the library schedules kernel threads onto CPUs C. PCS, because all kernel threads in the system are competing D. PCS, because the competing threads belong to the same process
[5.37]。
HW2-MC9 Exponential averaging → C

The predicted CPU burst is 10 ms, the actual burst is 6 ms, α = 0.25. What is the next prediction?

A. 6 ms B. 7 ms C. 9 ms D. 10 ms
0.25 × 6 + 0.75 × 10 = 9。
HW2-MC10 Little's formula → B

3 processes arrive per second on average; average queue length is 12. Average waiting time?

A. 0.25 s B. 4 s C. 9 s D. 36 s
W = n / λ = 12 / 3 = 4 s [5.57]。

P-ch1-1 Storage managed by → A

According to the storage characteristics table, which level of storage is managed by the compiler?

P-ch1-2 Bootstrap → C

A. DRAM B. Cache C. EEPROM D. Hard disk

P-ch1-3 Multiprocessor speedup → B

Why is the speed-up ratio with N processors less than N?

A. Each processor runs a different instruction set B. Overhead, e.g., contention for shared resources such as the bus or memory C. Only one processor can execute user processes D. Processors must share a single register set
[1.37]。

Which is NOT an advantage of multiprocessor systems listed in the lecture?

A. Increased throughput B. Economy of scale C. Increased reliability (graceful degradation) D. Simpler parallel programming

P-ch1-5 NUMA drawback → B

What is the potential drawback of a NUMA system?

A. All CPUs must share a single slow bus B. Increased latency when a CPU must access remote memory across the system interconnect C. CPUs cannot share one physical address space D. It consumes more power than multicore chips
[1.40]。
P-ch1-6 Cloud models → B

A database server provided as a ready-to-use software stack over the Internet is an example of:

A. SaaS B. PaaS C. IaaS D. MaaS

P-ch1-7 VMM → B

Which statement about virtualization components is correct?

[1.44]。
P-ch1-8 Timesharing → B

A. Multiprogramming is an extension of timesharing B. Timesharing switches jobs so frequently that users can interact with each job while it runs C. Timesharing requires that only one program be in memory D. Multiprogramming is not used in modern systems
timesharing = logical extension of multiprogramming [1.42]。
P-ch1-9 Mechanical vs electrical storage → B

A. Larger and cheaper per byte B. More costly, smaller, more reliable and faster C. Slower but more reliable D. Volatile
[1.31]。
P-ch1-10 AMAT → C

A cache has hit ratio 95%, hit time 1 ns and miss time 100 ns. The average access time is:

A. 1.0 ns B. 5.0 ns C. 5.95 ns D. 100 ns
0.95 × 1 + 0.05 × 100 = 0.95 + 5 = 5.95 ns。

P-ch2-7 MS-DOS → B

P-ch3-1 Default file descriptors → A

On UNIX, each process by default has three open file descriptors. Which are they?

P-ch3-2 Linux PCB → B

A. struct pcb in a hash table B. struct task_struct elements in a circular doubly linked list (task list) C. TCB in a binary tree D. ETHREAD
[3.13]。
P-ch3-3 Long-term scheduler → B

Which statement about the long-term scheduler is correct?

A. It is invoked every few milliseconds B. It controls the degree of multiprogramming and is mainly used in mainframes, not PCs C. It swaps processes out to disk D. It selects the next process from the ready queue
[3.17]。C = medium-term；D = short-term。
P-ch3-4 Register sets → B

Some processors (e.g., SUN UltraSPARC) provide multiple register sets. What is the effect on context switch?

P-ch4-8 One-to-one drawback → C

P-ch5-1 Dispatcher → D

P-ch5-12 RM utilization → B

Two periodic tasks: P1 (period 50, burst 25), P2 (period 80, burst 35). Under RM:

P-ch5-14 Response time (course definition) → C

RR with q = 4; P1 (24), P2 (3), P3 (3) all arrive at 0 in that order. Using the course definition, the average response time is:

A. 3.67 B. 5.67 C. 7 D. 15.67
end of first run − arrival = 4, 7, 10 → avg 7 [5.13]。（3.67 係課本「first start」定義；5.67 係 avg WT；15.67 係 avg TAT）
